# SOURCE NOTES FOR: Opening gaps: NDOG, NWOG, the RTH opening range gap and Event Horizon

Each block below is one concept section from study notes of a specific ICT video. Blocks are ordered 2022 Mentorship → 2024 Mentorship → 2026 Lectures, so you can see how the teaching evolved. Timestamp links inside are real.


######## 2022 MENTORSHIP ########

=== [2022 Ep11 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 11 (ID Sqw2bww93Zo)] — Exit management: market vs limit order; take profits into the raid and step aside for the gap fill
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - He was watching 13,590/13,586 as an objective but didn't use a limit order. If price ran fast and far lower he didn't want a limit capping his exit, so he left it open and closed manually at 13,612.25.
  - Once price runs into the low (the liquidity), take profits and move to the sidelines. A big gap is likely to come back and rebalance (fill), and it did.
- **Example in this video:** Short 13,858.25, exit 13,612.25 = 246 handles (×4 ticks per handle for tick counters). That's $4,920 on 1 NQ mini.
- **Timestamps:** [0:04:50](https://youtu.be/Sqw2bww93Zo?t=290) — no limit; exit 13,612.25; entry 13,858.25 · [0:20:07](https://youtu.be/Sqw2bww93Zo?t=1207) — take profits into the low; the gap gets refilled

=== [2022 Ep14 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 14 (ID NUdu1n-ML98)] — Opening gap fill (gap from Friday's close)
- **Kind:** example only
- **What ICT says:** He looks for a "gap fill from Friday's close" as the draw; when price got very close to closing that gap he took partial profits.
- **Example in this video:** MNQ — took 6 contracts off as price neared the closure of the gap; 14,220 seen as the full fill/objective.
- **Timestamps:** [0:00:01](https://youtu.be/NUdu1n-ML98?t=1), [0:03:09](https://youtu.be/NUdu1n-ML98?t=189).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Exact gap boundaries not stated in the transcript.

=== [2022 Ep34 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 34 (ID t8flQP5jy-Q)] — Sunday opening gap as a weekly dynamic level (precursor to NWOG)
- **Kind:** new framing / rule
- **What ICT says:** Plot the Sunday opening gap (the gap between Friday close and the Sunday evening reopen, ~6 PM ET) and extend it across the entire week. Many times there's "a lot of valuation around that gap" — it acts as a dynamic price level: if price is above it, look for it to act as support; if below, resistance. Some weeks run away from it and never return, but in consolidation weeks (e.g., an FOMC week) the choppy range-bound action keeps revisiting it. If price is going to trade down into the Sunday gap, look for it to first run above nearby swing highs / relative equal highs (buy-side).
- **How to identify:** Mark Friday's close and Sunday's open; shade the gap; extend right.
- **How to use:** Expect reactions at the gap's edges (he watched its lower end); combine with FVG/OB entries; in consolidation weeks use it as the range's pivot.
- **Example in this video:** ES: price traded below sell-side and into/through the Sunday gap down to an FVG, rallied to the opening range; at lunch retraced back up into the Sunday gap which acted as resistance; dropped to an order block (big down candle), accumulated, rallied back above the gap; retraced back into it as support (discount low); then FVG → 15m OB → run to 3952.75/3954.50.
- **Timestamps:** [0:04:48](https://youtu.be/t8flQP5jy-Q?t=288) — lower end of Sunday gap to watch, resistance from below; [0:05:35](https://youtu.be/t8flQP5jy-Q?t=335) — lunch retracement into gap as resistance; [0:08:48](https://youtu.be/t8flQP5jy-Q?t=528) — "dynamic support/resistance" (and "blasphemy" joke); [0:09:36](https://youtu.be/t8flQP5jy-Q?t=576) — rule above = support, below = resistance; run highs before trading down into it; [0:10:25](https://youtu.be/t8flQP5jy-Q?t=625) — FOMC-week consolidation.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He knowingly uses retail language ("support/resistance") only for this gap, to make it understandable. Contrast with Ep 33, where old lows are not trusted as S/R without an FVG.


######## 2024 MENTORSHIP ########

=== [2024 #1 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #1  August 5, 2024 (ID GKeLVR3dPuI)] — Fair value gap: SIBI/BISI naming, volume imbalance, CE  (aliases: SIBI, BISI, inefficiency, mid gap, consequent encroachment)
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - A fair value gap made by a down-close candle is a **SIBI** (sell-side imbalance, buy-side inefficiency).
  - One made by an up-close candle is a **BISI** (buy-side imbalance, sell-side inefficiency).
  - When a volume imbalance sits next to the gap, include it in the gap. Watch the candle bodies being encapsulated by it.
  - When price is above an FVG, that FVG is a discount array. Price trading down to its mid-gap (CE) and holding is the signal to look for the run.
  - FVGs visible on all three timeframes act as "stepping stones" toward the draw.
- **Example in this video:**
  - NQ 1m after 8:30: it took 6 candles from a volume imbalance to run above the short-term high and into the SIBI above.
  - Later, a wick traded down to the mid-gap of an FVG below price (a discount array), and then the run started.
  - A volume imbalance encapsulated in a gap (caption "SIII", unclear whether SIBI or BISI) saw price run exactly to that gap's high.
- **Timestamps:** [1:01:16](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=3676) — 6-candle count, SIBI/BISI definitions · [1:25:28](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=5128) — include the volume imbalance; bodies encapsulated · [1:30:21](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=5421) — trade into mid gap = discount array CE · [1:39:12](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=5952) — three timeframes; stepping stones

=== [2024 #3 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #3   August 7, 2024 (ID ZkLc4-adodg)] — New Day Opening Gap (NDOG)  (aliases: new day opening gap, NDOG; label "ND" + date)
- **Kind:** definition + rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Applies to the **index futures** in this teaching. Every day except Friday, the market stops at **5:00 PM ET**, pauses for one hour and reopens at **6:00 PM ET**.
  - On a **1-minute chart**, take the close of the **4:59 PM candle** (the last print before the stop) and the **opening price of the first 6:00 PM 1m candle**. The space between those two prices is the NDOG. You do not need live data for this, and the effect on price lasts for weeks.
  - **Three levels:** the gap high (whichever is higher of the 5 PM close and the 6 PM open), the gap low (whichever is lower), and **consequent encroachment (CE)**, the midpoint. He also adds the **0.25 and 0.75 levels** with a Fib, which splits the gap into equal quarters ("quadrants").
  - Draw each level with a **horizontal ray** that extends right forever. Put the label in the middle/right and **always include the date**, because the date tells you how old (and how important) the level is.
  - **Dating convention:** a gap that forms Tuesday evening at 6 PM is **Wednesday's** NDOG, because it is the reference for Wednesday's trading.
  - **Life cycle: 5 trading days** (not calendar days). If there is a holiday, skip it and add a day. Example: a gap formed Tuesday for Wednesday is valid Wed (day 1), Thu (2), Fri (3), Mon (4), Tue (5), so it is good "until Wednesday next week". Keep **five** NDOGs on the chart. This is a starting rule, not a hard cutoff: he still uses gaps that are **9 months old** when they fit the narrative.
  - Filling the gap does **not** make it stale. He contrasts this with retail "gap fill, done" thinking and with supply/demand "fresh zone" logic. Price keeps coming back to these levels.
  - Price is especially sensitive to an NDOG it has **touched only once**. It is still "hot" and price will very likely be drawn back to it ("just a guarantee", one of the very few guarantees in trading, he says).
- **How to identify:** 1m chart → 4:59 PM close vs. 6:00 PM first open → mark high, low, CE (and quarters) → label with date → keep the last 5.
- **How to use:**
  - It is the **underlying narrative / draw on liquidity** behind the intraday REH/REL objectives. An intraday target is "linked to" an NDOG and/or NWOG.
  - **Clustering filter:** look only at the last 5. If most of them sit above the current price (maybe one at or below), expect price to gravitate up, and the reverse when they cluster below. If you are anticipating a short but the gaps cluster above, give the first 30 minutes after 7:00/8:00/9:00 a chance to spike up into them in a thin price run. Then wait for a market structure shift or breaker and/or an **inversion FVG** before shorting.
  - Clustering is a **filter, not a hard rule**. A cluster below doesn't mean "short only". If he wants a long, he first waits to see whether they drop price toward the gaps.
  - The trade does not need to reach the NDOG that day to be profitable. If it hadn't traded there during the day, he would have looked for it to happen in **London that night**.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 1m. On Tue Aug 6, the 5 PM close was **above** the 6 PM open, so it was a **down gap**. That made it Wednesday Aug 7's NDOG. After the open, price traded up into the gap and stopped exactly at a quadrant level, **18,049.50** (caption "18, 04950"), which he calls perfect. It hovered around the **upper quadrant**, dropped to CE, then to the **lower quadrant**, and took a low there. It did not touch the actual gap low before rallying away. At 7:00, 8:00 and 9:00, price was far above the gap. In the afternoon (during the stream), price fell back and closed into it.
- **Timestamps:** [0:17:21](https://youtu.be/ZkLc4-adodg?t=1041) definition starts · [0:18:58](https://youtu.be/ZkLc4-adodg?t=1138) the 4:59 close and 6:00 first print · [0:27:53](https://youtu.be/ZkLc4-adodg?t=1673) dated for the next day, 5 trading days · [0:31:52](https://youtu.be/ZkLc4-adodg?t=1912) quarters, stop at 18,049.50
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He first introduced NDOGs in Twitter Spaces. He says "it's 2024" and that he is now showing his hand: the journal routine, the clustering filter, the 150-handle proximity rule and "touched once = hot". If the exchange ever went to 24-hour trading and the NDOG disappeared, he says you would still have NWOGs and REH/REL.

=== [2024 #3 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #3   August 7, 2024 (ID ZkLc4-adodg)] — New Week Opening Gap (NWOG)  (aliases: new week opening gap, NWOG; label "NW" + date)
- **Kind:** definition + rule
- **What ICT says:** The NWOG is the range between **Friday's 5:00 PM close** and **Sunday's 6:00 PM open**. Mark it the same way as the NDOG (high, low, CE) but label it NW. **Life cycle: 5 weeks**, so keep five of them. Like NDOGs, they work 6–9 months later because they are real places where the market didn't trade efficiently. There is only one NWOG per week against five NDOGs. **For a swing trade the NWOG has more impact**, because the market makers have had the whole weekend to digest information before setting that opening price.
- **How to identify:** 1m chart, Fri 4:59 PM close vs. Sun 6:00 PM first print.
- **How to use:** It is the higher-tier "black hole" draw. Use it together with NDOG clusters for the directional filter and as targets for holding longer.
- **Example in this video:** none specific. He points to price dipping into a gap and calls that the first and second time it passed into it.
- **Timestamps:** [0:28:41](https://youtu.be/ZkLc4-adodg?t=1721) definition and 5-week cycle · [0:45:19](https://youtu.be/ZkLc4-adodg?t=2719) NWOG vs NDOG for swing trades
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Keep the full history on a separate TradingView layout if you want to track older gaps. You must also keep count of how many times each has been referred to.

=== [2024 #4 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #4    August 8, 2024 (ID hdSscxZgE58)] — New Day Opening Gap (NDOG) — derivation & labeling  (aliases: new day opening gap, NDOG)
- **Kind:** definition / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - The NDOG for a trading day is formed the evening before: the gap between the 5:00 PM ET close (start of the one-hour break) and the 6:00 PM ET open. E.g. Tuesday's NDOG is created Monday at 6 PM; Wednesday's NDOG is created Tuesday at 6 PM.
  - If the 6 PM open is above the 5 PM close it is an up gap: the 6 PM open is the NDOG high and the 5 PM close is the low (and vice versa for a gap down).
  - Put a Fibonacci on the gap's high and low; the 0.5 level is the consequent encroachment (CE / midpoint).
  - Add 0.25 and 0.75 to your Fib settings to get the quadrant levels.
  - Quadrants are only worth plotting when the range is large. If the NDOG is small (he gives "10 or 15 handles, something less than 20 handles" on NQ) just eyeball the midpoint — the difference between entry and stop is too small to matter.
  - Example of a "significant" range: ~66 handles (caption reads "18,99 to 18,33"; exact prices unclear) — he would plot quadrants.
- **How to identify:** 5 PM ET close vs 6 PM ET open of the prior evening.
- **How to use:** reference levels (high, low, CE, quadrants) for where price gravitates, where bodies stop, where wicks overshoot. See "Opening-gap clustering bias" below.
- **Example in this video:** Before 8:30 on Aug 8, NQ was trading just below Wednesday's NDOG low and above today's (Thursday's) NDOG high — "in between", i.e. no-man's land.
- **Timestamps:** [0:08:27](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=507) — price sitting between Wednesday's NDOG low and today's NDOG high; [0:13:17](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=797) — 5 PM close / 6 PM open derivation; [0:14:06](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=846) — CE via Fib, quadrants only for ranges > ~20 handles; [0:15:40](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=940) — add 0.25/0.75 to Fib settings.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Continues Lecture #3 (Aug 7) teaching — he tells viewers to go back and watch that one.

=== [2024 #4 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #4    August 8, 2024 (ID hdSscxZgE58)] — New Week Opening Gap (NWOG) — labeling  (aliases: new week opening gap, NWOG)
- **Kind:** definition / refinement
- **What ICT says:** Measure from Friday's close to Sunday's 6 PM ET open; 50% = CE. For a significant NWOG, also plot the quadrants. To keep the chart clean, he measures with a Fib, then replaces it with a single horizontal line typed at the exact price (e.g. NWOG CE ~18,451.75 — caption also says "441.75", unclear) and removes the Fib.
- **How to identify:** Friday close vs Sunday 6 PM open.
- **How to use:** Same as NDOG — one of the clustered reference points that tells you where price will reach.
- **Example in this video:** Early in the week price spiked into the NWOG with wicks ("wicks do the damage"), hit the bottom of the NWOG and accelerated lower into Tuesday's NDOG CE, then Wednesday's NDOG with bodies respecting it, then CE again. On Aug 8 the NWOG was far above price — "it's going to take a real move to get up there".
- **Timestamps:** [0:16:30](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=990) — NWOG measured from Friday close to Sunday open, quadrants; [0:18:54](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=1134) — review of price respecting NWOG / NDOG levels earlier in the week.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Keep the **last five NDOGs** and the **last five NWOGs** on your reference layout.

=== [2024 #4 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #4    August 8, 2024 (ID hdSscxZgE58)] — Opening-gap clustering bias (which side will price press into at 8:30)  (aliases: stacking / clustering of new day opening gaps)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - At 8:00 ET look for relative equal highs and relative equal lows into 8:30.
  - At 8:30 (with or without news) ask: where is the stacking/clustering of the NDOGs and the NWOG — above or below the current price? Price is most likely to press into the side that has the inefficiencies, **even if they have already been traded to / "filled"**.
  - Gaps are not "used up" once touched — "the algorithm ... calls back to these points of reference based on time and price." They have a "life cycle" of sensitivity.
  - He estimates the direction is right "80% or higher" initially (explicitly "not 100%").
  - Retail breakout traders will try to buy above / sell below the nearby highs/lows — you wait, because between two gaps neither direction is more likely until the 8:30 move.
- **How to identify:** (1) Price location just before 8:30; (2) count NDOGs/NWOG above vs below; (3) context: has price already dropped into a deep discount of the recent range? (4) where have the profitable positions been (the side that has been winning recently is the side that will be squeezed).
- **How to use:** Defines the draw for the 8:30 run and subsequent scalps. If price had dropped into a clean FVG just before 8:30 (e.g. at 8:25), you could have bought into the news anticipating the run; without such a PD array, don't gamble — wait and drop to a 15-second chart to trade the inefficiencies/first order block that support the idea.
- **Example in this video:** Aug 8, NQ: just before 8:30 price was below Wednesday's NDOG and above Thursday's NDOG; Tuesday's NDOG, Wednesday's NDOG and the NWOG all sat above. Price had already dropped into a deep discount of the range from the low up to Wednesday's high, taken relative equal lows inside a BISI, and bounced off the upper quadrant of that BISI. At 8:30 it rallied up into Wednesday's NDOG low, CE, and high, then Tuesday's NDOG — as forecast the day before.
- **Timestamps:** [0:20:33](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=1233) — clustering is above, so bias is up; [0:26:23](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=1583) — 8:00 relative equal highs/lows, 8:30 clustering; [0:27:10](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=1630) — gaps already traded through still matter; [0:33:38](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=2018) — same signature without news, ~80%+.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Direct application of Lecture #3. Contrasts with the "gap is filled, forget it" view of everyone else in the industry.

=== [2024 #5 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #5    August 9, 2024 (ID ul6x6BwOg6M)] — NDOG at the 6 PM restart (Asia application)  (aliases: new day opening gap, NDOG)
- **Kind:** rule / refinement
- **What ICT says:** At 5 PM ET indices take a one-hour break and reopen at 6 PM. NDOG = 5 PM close vs 6 PM open; 6 PM open above the close → it's the NDOG high; below → NDOG low. CE = midpoint. If the gap is over 20 handles, he always has the CE annotated; under 20, he eyeballs the CE and the quadrants.
- **Example in this video:** Thursday Aug 8 evening (for Friday's trading), NQ: the 4:59 PM candle closed at 18,558; 6 PM opened at ~18,550.25 (caption "18550 point2") — a gap lower of less than 10 handles, so just eyeball the middle.
- **Timestamps:** [0:19:52](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=1192) — 6 PM derivation; [0:20:40](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=1240) — 20-handle rule; [0:31:22](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=1882) — 18,558 close / 18,550 open; [0:32:12](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=1932) — less than 10 handles, eyeball.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Do not trade as soon as the gap is noted — time must be the trigger first.

=== [2024 #5 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #5    August 9, 2024 (ID ul6x6BwOg6M)] — Initial buy-side / initial sell-side liquidity (the 6 PM / NDOG protocol)  (aliases: initial BSL, initial SSL, minor buy-side/sell-side liquidity pool, "initial range for building and engineering liquidity")
- **Kind:** new coinage / entry model framework
- **What ICT says:**
  - After the 6 PM open, you want to see price do two things: (1) move away from the NDOG and take a short-term high (a minor buy-side liquidity pool), then (2) cross back over the NDOG and take a short-term low (a minor sell-side liquidity pool) — or the reverse order. Simply trading below the NDOG isn't enough; a short-term low must be taken.
  - Once both happen, that new high and new low after 6 PM are the **initial buy-side liquidity** and **initial sell-side liquidity** for the day — "the initial high and low that every trader that trades at that moment is going to refer to for where their stop loss is going to be." This visually represents how the algorithm "calibrates" and engineers liquidity.
  - Price "coloring outside the lines" of the NDOG (crossing through it) is expected — it is not a failure of the level.
  - If after 6 PM price "dilly-dallies" and doesn't bump above or below into 7 PM, Asia will likely be dull — don't trade; go to bed.
  - These initial levels later act as the real support/resistance the algorithm refers back to (overnight, London, and even the NY open).
- **How to identify:** Mark NDOG → raid of short-term high → cross back through NDOG → raid of short-term low (or mirror). Label initial BSL small at top-left of the line, initial SSL bottom-left; keep them muted. Then look left for larger pools of liquidity (e.g. relative equal highs).
- **How to use:** At the key time (7 PM for Asia), look for price to retrace into the NDOG while not going back to the opposite initial liquidity; then trade toward the initial liquidity on the other side (and the larger pools beyond it).
- **Example in this video:** NQ, Thursday Aug 8 evening: after the 6 PM open price ran above a short-term high (initial BSL), dropped back below the NDOG to take a short-term low (initial SSL), crossed back above the NDOG with displacement before 7 PM. On the 7:00 PM candle it dipped back and touched the NDOG high — did not go near the initial SSL. Relative equal highs and the initial BSL sat above.
- **Timestamps:** [0:36:16](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=2176) — the two things to see; [0:41:06](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=2466) — why they're labeled "initial"; [0:41:56](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=2516) — algorithm calibrating; stops reference; [0:43:32](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=2612) — dull Asia if no bump either side.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He says the same "initial BSL/SSL" logic applies to the 9:30–10:00 opening range and (with the 12:00–12:30 range) to London.

=== [2024 #5 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #5    August 9, 2024 (ID ul6x6BwOg6M)] — Bias from opening gaps, scalable by timeframe  (aliases: daily bias, session bias)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** NWOG and NDOG are your primary factors for determining bias. Bias can be reduced to the session you trade, the next hour, the next 3 hours (≈ a session), the morning before NY lunch, or the whole daily range — same tools, you just pick the starting point (e.g. 6 PM for the evening). This week he used nothing above a 15-minute chart except Monday's daily-chart NWOG.
- **Timestamps:** [0:19:03](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=1143); [0:29:32](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=1772).

=== [2024 #5 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #5    August 9, 2024 (ID ul6x6BwOg6M)] — RTH opening range gap & the 9:30–10:00 opening range  (aliases: regular trading hours gap, opening range, midgap)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Switch the chart from electronic trading hours (ETH) to regular trading hours (RTH) to see the RTH gap: the difference between the previous day's 4:15 PM ET settlement close and the 9:30 AM open. (4:00 bell, 15 minutes settling, 4:15 day session done; electronic trading continues to 5 PM, restarts at 6 PM.)
  - The opening range is algorithmically 30 minutes, 9:30–10:00 — "there is no 15-minute opening range." Within it the market establishes initial buy-side and sell-side, like the 6 PM NDOG protocol. Sometimes there's no opening-range impact and price just runs.
  - Highlight the whole gap range and watch. With a large gap, price will normally trade a bit against the open, then work back, often taking/approaching the low formed.
  - After a big gap lower, there is about a **70% chance** price returns to **midgap** (the gap's midpoint) — even if bearish overall; "not 100%".
  - Whether the gap closes fully is judged using the initial BSL/SSL levels and other factors.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Aug 9: gapped down on RTH; first candle green (open = low); price ran up because it had already taken a low, reached midgap, bumped the low again, then ran up to the initial SSL reference level from the 6 PM protocol — not a full gap closure (it could have reached the 4:15 close or half the wick).
- **Timestamps:** [1:21:06](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=4866) — ETH vs RTH; [1:22:41](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=4961) — 30-minute opening range; [1:24:19](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=5059) — highlight the gap; [1:25:09](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=5109) — 70% midgap.

=== [2024 #6 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #6   August 12, 2024 Begins 9:15am ET (ID woAQ1XJeD7Y)] — New Week Opening Gap (NWOG) and New Day Opening Gap (NDOG) as the draw and as bias inputs  (aliases: "old inefficiencies", "clustering of new day opening gaps", "black hole")
- **Kind:** definition + rule (re-stated from Lecture #5)
- **What ICT says:**
  - NWOG for this week: Friday's 5:00 p.m. closing/settlement price to Sunday's 6:00 p.m. opening price (Sunday Aug 11 here). NDOG: the prior day's 5:00 p.m. close to the 6:00 p.m. open one hour later. Example: the Friday Aug 9 NDOG was formed from Thursday evening's 5:00 p.m. close and the 6:00 p.m. open. A new NDOG forms each evening.
  - NDOGs have a shelf life / life cycle; he suggests working with them as if about five days, "not that it's limited to five days". The market gravitates back and forth to these inefficiencies, like a magnet; they are the "first central tenets" of bias.
  - A gap under 20 handles is not subdivided into quadrants (he does not annotate quadrants or the midpoint on it). He eyeballs it (roughly upper/lower half). Implication: quadrants matter for larger gaps.
  - The algorithm "has to refer back" to old inefficiencies at the opening bell, so you gravitate toward them rather than watching order-book heat maps.
  - Price on Monday morning was above the NWOG and above the Friday NDOG and the cluster of earlier NDOGs and last week's NWOG; therefore the weight of the market is down, to discount arrays below.
- **How to identify:** compare price at your start time to the NWOG and NDOG cluster. Above them = expect sell-side delivery toward them.
- **How to use:** primary draw on liquidity for the session; targets are the NWOG (bodies stopped at it), then the next NDOG (Fri Aug 9), and the midpoint (CE) of the NDOG as the end of the morning's interest.
- **Example in this video:** Price traded lower from the bearish order block, through layered sell-side and the upper quadrant region, and the candle bodies stopped right at the NWOG; it then continued to last Friday's NDOG and traded to the NDOG's midpoint (consequent encroachment), where he said "that ends my interest in the morning session". Price then showed willingness to go higher and traded back over the area.
- **Timestamps:** [0:27:14](https://youtu.be/woAQ1XJeD7Y?t=1634) — NWOG as the draw; [0:28:19](https://youtu.be/woAQ1XJeD7Y?t=1699) — NDOG life cycle and the <20 handles quadrant rule; [0:29:16](https://youtu.be/woAQ1XJeD7Y?t=1756) — how the Friday NDOG is calibrated; [0:32:49](https://youtu.be/woAQ1XJeD7Y?t=1969) — trading to the NDOG midpoint ends the morning's interest.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He says he introduced NWOG/NDOG publicly in spring/summer 2023 on Twitter Spaces and deliberately let public interest fade because it is "too good" to be in everyone's hands.

=== [2024 #6 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #6   August 12, 2024 Begins 9:15am ET (ID woAQ1XJeD7Y)] — Session-bias checklist at 7:00 (price vs NWOG/NDOG cluster)  (aliases: "first elements of bias", session bias, "weight of the market")
- **Kind:** rule / entry framework
- **What ICT says:**
  - If at 7:00 price is above the new week opening gap and the NDOGs are clustered below price (all of last week's NDOGs, Friday's NDOG, last week's NWOG), the market is likely to enter a sell program: sell-side delivery, a sustained run lower. He then does not look to go long even if price springs higher; he expects that rally to fizzle above old highs formed after 7:00.
  - Bias here is a session bias; it need not be the daily bias (predicting close above/below the open). Focus on the morning session: a run that is measurable, one-sided, that you can participate in.
  - When the weight is down, the market "discovers / probes into discount arrays" below: inefficiencies, deep retracements into the previous week's range, or short-term lows that can be identified on the 15-minute chart.
  - The run has to be "definitely a protraction", one-directional, going to a level that can be predetermined, "not random".
  - He is not trying to predict the day's close, only one session run.
- **How to identify:** (1) price vs NWOG; (2) where the NDOG cluster sits; (3) TGIF/weekly-range retracement status (below); (4) time of day (7:00+).
- **How to use:** with bias down: wait for relative equal highs after 7:00, wait for the pierce, then enter a bearish PD array (list below) and aim for sell-side liquidity, NWOG, and NDOG.
- **Example in this video:** exactly the Aug 12 morning described above.
- **Timestamps:** [1:09:55](https://youtu.be/woAQ1XJeD7Y?t=4195) — session bias vs daily bias, probing discount arrays; [1:35:09](https://youtu.be/woAQ1XJeD7Y?t=5709) — "if we're above NWOG and the clustering of NDOGs"; [1:38:13](https://youtu.be/woAQ1XJeD7Y?t=5893) — above NWOG is a checkbox for sell-side delivery; [2:16:16](https://youtu.be/woAQ1XJeD7Y?t=8176) — strip it down to the chrome.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He calls this "bland, not sexy, rule-based". He says the mirror for longs (price below NWOG with NDOGs above) follows the same logic but does not walk through it here (inference, not stated).

=== [2024 #7 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #7   August 13, 2024 (ID -WItxLARGpE)] — New Day Opening Gap (NDOG): CE rule and as a level  (aliases: new day opening gap; 5 pm close → 6 pm reopen)
- **Kind:** rule / example
- **What ICT says:**
  - The NDOG is the difference between the 5:00 pm ET close and the 6:00 pm ET reopen. "Sticking with the rules", if it is **over 20 handles** (NQ) he annotates its consequent encroachment. Price traded back to the CE level, then rallied.
  - Friday's NDOG and the new week opening gap are also downside targets: if price goes below the NWOG it can run to Friday's NDOG.
- **How to identify:** 5 pm close vs 6 pm open gap on NQ, larger than 20 handles → draw high, low and CE.
- **How to use:** CE of a large NDOG is a retracement/support level on the next session (here, the 8:30 run's origin). The NDOG high was also noted as a level that was formed "last evening".
- **Example in this video:** The NDOG high formed 5→6 pm Monday. Early Tuesday, price traded back down to the NDOG CE, then rallied aggressively (an order block and a breaker block with the CE and a sell-side imbalance/buy-side inefficiency converging). Price then pushed above the London high.
- **Timestamps:** [0:11:38](https://youtu.be/-WItxLARGpE?t=698) — NDOG high, over-20-handle CE rule · [0:18:59](https://youtu.be/-WItxLARGpE?t=1139) — walk-through of the rally from the NDOG
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** the explicit "over 20 handles → mark CE" threshold is the number to keep. NDOG origin was used as the start of the measuring leg for the whole day's study.

=== [2024 #8 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #8   August 14, 2024 (ID zU7iAqg4sSA)] — 6 PM opening price, NDOG and NWOG as reference levels  (aliases: new session opening price, new day opening gap, new week opening gap)
- **Kind:** rule / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - If there is no difference between the 5:00 PM close/settlement and the 6:00 PM first print, there is no gap, so you draw one single line at the 6 PM opening price. Use the 6 PM price (the first print), not the 5 PM. It is a single price level, not a range or inefficiency. It can be swept and revisited, and works like a new day opening gap.
  - If there is a difference, annotate the NDOG (the 5 PM-to-6 PM gap).
  - Baseline for charts: last five weeks of NWOGs and last five days of NDOGs. More if you can keep the chart tidy.
  - Old/filled gaps still act as magnets: a NWOG from Jul 28, 2024 that had been traded through was used as the upside objective on Aug 14 ("filled, cast it out, no, it's one and done: no it's not").
  - Without a gap and without an FVG or wick, price still gravitates back to the 6 PM price as the first real level, ahead of the FVG, which can stay open.
- **How to use:** mark them before the session, use as objectives/retracement targets, and as the first target of a retracement after a CISD.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Aug 14: price went back up to the 6 PM opening line during the retracement, the same area as an FVG and the CE of a wick.
- **Timestamps:** [0:09:27](https://youtu.be/zU7iAqg4sSA?t=567) — 6 PM line, a single level · [1:53:20](https://youtu.be/zU7iAqg4sSA?t=6800) — 6 PM not 5 PM, why · [2:22:51](https://youtu.be/zU7iAqg4sSA?t=8571) — five weeks of NWOG, five days of NDOG
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Nothing contradicts earlier teaching.

=== [2024 #9 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #9  August 15, 2024 (ID pg5XBNkpotM)] — Opening range, 9:30 opening price as fair value, and the gap-open magnet  (aliases: "opening range gap high", "opening range high", "new day opening gap", "RTH opening gap")
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:** The opening range is the first 30 minutes, 9:30–10:00 ET (set the chart to New York time so what you learn matches the channel). The 9:30 open price is annotated; on a gap-up open it forms the "opening range gap high" (top edge of the gap between the prior RTH close/settlement and the open) and the opening range high. Many times, even when price runs straight away from the open, it comes back down to touch the opening price, and then either continues higher later or goes lower into the close. "Fair value for the sake of fair value" is that 9:30 open price. A large premium opening does not always mean a complete return to the prior settlement, but watch for signs it will try. He also wants price to be traded back through; touching alone is not enough for him ("I want a little bit more than that").
- **How to identify:** Drop a line at the RTH close (4:00 pm settlement) and at the 9:30 open price (he preps a horizontal line near market before 9:30 and sets it to the actual open once it prints).
- **How to use:** Use the opening price as the first target for a short from a premium; the prior settlement as the deeper magnet; lunch/afternoon as the time for a delayed return if it does not happen before 1:00 pm.
- **Example in this video:** NQ, open at "352" (partial digits; e.g., the opening price line adjusted from "348" to "352" once the open printed); price spent the first hour above it. His expectation for the afternoon: "I believe we're going to see that again today" (return to this gap and the opening price level).
- **Timestamps:** [0:03:46](https://youtu.be/pg5XBNkpotM?t=226) annotating the 9:30 open · [0:04:36](https://youtu.be/pg5XBNkpotM?t=276) opening range is 9:30–10:00, set platform to NY time · [0:34:51](https://youtu.be/pg5XBNkpotM?t=2091) price often comes back to touch the opening range high = opening price · [0:39:31](https://youtu.be/pg5XBNkpotM?t=2371) fair value is the 9:30 opening price · [1:02:30](https://youtu.be/pg5XBNkpotM?t=3750) expect it in the afternoon if not before 1:00
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Calling the 9:30 price the "opening range high" is his usage on a gap-up open day; don't over-generalize to days with no gap.

=== [2024 #10 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #10  August 16, 2024 (ID qoNQfVzVXZQ)] — Opening Range Gap (ORG) vs. New Day Opening Gap (NDOG)  (aliases: "opening range gap high/low", "RTH gap", "new day opening gap")
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** The **opening range gap** is the difference between the previous day's regular-session (RTH) settlement price and the first print at 9:30 (first tick). On a gap-down open (his example) the settlement price is the "opening range gap high" and the 9:30 open is the "opening range gap low"; for a gap-up the settlement would be the low edge and the open the high edge (my inference, consistent with lecture 9, where the 9:30 open was the gap high). It is an inefficiency (a gap), whereas the opening range is a travelled distance; "one's an inefficiency as a gap, one's an actual travelling from an opening price at 9:30 to the extreme". The **NDOG** is the separate gap between the 5:00 pm settlement and the 6:00 pm reopening (globex), from yesterday's formation. Quadrants can be run on the ORG too, but only when price is near it.
- **How to identify:** Toggle the platform from electronic trading hours (ETH) to regular trading hours (RTH): in RTH view the gap between the previous RTH settlement (he mentions a 4:15 settlement time, caption slightly unclear) and the 9:30 open is visible; in ETH view it gets filled and disappears, so you must draw the settlement and open levels manually (and the same applies on platforms lacking an RTH toggle).
- **How to use:** Treat gap highs/lows, CE, and the 9:30 open as targets and reaction levels; keep them on a notepad rather than the chart.
- **Example in this video:** Thursday: settlement of Wednesday's session vs. Thursday's 9:30 open. On Friday price traded up into the Thursday ORG area and the NDOG (a wick traveled through the previous settlement; relative equal highs at the open swept to roughly the ORG high). Levels shown on a 1m chart by dropping the RTH view, then back to ETH to show the gap vanishes.
- **Timestamps:** [0:23:24](https://youtu.be/qoNQfVzVXZQ?t=1404) settlement vs yesterday's opening price = ORG · [0:25:38](https://youtu.be/qoNQfVzVXZQ?t=1538) settlement and first print define the gap; chart view change shows it · [0:26:28](https://youtu.be/qoNQfVzVXZQ?t=1588) naming gap high vs gap low · [0:41:19](https://youtu.be/qoNQfVzVXZQ?t=2479) ETH vs RTH, draw levels if unavailable
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** "Opening range gap" in this video is strictly the RTH close-to-9:30-open difference; the earlier uses of "new day opening gap" (6 pm) remain separate. The exact wording of which end is "high" depends on gap direction (he gives the gap-down case).

=== [2024 #10 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #10  August 16, 2024 (ID qoNQfVzVXZQ)] — Carry-forward of OR / ORG levels for three days  (aliases: "key levels list", "naked chart with a notepad")
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** Every opening range and opening range gap, if the levels have not been completely used (explored, tested, traded to, repriced), is carried forward as a look-back reference for **three days**. "Very influential in terms of price delivery"; wonderful setups form around them. He keeps the levels on a notepad (or a screenshot of it on his phone) and trades off a **naked chart**, because lines on the chart pull his eye and bias him; he is deliberately quiet about a few purely personal notes that "aren't make-or-break".
- **How to identify:** Yesterday's and the previous day's: OR high, CE, low, lower/upper quadrant, opening price, ORG/NDOG levels, plus weekly 20%/30% levels.
- **How to use:** Ask which of these levels have been "booked" already; use the unvisited ones as draws. If price rips through all intraday levels, go to the 15m/60m/4H/daily to see what it is reaching for (e.g., an old daily high discussed Tuesday).
- **Example in this video:** Friday: today's price had touched the Thursday OR high and upper quadrant but not the CE or lower quadrant, so he watched for an attempt at the CE.
- **Timestamps:** [0:08:04](https://youtu.be/qoNQfVzVXZQ?t=484) carry forward three days · [0:12:56](https://youtu.be/qoNQfVzVXZQ?t=776) notepad abbreviations · [0:14:33](https://youtu.be/qoNQfVzVXZQ?t=873) why a naked chart · [0:43:41](https://youtu.be/qoNQfVzVXZQ?t=2621) if it rips through all of them, go to a higher timeframe
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** First time he states the "three days" carry rule this explicitly; levels you annotate publicly shouldn't be used as your own stop (see Stops/liquidity below).

=== [2024 #11 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #11  August 19, 2024 Begins 9:15am ET (ID aONkyy-HTqE)] — RTH Opening Range Gap (and RTH vs ETH awareness)  (aliases: opening range gap, ORG)
- **Kind:** definition + refinement
- **What ICT says:** The opening range gap is the difference between the regular-session settlement (4:15 pm ET on the prior day; the last tick is the "close") and the 9:30 ET regular-trading-hours opening price. He explains the session mechanics: electronic trading continues until 5:00 pm ET, stops, and resumes at 6:00 pm ET on a normal day. On Friday (or a holiday) it does not resume until Sunday 6:00 pm ET. On an RTH chart there is no candle at 4:15, so the next candle is Monday 9:30.
- **How to identify:** Anchor one edge at the prior-day 4:15 pm settlement (Friday Aug 16 here) and the other at the 9:30 open. Draw a fib on the low and high to get quadrants (lower quadrant, consequent encroachment, upper quadrant). Shade it.
- **How to use:** Price uses the gap's quadrants as reaction levels: "found footing" off the lower quadrant, support at the midpoint, "bodies staying inside" while it grinds. Spending too long inside the gap means they are "not in a hurry" to pierce the high. What he wants to see for upside: a close above the gap's CE, then a next candle that comes down and fails to touch the CE, then a spring toward the upper quadrant (0:25:58).
- **Example in this video:** Price came back up about three-quarters of the gap early (just short of the upper quadrant), later respected the CE and the high repeatedly. Bodies stayed inside, wicks overshot. Candle pokes above the high did not close above it.
- **Timestamps:** [0:17:13](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=1033) what makes it the opening range gap · [0:18:02](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=1082) 4:15 pm settlement, 5:00/6:00 restart rules · [0:55:11](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=3311) RTH vs ETH toggle warning
- **Nuance / caveats:** (My inference from his remarks) The ORG's high edge is Friday's 4:15 pm settlement (19,604), which is also the NWOG's low edge, so the pink ORG sat just below the NWOG; price had to climb through it to reach the NWOG low/lower quadrant. ICT warns he has mis-weighted a level before because ETH vs RTH was toggled on the chart. His habit is to say aloud "I'm back in electronic trading hours" and then confirm the chart label reads ETH.

=== [2024 #11 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #11  August 19, 2024 Begins 9:15am ET (ID aONkyy-HTqE)] — New Week Opening Gap (NWOG)  (aliases: new week opening gap, NWOG high/low/CE)
- **Kind:** definition + refinement
- **What ICT says:** NWOG = Friday's settlement price (the last tick/close) vs. Sunday's 6:00 pm ET opening tick. Whichever is higher is the NWOG high, whichever is lower is the NWOG low. Annotate it as "New Week Opening Gap August 18 2024". A viewer asked why the gap isn't measured lower; he answers that the last tick is the close and that is what you anchor to. In this video Friday's settlement is 19,604 (NQ) and the gap is just under 20 handles (exact edges garbled, caption unclear).
- **How to identify:** Use the 1-minute chart to calibrate the exact settlement and open prints. Drop a fib on the gap low and high for CE, lower quadrant (25%) and upper quadrant (75%). Extend it forward.
- **How to use:** The NWOG is "highly influential ... like a black hole", and price gravitates to it. "If you don't extend the new week opening gaps on your chart and keep them on there for a few weeks you're trading blind." It is a PD array that can serve as a draw once you have identified the nearest liquidity. See *Target selection* below.
- **Example in this video:** Early: price comes back up to the NWOG low and reacts off the lower quadrant of the opening range gap (0:11:28). Later: price runs from the IOFED entry to the NWOG low, then CE and the NWOG high, with bodies respecting each level. The pre-7:00 exit target was the NWOG's lower quadrant (see Target selection).
- **Timestamps:** [0:07:22](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=442) calibrate Friday close and Sunday open on the 1-minute chart · [3:08:50](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=11330) how to define the NWOG high/low · [3:40:25](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=13225) keep NWOGs on the chart for weeks
- **Nuance / caveats:** "Nothing gets stale around here": older gaps (NDOG/NWOG) remain relevant.

=== [2024 #11 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #11  August 19, 2024 Begins 9:15am ET (ID aONkyy-HTqE)] — New Day Opening Gap (NDOG) as a sell-side reach  (aliases: new day opening gap, NDOG, old NDOG)
- **Kind:** refinement / example
- **What ICT says:** When you ask "how far below the sell-side liquidity can price reach?", look for an old NDOG below. Here the Friday Aug 16 NDOG is the outer limit. Price can trade through an old NDOG; ICT says he is not demanding a precision stop-and-turn there. What matters is how price behaves after it trades through (it can go through the low and wick). He teaches to use the specific NDOG whose bodies show respect: "I'm going to refer to this one" over a lower one that did not respond.
- **How to use:** Mark both the NDOG high and low on the chart and the mid-level; expect a retrace to its high at least after a reclaim. Specifically he wanted price to get back above the NDOG, drop to the NDOG high (touch or fail to touch), then run, and not to see a close below the CE of the low-wick on the closing basis.
- **Example in this video:** After the first sweep below the equal lows, price trades down into the Aug 16 NDOG, rallies back, reclaims it and trades down only to the NDOG high, then runs up. Reaction at the NDOG high, then the IOFED gap forms.
- **Timestamps:** [0:12:19](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=739) NDOG as the reach on the sell side · [0:50:27](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=3027) price can trade through old gaps, see how it behaves · [2:00:06](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=7206) which low/NDOG the algorithm refers back to
- **Nuance / caveats:** Referred to as an "old, stale" gap by viewers. ICT rejects that framing.

=== [2024 #12 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #12  August 19, 2024 (ID EQfTD6ntB4A)] — New Week Opening Gap (NWOG)  (aliases: new week opening gap, "coloring book")
- **Kind:** definition + example
- **What ICT says:** NWOG = Sunday 6 PM open compared with Friday's settlement price. Price was above it all day, and the gap was used as support for the buy. Price is likened to children coloring inside a coloring book: it can color outside the lines and he wants to see that, because it gives the opportunity. "Let the wicks do the damage."
- **How to use:** treat the NWOG as a magnet and support on a bullish day; the retracement into it (and the order block nearby) is the buy zone; the lunch macro sell-off also engaged the NWOG.
- **Example in this video:** ES, week of Aug 19. Price swept through the NWOG, cleared inefficiency into the order block (two stacked), CISD, rally; at 11:30-ish the lunch-macro drop came back to the NWOG and the 11:17 low, then rallied.
- **Timestamps:** [0:18:21](https://youtu.be/EQfTD6ntB4A?t=1101) NWOG defined (Sunday 6 PM vs Friday settlement) · [0:28:43](https://youtu.be/EQfTD6ntB4A?t=1723) coloring inside the lines · [0:31:06](https://youtu.be/EQfTD6ntB4A?t=1866) lunch macro engages NWOG
- **Nuance / caveats:** also mentions a new day opening gap and an opening range gap, but does not define them here.

=== [2024 #13 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #13  August 20, 2024 Begins 9:15am ET (ID LMi_mO3AP08)] — New Day Opening Gap (NDOG)  (aliases: new day opening gap, "the gap carried forward")
- **Kind:** definition + rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - NDOG = the difference between the 5:00 PM ET settlement and the 6:00 PM ET reopen (one-hour pause in trading). It is labeled for the next trading day (Aug 19 settle to 6 PM open is "the 20th"), and you carry it forward into the day you are watching.
  - Once price trades through it, do not discard it. He says the common error is to disregard a gap as soon as it fills ("throwing the baby out with the bathwater"); the algorithm refers back to the high, low and quadrant levels again and again whenever price trades through and back to them.
  - Bodies of candles tell you the gap is respected; wicks are allowed to do the damage.
  - Caleb's model: the NDOG or NWOG is the draw. Lookback he gives for Caleb: NDOGs up to about 5 days back, NWOGs up to about 5 weeks back. He adds that older NWOGs do not get stale for sentiment and can still produce reactions.
- **How to identify:** compare the 5:00 PM settlement candle with the 6:00 PM open on a one-minute chart, mark the high and low, optionally add quadrants.
- **How to use:** (bullish case) look for relative equal highs just below or at the NDOG/NWOG so there is liquidity to run on the way to the magnet; (bearish case) relative equal lows just above the NDOG/NWOG. Treat the gap as support after price goes through it and returns (like an old FVG).
- **Example in this video:** NQ, Aug 20 open. Price opens below the NDOG; ICT watches the NDOG high as the draw. A small 15-second gap forms, the wick stops it and price rallies into the NDOG. He highlights a 15-second BISI overlapped with the NDOG as "a very very easy bread and butter setup." Later price drops back to the NDOG low, the candle bodies hold it, and price rallies (see the entry drill concept).
- **Timestamps:** [0:00:49](https://youtu.be/LMi_mO3AP08?t=49) opens below the NDOG, watching it as a draw · [0:05:48](https://youtu.be/LMi_mO3AP08?t=348) "that is my son's model," the draw is the NDOG/NWOG · [0:06:34](https://youtu.be/LMi_mO3AP08?t=394) REH/REL placement and 5-day / 5-week lookback · [0:09:53](https://youtu.be/LMi_mO3AP08?t=593) how the NDOG is built and labeled · [0:10:43](https://youtu.be/LMi_mO3AP08?t=643) don't discard it once filled
- **Nuance / caveats:** the opening range and the NDOG are different things; he reminds viewers of that when he mixes terms. "Five days / five weeks" is the criteria he gives Caleb, not a hard rule for everyone.

=== [2024 #13 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #13  August 20, 2024 Begins 9:15am ET (ID LMi_mO3AP08)] — Opening Range Gap (RTH opening gap) and its midpoint  (aliases: opening range gap, ORG, "the gap from yesterday's settlement to today's 9:30")
- **Kind:** definition + rule (with a high-probability tendency)
- **What ICT says:**
  - The opening range gap is the difference between the previous session's settlement (the RTH close candle at 4:15 PM ET per his description) and the 9:30 AM open. Use the candle close to open as the edges, break it into quadrants (he stresses this is not "quarters theory"), keep it on the chart and extend it through the day.
  - Price will use the high, low and quadrants repeatedly whenever it trades through it and back.
  - Gap lower is a lure to get traders short; you then expect a gap fill. At the very minimum, expect price to go to the 50% (midpoint, consequent encroachment) of the gap; he calls this "one of the strongest things in trading." Use a Fibonacci to mark the midpoint.
  - The gap does not always close completely: it can go halfway and then rocket in the direction of the gap. If it will go lower it goes halfway (or a little more) and fails to close, then drops.
  - You do not even need to be right on bias for this retracement trade.
  - He also uses the "opening range" as 9:30 to 10:00 (30 minutes) for the beginning of the session; later he corrects himself for using "opening range" for the 9:31 moment.
- **How to use:** if the open gaps lower, drop the fib on the first tick; if there is range between current price and the gap midpoint, he will go long right there (he says he has done it many times) and target the midpoint. If bullish with a higher-timeframe target, expect price to go above the gap and then use the gap as support.
- **Example in this video:** NQ, Aug 20. Price gapped lower from Monday's settlement, took the ORG, went above it, came back and used it as support. A consequent encroachment of about "834" (caption; likely 19,834) is cited as the midpoint; the ORG high is around 19,850.25 (he places it "on the close of that candle").
- **Timestamps:** [0:17:14](https://youtu.be/LMi_mO3AP08?t=1034) ORG on the 1m chart · [0:37:21](https://youtu.be/LMi_mO3AP08?t=2241) ORG high at 19,850.25, extend through the day · [0:38:09](https://youtu.be/LMi_mO3AP08?t=2289) gap lower, mark 50%, no bias needed · [0:49:29](https://youtu.be/LMi_mO3AP08?t=2969) go long on the first tick if there is range to the midpoint
- **Nuance / caveats:** he says people disregard gaps because "everybody knows about gaps," which is why the setup is ignored; the half-fill is the minimum expectation, not a guarantee.

=== [2024 #13 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #13  August 20, 2024 Begins 9:15am ET (ID LMi_mO3AP08)] — Institutional Order Entry Drill (as applied to the NDOG)  (aliases: institutional orderflow entry drill)

He says the entry is the candle plus one tick, which gets filled on the retrace. The first objective is the high-water mark of the daily SIBI, then the volume imbalance (pink/yellow area).

Price takes the NDOG low, rallies (displacement), pulls back, the FVG forms, the entry triggers, price rallies to the SIBI high; it then digs back into an order block and rebounds almost immediately; it did not fill the lower part so a portion was left open; price then reaches the lower quadrant with speed and goes straight to CE. Short-term high made above after the target; he says he would have taken a partial.

=== [2024 #16 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #16  August 24, 2024 (ID kuURDD1ajdQ)] — Opening Range Gap (RTH) and its 70% midpoint tendency  (aliases: ORG, premium/discount opening range gap)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - ORG = previous regular-trading-hours close (4:15 PM ET close; he cites the 4:14 candle) to the 9:30 open the next trading day. If it opens above, that is a premium ORG; below is a discount ORG.
  - He says he taught this year that 70% of the time the midpoint (CE) of that gap gets traded to. Measure it on the first tick, then add quadrants; the midpoint matters most.
  - Track the undelivered portion (from previous settlement to the day's low, if it hasn't fully closed) and project it forward.
- **Example in this video:** NQ, Aug 23: he wanted to see price drop to the ORG CE but would only act at premium levels. Price dropped into the undelivered portion during the lunch hour; the candle bodies respected the Aug 18 NWOG, and the gap delivered.
- **Timestamps:** [0:34:12](https://youtu.be/kuURDD1ajdQ?t=2052) ORG definition and 70% CE · [0:34:59](https://youtu.be/kuURDD1ajdQ?t=2099) undelivered portion · [0:35:46](https://youtu.be/kuURDD1ajdQ?t=2146) lunch-hour delivery respects the NWOG
- **Nuance / caveats:** "70%" is his statement; adds a number to M24-013's "very high probability" language.

=== [2024 #17 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #17  August 26, 2024  Impeding Barriers (ID iLe_4m84_yw)] — Opening Range Gap (RTH) — small / "anemic" gap protocol  (aliases ICT uses: opening range gap high/low, "previous settlement", "little unchanged", discount gap / lower gap opening)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - On the RTH chart, the opening range gap (ORG) is the space between the previous day's regular-hours settlement and the 9:30 ET open. The previous settlement (Friday's 4:14 pm ET RTH price here) is the ORG high when the open is lower, and the 9:30 opening price is the ORG low. On Friday the market keeps trading electronically after the RTH close for another 46 minutes. On other days it closes and reopens an hour later at 6:00 pm ET.
  - Before 9:30 he labels the ORG low "opening range gap low / settlement" and fixes the label when the real open prints. Toggling between RTH and electronic (ETH) hours lets you see the gap.
  - "Little unchanged" means the 9:30 open is less than about 30–40 handles from the previous settlement. If the market cannot muster a bigger difference at the opening bell, he sits on his hands until 10:00.
  - The first question for a morning session is whether the market is opening significantly higher or lower than the previous settlement. Larger gaps "invite larger volatility and a lot more protraction". A very small gap signals a market reluctant to run.
  - Scale reference: a 20-handle move is 19,732 to about 19,752 on the chart (the caption says "7,52", so 19,752 is inferred). The ORG that day was not much bigger than that.
- **How to identify:** compare the previous RTH settlement price (4:14 pm ET candle) with the 9:30 open on the RTH chart. If the difference is under about 30–40 handles, it is a weak gap.
- **How to use:**
  - Protocol for a weak gap: sit still, wait until 10:00, wait for the Silver Bullet or something more obvious, and give the algorithm the full first 30 minutes (9:30–10:00). "I don't care if there's a fair value gap." Ignore anything that shows up before then.
  - Don't elect a liquidity target or take a trade early. He says that over about 25 years of using ORGs he has been wrong when he forced it.
  - Conditional he gave: had the open been a higher gap, at or above the 8/21 NDOG, he would have looked for a drop into the 8/25 NWOG (using its consequent encroachment) and then a long up into the buy side above.
  - Do not treat the ORG high as the CE. The CE is the midpoint between the ORG low and high, which is a different price.
- **Example in this video:** Mon 8/26/2024 (stream starts about 9:31 ET). Pre-open, price had a higher-gap setup but erased it. On the 2-minute chart it hammered the CE of the 8/25 NWOG and Friday's settlement, then dropped and displaced below relative equal lows (sell side disturbed). It had already taken a buy-side level and was seeking minor short-term buy side above. At 9:30 it opened with a small discount (lower) gap just under settlement. Price traded back above the ORG high (settlement), stayed inside the 8/25 NWOG, failed hard near the 8/21 NDOG, and returned to the southern end of the ORG. He sat out all of 9:30–10:00.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:01:41](https://youtu.be/iLe_4m84_yw?t=101) — RTH vs ETH, labelling ORG low/settlement.
  - [0:08:55](https://youtu.be/iLe_4m84_yw?t=535) — "little unchanged" = under 30–40 handles, so wait until 10:00.
  - [0:11:21](https://youtu.be/iLe_4m84_yw?t=681) — rules of engagement, 4:14 pm settlement mechanics.
  - [0:16:58](https://youtu.be/iLe_4m84_yw?t=1018) — "sit still, wait till 10:00, wait for Silver Bullet", the 25-years remark.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** this is a "don't engage" filter, not an entry. He teaches Caleb to treat a weak ORG as a cue for patience (he also uses the ORG in the coin-flip challenge below). The 10:00 wait is framed as the protocol he elected for Caleb. He says other traders may be positioned early and that is fine.

=== [2024 #17 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #17  August 26, 2024  Impeding Barriers (ID iLe_4m84_yw)] — New Day / New Week Opening Gaps (NDOG / NWOG) as nested magnets — "Caleb's model" draw  (aliases ICT uses: "black hole", draw on liquidity, layered/nested gaps)
- **Kind:** rule / entry model (draw only; entry is an FVG or iFVG)
- **What ICT says:**
  - Caleb's draw on liquidity is NDOGs and NWOGs. When several are layered or nested together they act "like a big black hole" and a very large magnet that "sucks price in". Retail tools (support/resistance lines, pitchforks) will not show this.
  - The model chain he lays out: OTE (his long-running flagship), then the 2022 model (his daughter's), then the Silver Bullet (Cameron's), and now NDOG/NWOG plus an FVG, "first presentation", as Caleb's model.
  - Gap CE and individual levels matter. Price hammering the CE of the 8/25 NWOG was part of pre-market reading.
- **How to identify:** mark each NDOG/NWOG on the chart and extend it right. Look for clusters where two or more overlap or sit close together below (or above) price.
- **How to use:** target the nested cluster as the draw once a valid entry appears. A "real nice setup": price trades up into an FVG or iFVG, rejects, and then runs down into the nested gaps. Use the CE of a gap as the reaction level.
- **Example in this video:** the 8/23 NDOG and the 8/18 NWOG were nested below price, with the 8/21 NDOG and 8/25 NWOG above. After the 10:00 hour price dropped into the nested 8/23 NDOG / 8/18 NWOG. He pointed to bodies sitting right on the high of the 8/23 NDOG and then accelerating down. "Last Friday" he had said the same cluster would draw price.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:04:04](https://youtu.be/iLe_4m84_yw?t=244) — working inside the 8/25 NWOG.
  - [0:38:08](https://youtu.be/iLe_4m84_yw?t=2288) — layered 8/23 NDOG and 8/18 NWOG as a big draw.
  - [0:51:04](https://youtu.be/iLe_4m84_yw?t=3064) — "huge black hole... Caleb needs to target NDOGs and NWOGs for his draw".
  - [1:17:40](https://youtu.be/iLe_4m84_yw?t=4660) — model progression, including this as Caleb's model.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** he stresses the model must not be swapped for another PD array just because this one has no trade today. Silver Bullet "is there every day… nothing's changed", and he rebuts creators claiming it stopped working.

=== [2024 #18 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #18  August 27, 2024 (ID yOZHawAE8QI)] — Opening Range Gap (RTH ORG) — previous settlement to 9:30 open  (aliases: opening range gap, "settlement gap", discount opening range gap)
- **Kind:** rule / definition
- **What ICT says:**
  - The ORG runs from the previous day's settlement, the last print at 4:14 PM ET, to the 9:30 AM ET opening price. The session ends at 4:15, so there is no 4:15 print. Electronic trading continues until 5:00 PM, pauses for an hour, and resumes at 6:00 PM ET.
  - The "opening range" is 9:30-10:00, a 30-minute interval, not 15 or 5. He calls the other timeframes people teach wrong.
  - First order of business each day is to determine whether there is a gap. A "significant" gap is more than about 20-30 handles on NQ.
  - The midpoint (consequent encroachment, CE) is "our first threshold". Roughly 70% of the time, price trades at least to the CE in the first 30 minutes. He restates this as "you got 70% chance" that at least half the gap is filled. He calls this algorithmic, and the captions once render it as "7%" in error.
  - No bias is needed to study this: no daily bias and no session bias. It is just a way to watch how price is delivered.
  - Plot quadrant levels (25/50/75%) on the gap. Set an alert on the CE so a one-tick cross rings.
- **How to identify:** High = previous settlement (4:14 PM print). Low = 9:30 opening tick (when the open is below settlement, a "discount" ORG). Reverse if the open is above settlement. Extend the lines across the day.
- **How to use:**
  - Pre-plot the settlement line before the open (he did this at ~9:25). Park a second line away from price during electronic hours, then drag it to the 9:30 print when it appears.
  - Watch for the first reach to the lower quadrant, then the CE, then the full closure. The partial-fill sequence is the study.
  - If no FVG has formed yet on the 1m and there is minor sell-side liquidity just under price, he flags a profile to study: drop, clear the sell side, then return up and hit the mid-gap.
  - "Don't use it to pick every entry". Use it to learn what constitutes a valid run from a level.
- **Example in this video:**
  - NQ 1m, Tue 8/27/24. Settlement is quoted as "568 and 3/4" (likely 19568.75, caption unclear). The open was well below it, so the gap is a "very deep discounted" ORG, and the settlement price sat inside the Aug 16 NDOG.
  - Price first dipped to the lower quadrant and could not reach the CE (swept minor sell-side liquidity first).
  - It later ran back up and closed about 3/4 of the gap, "fell short" of full closure. ICT notes this meant the 70% half-fill happened before the 10:00 mark.
  - Afterwards price consolidated near the gap high (the settlement price) and then ran on into the NDOG/NWOG cluster.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:02:16](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=136) — plots settlement and prepares the ORG.
  - [0:07:55](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=475) — CE of the ORG, 9:30-10:00 is 30 minutes not 15, 70% trade to mid-gap.
  - [0:30:38](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=1838) — restates: gap over ~30 handles, 70%, half minimum.
  - [0:47:32](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=2852) — settlement is the 4:14 PM print; session hours.
- **Nuance / caveats:**
  - ICT says "find it in a book, it's not out there".
  - He uses the ORG purely as a draw/target framework, not a trade signal.
  - Extra context: he opens his operating window at 7:00 ET (see Rules).

Price broke lower and created an order block, which he used as initial entry. Then, as price wicked up to the CE of the wick/inefficiency, he added ("I'll take that too").
