=== [2024 #18 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #18  August 27, 2024 (ID yOZHawAE8QI)] — New Day / New Week Opening Gap clusters ("black hole") and shelf life  (aliases: NDOG, NWOG, nested opening gaps)
- **Kind:** rule / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - When NDOGs and NWOGs are nested or clustered, they act like a gravitational pool that keeps drawing price in, trading back and forth to them over several days. He says it is "not one and done".
  - Do not treat them as stale after one visit. "Keep them" and extend them. Do not do the "old pivots (R1, R2) trace it and throw it away" behavior.
  - Beginner scope: for a month or two, only focus on NDOGs within the last 5 days and NWOGs within the last 5 weeks. He says NDOGs older than that can be used, as he addressed in comments, and he used the Aug 16 NDOG as the NQ short target on Aug 27.
  - A single low can be a three-way confluence: the old NDOG low (Aug 23), the lower quadrant of the NWOG of Aug 18, and the CE of a candle's wick. Price stopped to the tick there and ran higher.
  - Backtest note: log how many candles it took to reach the CE of the NWOG and how much drawdown in handles it had before.
- **How to use:** Draw on liquidity, targets, and confluence for reversals ("the algorithm is going to refer to them").
- **Timestamps:**
  - [1:01:14](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=3674) — "huge black hole" of nested NDOG/NWOG.
  - [1:25:31](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=5131) — clustering; don't throw them out when traded to.
  - [1:39:59](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=5999) — Aug 23 NDOG low + Aug 18 NWOG lower quadrant + wick CE.
  - [2:27:26](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=8846) — 5 days / 5 weeks beginner rule.
- **Nuance / caveats:** The week-opening gap on "the 18th of August" is a Sunday open (i.e. the NWOG of the week beginning Aug 19); the captions give it as stated.

=== [2024 #19 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #19  August 28, 2024 (ID i3olkndPmEE)] — Opening range gap (RTH opening gap, ORG)  (aliases: opening range gap, RTH gap, premium/discount gap)
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:** In regular trading hours view, the ORG is the space between the previous day's RTH settlement (the 4:14/4:15 PM ET close shown on the chart) and the 9:30 AM opening price. If the open is lower than the settle it is a discount gap (he colours it blue; premium gap would be the opposite). He expects price to retrace at least half of the gap within the first 30 minutes of trading, with a roughly 70% chance. Annotate it by drawing a rectangle from the opening price to the settlement price. For a discount gap, the expectation is buy-side delivery (rally) back toward the settle price; minimum 50% in 30 minutes.
- **How to identify:** Toggle RTH on; the 9:30 candle gaps from the prior 4:14 candle. In electronic (24-hour) trading that 9:30 candle has no gap, so the gap does not exist there.
- **How to use:** Treat the ORG's low (the 9:30 opening price) as a magnet/reference. In this video the ORG low coincided with NDOG levels and acted as the target for a sell-off after price ran up to the first FVG.
- **Example in this video:** Wed Aug 28: opens below the 4:14 PM Tuesday settlement (discount gap). Price rallies to take half the gap and the full gap (also overlapping the 6 PM NDOG's two-tick gap), sweeps buy-side, then reverses.
- **Timestamps:** [1:00:07](https://youtu.be/i3olkndPmEE?t=3607) — ORG is not an FVG; [1:06:30](https://youtu.be/i3olkndPmEE?t=3990) — RTH view, 4:14 settle vs 9:30 open; [1:07:21](https://youtu.be/i3olkndPmEE?t=4041) — 70% chance half in 30 minutes.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He is re-teaching because students rushed. The new day opening gap (6:00 PM ET open vs 5:00 PM close) here was only about two ticks, overlapping half of the ORG.

=== [2024 #20 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #20  August 29, 2024 (ID NMCy1H2_Wr8)] — Opening range gap (premium gap) and "too expensive" — not chasing  (aliases: ORG, RTH gap, premium gap, don't chase)
- **Kind:** rule / application
- **What ICT says:** NQ opened Thursday with a large gap above the previous settlement (premium opening range gap) and ran straight up for the first minutes without any retracement. When price is "really, really elevated" from the ORG low (the previous settlement), it is expensive: he elects to look for shorts rather than chase longs. The gap can stay open all day, but eventually price tends to trade back to it, giving a better discount. Do not try to catch a run just because it is running ("mass transit": if you miss the bus, wait 20 minutes for the next one, like another macro). If price takes off and you are not already positioned, sit on your hands and let it come back for a low-risk entry (he referenced a Tuesday two weeks earlier).
- **How to use:** Halfway of the ORG is marked as a reference; the ORG low is the prior settle.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Aug 29: opens with a huge premium gap, runs to the Aug 27 NDOG and the Aug 18 NWOG area and through them in about two 1m candles, toward quadruple-high buy-side at 19,694.25. He does not buy; plan is short on the first valid presentation if it breaks down.
- **Timestamps:** [0:18:34](https://youtu.be/NMCy1H2_Wr8?t=1114) — don't chase, let it come back; [0:19:22](https://youtu.be/NMCy1H2_Wr8?t=1162) — bus analogy; [0:29:05](https://youtu.be/NMCy1H2_Wr8?t=1745) — very expensive vs ORG low, ORG may stay open all day.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Continuation of the ORG discussion from lecture 19, now with a premium gap instead of a discount gap.

=== [2024 #20 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #20  August 29, 2024 (ID NMCy1H2_Wr8)] — Layered NDOG/NWOG levels as a high-resistance liquidity run  (aliases: high resistance liquidity run, spider web, opening gaps layered)
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:** When many new day and new week opening gaps (here the Aug 27 NDOG, the Aug 23 gap he had called a huge magnet / "black hole", and the Aug 18 NWOG) are stacked around price, a run through them is held back "like a cobweb tangled around its legs". It is not an absence of buying/selling pressure; the algorithm references all those levels and offers back-and-forth price. If price blasts through them in one or two candles it should have no problem reaching the target; if it keeps petering out above them it is a high-resistance run. Traders who do not plot these gaps never notice why. A strong "fly" can still escape the web and run to "the sugar" (the buy-side).
- **How to identify:** Plot NWOG/NDOG levels; look for price stalling and chopping between them instead of expanding.
- **How to use:** If price drives through them fast it implies the buy-side at 19,694 gets targeted and then likely a puke-out and drop into the ORG; if it chops, stay out. Judge strength above the high: does it accelerate through the new week opening gap high and keep a bullish body without wicks retracing?
- **Example in this video:** NQ Aug 29 morning: runs through the gap levels within two candles, gets close to the 19,694 quadruple high and peters out (twice), then hangs around inside the gap cluster for most of the session.
- **Timestamps:** [0:13:25](https://youtu.be/NMCy1H2_Wr8?t=805) — NDOG Aug 27 and NWOG above; [0:30:41](https://youtu.be/NMCy1H2_Wr8?t=1841) — aggressively slicing through them implies a run to buy-side; [1:49:05](https://youtu.be/NMCy1H2_Wr8?t=6545) — high-resistance explanation; [1:52:16](https://youtu.be/NMCy1H2_Wr8?t=6736) — spider web analogy.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He explains that the paid mentorship streams were built on identifying high- vs low-resistance liquidity run signatures day by day.

=== [2024 #21 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #21  August 30, 2024 (ID eYYyonGwTRs)] — Layered NDOG/NWOG clusters as speed bumps  (aliases: nested opening gaps, high-resistance run conditions, "congestion")
- **Kind:** rule / condition filter
- **What ICT says:** When new day and new week opening gaps are nested close together near price (here the Aug 27 NDOG, the Aug 18 NWOG and the Aug 16 NDOG), price needs a real, significant move to slice through, and the algorithm "constantly refers back" to them while they are within the time frame he specifies. A cluster acts as an initial draw. If price fails to even reach the lowest threshold of the cluster (here the Aug 18 NWOG low) and drops below its low, it is tipping its hand that it does not want to go higher. This is not a lack of buyers or a sellers' imbalance (he calls that nonsensical): you are measuring the efficiency of price reaching premium arrays and whether it has the "pursuit" to reach the next one. If instead price slices aggressively through (one or two candles), ignoring them, it signals it is in a hurry, usually because of an imbalance or liquidity pool it is targeting.
- **How to identify:** Plot the last few NDOG/NWOG ranges; check proximity to current price. With few of them near price, runs are cleaner and more elongated ("interstate highway early in the morning"); clustered close together they impede flow and produce short-lived moves ("speed bumps").
- **How to use:** If bearish, you want discount arrays "demolished" with no respect, for a fast run; if price struggles at each one, the market is not fast, do not push. Test: does a premium level repel price, does a discount level hold it?
- **Example in this video:** NQ Aug 30: opens with a huge premium opening gap above, runs up, falls about one tick short of the Aug 18 NWOG high (about 19,629 vs a 19,628.75 high), rolls back into the 9:30 opening price / Aug 27 NDOG high, and chops for the rest of the morning with repeated short-term highs taken and little follow-through.
- **Timestamps:** [0:24:20](https://youtu.be/eYYyonGwTRs?t=1460) — nested gaps, what slicing through means; [0:25:08](https://youtu.be/eYYyonGwTRs?t=1508) — failing to reach the NWOG low = tipping its hand; [0:31:15](https://youtu.be/eYYyonGwTRs?t=1875) — cleaner when gaps are far apart; [0:32:49](https://youtu.be/eYYyonGwTRs?t=1969) — disrespect of the array shows urgency; [0:38:38](https://youtu.be/eYYyonGwTRs?t=2318) — one tick short of the NWOG high.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Builds directly on lecture 19/20 (layered gaps, high-resistance runs). New here: the "tipping its hand" test and the explicit proximity/clean-run comparison. He also calls the resulting chop "time distortion" (caption unclear).

=== [2024 #22 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ September 04, 2024 (ID Ipjk9S8HTbA)] — RTH Opening Range Gap (ORG) and its 70% midpoint rule  (aliases ICT uses: opening range gap, "regular trading hours gap", "midgap", "half the gap", "the gap" between settlement and open)
- **Kind:** rule (statistical tendency) / definition
- **What ICT says:**
  - Toggle TradingView to regular trading hours (RTH) to see it: the gap runs from the previous day's closing/settlement price to the first traded price at the 9:30 open. He toggles the chart between RTH and ETH (electronic trading hours) to show it.
  - If price opens below the prior settlement it is a **discount ORG**; if it opens above it is a **premium ORG** (he only walks through the discount case in detail, so the premium side is the implied mirror).
  - The midpoint of the gap is the key level: "70% of the time" price will trade to that midpoint ("half the gap") **between 9:30 and 10:00 ET**. He says he derived the figure going back to 1987 using pre-electronic/Globex numbers, and tells students to journal it daily to verify it themselves.
  - Because the open was lower than settlement, price is more likely to trade **up** first, seeking buy-side liquidity. As a student you are not trying to call full gap closure, just the move into the midpoint.
  - 70% is not 100%: the previous day (Tue 9/3) opened lower, only a tiny wick got into the gap, never reached even a quarter of it, and the market "fell out of bed" — he uses this as the proof it is a probability, not a guarantee.
- **How to identify:** Switch to RTH; draw the box from prior RTH close to 9:30 open; mark its midpoint (50%). Note the size — on 9/4 NQ was "over 100 handles" below prior settlement, which he calls a very large discount gap. Log in your journal whether the midpoint traded between 9:30 and 10:00.
- **How to use:** Treat the midpoint as a first target (partial) for longs taken after a discount open, or as an area where a trade is "done" for the learner — "you don't care what it does after that." Combine with the first-presentation FVG as an entry and a New Day/Week Opening Gap above the midpoint as the next objective (see below).
- **Example in this video:** NQ, Wed 9/4/2024. Large discount ORG (>100 handles below Tue's settlement). At the 9:30 open price makes a small drop, then rallies, creating the first 1-min FVG (shaded white). Price then trades up to the ORG midpoint inside 9:30–10:00 — ICT: "there's the 70% strike rate." The prior day (Tue 9/3) is the counterexample (gap opened lower, midpoint never tagged).
- **Timestamps:** [0:14:48](https://youtu.be/Ipjk9S8HTbA?t=888) — "70% of the time between 9:30 and 10:00" the midgap is traded to · [0:31:55](https://youtu.be/Ipjk9S8HTbA?t=1915) — premium vs discount ORG and the midpoint · [0:32:47](https://youtu.be/Ipjk9S8HTbA?t=1967) — Tuesday's failure case · [0:33:35](https://youtu.be/Ipjk9S8HTbA?t=2015) — origin of the 70% figure (back to 1987)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He frames this as a "low-hanging fruit" model, not the best-of-the-best setup. Wicks may exceed levels; the probability is for trading *to* the midpoint, not holding it.

=== [2024 #22 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ September 04, 2024 (ID Ipjk9S8HTbA)] — NDOG / NWOG inside the ORG as the extension target  (aliases ICT uses: new day opening gap, new week opening gap, "Terminus" for the final target)
- **Kind:** entry model / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - If a New Day Opening Gap (or New Week Opening Gap) sits **inside the ORG and above its midpoint** on a discount-gap day, the odds improve that price keeps going from the midpoint up to that gap — "a very strong 70%."
  - It does not need to be both an NDOG and an NWOG, and you do not need the whole gap: "it could be just the low" of the gap — the first level reached is the target ("low-hanging fruit"). Do not hunt "the best of the best."
  - The model as he states it at 0:59:51: **buy the first fair value gap → partial at the midgap → Terminus at the NDOG low.**
  - After that, expect price to possibly sweep around both sides of the NDOG and reach for a full gap closure (the ORG high) — treated as a bonus, not the plan.
- **How to identify:** On RTH, draw the ORG; mark its midpoint; plot the NDOG and check whether it lies in the upper half of the ORG. NDOG on 9/4 was around 18993.75 and 18999.75 (caption unclear: "18993 and three quarters, 18,999 and three quarters").
- **How to use:** Long entry in the first FVG, take a partial at ORG midpoint, run the remainder to the NDOG low. Longs that did not take profit at the NDOG got run over fast — he stresses taking something off there.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 9/4/2024: after the rally to the ORG midpoint, price continues to the NDOG (upper quadrant of the ORG), then sells off sharply around the 10:00 ET news print (see liquidity concept) toward Tuesday's daily low. Later in the session price pushes slightly above the relative equal highs and the NDOG top — "went just a little above it" — and he leaves open a further attempt to the full gap closure.
- **Timestamps:** [0:33:35](https://youtu.be/Ipjk9S8HTbA?t=2015) — NDOG/NWOG inside the ORG above midpoint · [0:36:47](https://youtu.be/Ipjk9S8HTbA?t=2207) — the whole sequence recapped as a model that "will hit, will stick" · [0:37:35](https://youtu.be/Ipjk9S8HTbA?t=2255) — only one level needed, low-hanging fruit · [0:59:51](https://youtu.be/Ipjk9S8HTbA?t=3591) — FVG → partial midgap → Terminus NDOG low
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** "Terminus" is used loosely here as the final target of the trade (he also says "wait for my partials and wait for my Terminus to be hit," [0:56:43](https://youtu.be/Ipjk9S8HTbA?t=3403)); he does not define it as a named model in this video.

=== [2024 #22 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ September 04, 2024 (ID Ipjk9S8HTbA)] — Consequent Encroachment (CE) of the Aug 13, 2024 daily FVG  (aliases ICT uses: midpoint, "mid gap," BISI = buy-side imbalance sell-side inefficiency)
- **Kind:** definition / example
- **What ICT says:**
  - An FVG is bounded by the low of one candle and the high of another (the gap between candles 1 and 3); its midpoint is the consequent encroachment. He describes the Aug 13 daily FVG as a BISI.
  - He draws the CE as "the middle of the range defined by this candle's low and that candle's high" and uses the same midpoint idea on the daily FVG, the ORG and the 1-min first FVG.
  - If price intends to rise from a lower level, it is better if it does **not** come back down to touch a midpoint below (which gap's midpoint is not clear from the captions).
  - He notes a small volume imbalance in the Aug 13 FVG area that "gets worked out" naturally by the candle's high.
- **How to identify:** Mark the gap between the two outer candles' wicks; add a line at 50%.
- **How to use:** Use the Aug 13 daily FVG high / CE / low as the daily-timeframe draw for the pre-open plan: price ranging between the FVG's high and CE before the open; if price rolls over and takes the opening-range low, the next draw is into that daily FVG below.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 15-min pre-open: price ranges between the Aug 13 daily FVG high and its CE; ICT's stated plan ([0:09:45](https://youtu.be/Ipjk9S8HTbA?t=585)) is to see price trade to the ORG midgap, possibly reach the upper part of the ORG, then roll over and, if it takes the low formed in the new opening range, drop into the Aug 13 FVG.
- **Timestamps:** [0:06:33](https://youtu.be/Ipjk9S8HTbA?t=393) — Aug 13 daily FVG definition and midpoint · [0:10:34](https://youtu.be/Ipjk9S8HTbA?t=634) — plan: midgap then maybe the Aug 13 FVG below · [0:22:08](https://youtu.be/Ipjk9S8HTbA?t=1328) — "here is consequent encroachment"
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He explicitly says he is "not trying to push a narrative" pre-open — exploring both sides.

=== [2024 #24 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ NonFarm Payroll September 06, 2024 (ID MxaJD1WIzos)] — New Day Opening Gap (NDOG) and RTH opening gap annotation
- **Kind:** example only / how-to
- **What ICT says:** On a 1-minute chart he marks the 6:00 PM open (the low of the gap, in this case) and the 4:59 PM close (the high) to define the New Day Opening Gap. For the RTH opening gap he anchors on the 1m chart: previous close/settlement to the first print at 9:30 (he turned off electronic trading hours for the settlement line). Friday price traded up into the NDOG before 9:30, and at the 9:30 open left a small gap that price tried to fill from the open, then traded back inside.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Sept 6: pre-market run into the NDOG; at 9:30 a small opening gap partly filled; he cites old NDOGs as places the algorithm uses to build fake bull flags (see Liquidity engineering).
- **Timestamps:** [0:05:35](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=335) marking the 6:00 open / 4:59 close; [1:15:30](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=4530) opening range gap on 1m
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Mechanical annotation only; no new rule.

=== [2024 #25 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ September 09, 2024 (ID Onca4KgfQB4)] — RTH Opening Range Gap (ORG), its midpoint ("mid gap") and quadrants  (aliases ICT uses: opening range gap, opening gap, "the Gap", half gap, upper/lower quadrant)
- **Kind:** definition + rule (with a stated statistic)
- **What ICT says:**
  - The opening range is 9:30 to 10:00 ET.
  - The opening range gap is previous day's settlement (the 4:15 PM ET close for index futures) to the very first print at the 9:30 open. It must be viewed with RTH (regular trading hours) in mind.
  - He divides the gap into halves and quadrants: mid gap (half gap, consequent-encroachment-style level), upper quadrant and lower quadrant levels. He puts these on both the 15-second and 1-minute charts (matching colors, e.g. the blue line = upper quadrant on both).
  - Statistic: "70% of the time" the half-gap price level gets traded to / repriced to in the first 30 minutes of trading. He stresses 70% is not 100%.
  - Gaps have a magnetic effect on price in the opening minutes "but not all the time"; a gap can stay open for days, so a gap alone is not a 100% probability fill.
  - How the market opens relative to the previous day's settlement is what frames the morning-session setup. The afternoon is judged by different things (lunch/afternoon he looks at other features).
- **How to identify:** Mark Friday 4:15 PM ET settlement and the 9:30 first print; draw the midpoint (50%), the upper quadrant (the level between the midpoint and the opening print, i.e. the upper-quarter line of the gap) and the lower quadrant (the level between the midpoint and the settlement). Exact quadrant arithmetic is not spelled out in this video.
- **How to use:** A large gap opening higher builds an "expensive" premium. He will not buy it. Any move down from the premium sets the stage for a trade toward half the gap. His selling zone this day was between a broken short-term low and the upper quadrant of the gap, with the mid gap as target and the lower quadrant as a further level to watch.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Mon 2024-09-09. Large gap up from Friday's settlement (a big Friday drop preceded it). Price opens higher into the bottom of a weekly inversion FVG, builds a series of small short-term highs, then breaks a short-term low. ICT wants a PD array to form between that low and the upper quadrant of the gap; around 10:30 to 10:36 ET (video ≈1:05–1:10, clock time inferred as video time + ~9:30) an institutional order flow entry drill forms, price rolls over and trades down to the mid gap. He notes it did NOT reach half gap within the first 30 minutes, but it did get there, so the 70% rule was not "wrong".
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:10:28](https://youtu.be/Onca4KgfQB4?t=628) — defines opening range (9:30–10:00) and the opening range gap (settlement to first 9:30 print)
  - [0:26:56](https://youtu.be/Onca4KgfQB4?t=1616) — "70% of the time" half gap is traded to in the first 30 minutes; 4:15 PM ET settlement
  - [0:48:55](https://youtu.be/Onca4KgfQB4?t=2935) — only needs price to reach the area below the low; target is the gap between candle low and upper quadrant, then mid gap
  - [1:20:25](https://youtu.be/Onca4KgfQB4?t=4825) — post-mortem: not within 30 minutes, but did trade to mid gap and gave the PD array
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Clarifies that the 70% half-gap statistic is a tendency, not a rule that it must happen. The specific price levels he quotes ("64.75", "18,583.20", "84.75%", "791") are garbled in the captions (caption unclear), so no precise gap boundaries can be reconstructed from this video.

=== [2024 #26 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ September 11, 2024 (ID A1mLNIoEsq0)] — Opening range gap — half-gap and full-gap closure at the open (RTH)  (aliases: opening gap, previous settlement to opening price, gap closure)
- **Kind:** example only (applies earlier teaching) + RTH/ETH nuance
- **What ICT says:**
  - The opening range gap is previous settlement (RTH close) to the open price. Today's reference: previous settlement 18,846 and an open in the high 18,920s, "a large gap". He shows it on RTH data.
  - Price immediately traded half the gap (opening bell, ~9:30 ET) and then did an almost full gap closure; a deep retracement back inside the opening range gap followed. He marks the midpoint (CE) of the gap and the "full gap closure" level, and notes that after the closure price kept "wicking" and printing bodies at a key level near the previous-settlement area.
  - For regular-trading-hours versus electronic-trading-hours: he deliberately switches the 1-minute chart to ETH (electronic trading hours) data when he wants to use a particular wick (the wick consequent-encroachment idea below), because those candles' highs/lows differ from RTH-only candles. At the end (1:17:33) he again notes the pocket he's studying shows on ETH data.
  - After the full gap closure it is "retracement back into the open range" in the first 30 minutes, and then "new day opening gap" levels (Sept 11 and Sept 10) act as draw on liquidity targets.
- **How to identify:** Mark prior settlement (4:15 PM ET close) and the first 9:30 print; mark the midpoint.
- **How to use:** Treat the gap's midpoint and far edge as targets from a premium; after closure, watch whether bodies respect the gap's prior-settlement level (deliver below = decidedly weak).
- **Example in this video:** NQ Wed 2024-09-11: settlement 18,846, opens ~18,920s; half gap traded in the first minute after the opening bell (video 0:06:50), near-full gap closure by 0:07:38, then deep retracement and a long back-and-forth.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:02:11](https://youtu.be/A1mLNIoEsq0?t=131) — previous settlement 18,846, huge premium opening on 15m
  - [0:06:50](https://youtu.be/A1mLNIoEsq0?t=410) — opening bell: half the gap already traded
  - [0:07:38](https://youtu.be/A1mLNIoEsq0?t=458) — almost full gap closure, retracement inside the gap
  - [0:11:51](https://youtu.be/A1mLNIoEsq0?t=711) — new day opening gap as draw on liquidity; full gap closure then bodies at a key level
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Same opening range gap framework as 09-09 (M24-025) but the gap here closed within minutes. He does not state the 70% / 30-minute rule again in this video.

=== [2024 #27 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ September 12, 2024 (ID DRuEMX4RhMw)] — Opening range gap size and pre-market behaviour  (aliases: ORG high/low, previous settlement 4:15 PM ET)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Previous settlement is 4:15 PM ET (shown with RTH data). The opening range gap high is the higher of previous settlement and today's open; by definition the other is the gap low.
  - "You guys know the protocol": in the first 30 minutes look for half the gap to fill. Today the gap was about 15 handles and essentially filled almost immediately, also sitting inside the new day opening gap, so it was lackluster.
  - A small opening range gap means a poor, muddy start; don't expect excitement from the opening range gap on those days. A big gap with obvious buy-side and sell-side pools away from the open is better.
  - Pre-market (anything before 9:30): watch whether the gap is being maintained or shrinking toward 9:30. If it shrinks, expect a very muddy morning or at least a muddy first 30 minutes.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 2024-09-12: tiny premium gap above 4:15 PM settlement; price trades down below it at the open; the NDOG for Sept 12 and its CE are also in play.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:02:30](https://youtu.be/DRuEMX4RhMw?t=150) — opening range gap high; half gap "essentially done"; inside NDOG
  - [0:15:54](https://youtu.be/DRuEMX4RhMw?t=954) — small gap (about 15 handles); definition of gap high/low
  - [0:16:43](https://youtu.be/DRuEMX4RhMw?t=1003) — pre-market: gap maintained or shrinking?
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Complements M24-025 (big gap: don't buy premium) with the small-gap case (don't expect much).

=== [2024 #28 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ How To Trade ICT FVGs Correctly \ September 16, 2024 (ID HhgWGduQZQY)] — Opening range gap CE ("mid gap") — first draw of the day, 70% within 30 minutes  (aliases: RTH opening range gap, "consequent encroachment of the opening range gap", ORG midpoint)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Draw a Fibonacci from the previous day's settlement price to the first opening tick at 9:30 (RTH). The 50% is the ORG CE; mark the lower/upper quadrants too.
  - About 70% of the time the midpoint is touched by 10:00 (within the first 30 minutes). "Not 100%": in the mentorship it has failed "one or two times," and it gets hit eventually.
  - It is "my always first draw." Even when price doesn't reach it, the CE is the bias anchor: you expect price to try for it, and the targets before it are the high of day, the opening price (a static price level, not a zone), buy-side liquidity (smooth/equal highs), and the lower quadrant of the ORG.
- **How to use:** Use it as a draw, a first partial or a Terminus. Choosing the easiest first partial (e.g., the lower-quadrant or CE of the gap, or the first FVG) is fine.
- **Example in this video:** 9/16/2024 NQ: gap opening lower; price rallies to the lower quadrant of the ORG but not CE by 10:00; he says "that need not be filled today." The level stays a key level for the session.
- **Timestamps:** [0:45:53](https://youtu.be/HhgWGduQZQY?t=2753) 70% rule; [0:46:41](https://youtu.be/HhgWGduQZQY?t=2801) half the gap hit by 10:00; [0:58:46](https://youtu.be/HhgWGduQZQY?t=3526) how to draw the FIB; [1:15:47](https://youtu.be/HhgWGduQZQY?t=4547) "my always first draw."
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Captions at 0:45 say "7%"; clearly 70%. Appears consistent with earlier mentorship teaching on opening range gaps.

=== [2024 #29 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ How To Manage Missed Entries \ September 17, 2024 (ID fhBnnU44A6E)] — Opening range gap CE ("mid gap") — the 70% first-30-minutes draw and its size filter  (aliases: ORG midpoint, "consequent encroachment of the opening range gap", "70% strike rate")
- **Kind:** rule / model
- **What ICT says:**
  - Opening range gap = difference between yesterday's RTH settlement (last print at 4:14 PM ET; "settles at 4:15") and the first opening tick at 9:30 ET. The "opening range" is the 30-minute interval to 10:00.
  - Drop a Fibonacci from the previous settlement to the 9:30 open; the 50% level (CE) is the bias. About 70% of the time (not 100%, not a guarantee) price trades to the midpoint within the first 30 minutes (before 10:00).
  - Size filter: the gap should be more than 40 handles ("minimum 40"); larger is better; roughly 100 handles is a sweet spot; above about 200 handles he defers the setup because "heavy manipulation" means price may keep running.
  - If the gap is above price it makes the bias for the first 30 minutes down (premium gap -> expect a return to mid gap); mirror for a gap lower.
  - Combine with the economic calendar to improve the hit rate. A gap open above previous day's settlement is also described as a "premium" gap.
  - Expectation: one trade a week from this alone (~50 handles ≈ $1,000 on one mini, per ICT) would already be meaningful; "you have an experiment laboratory for 30 minutes every single day."
- **How to identify:** Check the gap size (settlement to 9:30 open) in handles, plot the 50% and the 25%/75% quadrants.
- **How to use:** Direction of the first FVG trade is set by where mid gap sits relative to price; target = mid gap (or partials en route, lower quadrant, etc.).
- **Example in this video:** NQ Dec contract, Tue 9/17/2024: previous settlement about 19,661.5, open about 19,795 (over 100 handles up, caption figures partly garbled), mid gap 19,728.25, lower quadrant about 19,694.5. Price sweeps Friday's high, forms a 9:33 FVG, trades down and delivers mid gap before 10:00 ("70% strike rate true today"), then lower quadrant 19,694.5 is the next objective. ICT says the gap-down mirror is to go long off the first FVG for mid gap.
- **Timestamps:** [0:13:39](https://youtu.be/fhBnnU44A6E?t=819) how the gap is defined (4:14/4:15 settlement to 9:30 open); [0:24:45](https://youtu.be/fhBnnU44A6E?t=1485) mid gap delivered; [0:33:04](https://youtu.be/fhBnnU44A6E?t=1984) "gap open higher more than 40 handles... 70% strike rate"; [0:52:29](https://youtu.be/fhBnnU44A6E?t=3149) larger gap = more opportunity; [1:09:21](https://youtu.be/fhBnnU44A6E?t=4161) 40 minimum / ~100 sweet spot / over 200 defer.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Adds the explicit 40-handle minimum and 200-handle caution to the #28 teaching of the 70% rule. He says "50 handles" to mid gap although the quoted figures work out to roughly 67 handles; treat the numbers as approximate.

=== [2024 #29 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ How To Manage Missed Entries \ September 17, 2024 (ID fhBnnU44A6E)] — Managing the first-FVG trade — partials, break-even, and holding for mid gap
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Once price breaks a short-term low after you are short, move the stop to cover costs (break-even); if you have more than one contract, take a partial below that short-term low.
  - At mid gap he suggests taking 75–80% off; if you want to try for the whole gap, put the stop above the short-term low and leave the balance ("if it stops you out on the balance, who cares, you had a free look").
  - If you don't know how to select partials: take two partials, keep the stop where it covers costs, and "let the market take you out" on the remainder; the target is only a reference. This "removes all the I have to be right stuff."
  - If trading one contract and the stop runs, accept it and record how you felt; don't think "I wish I had closed it at the maximum open profit."
  - After a loss do not "make up": next day, simply take the setup by the parameters on the smallest size.
- **How to use:** Stage 1: one exit at mid gap; later: partial -> stop to cost -> second partial at the next liquidity (e.g., relative equal lows) -> trail.
- **Example in this video:** In the hypothetical late-session short he walks through (2:00–2:07): short limit one tick above the CE of a wick (~19,745.25), stop above that wick's CE, first partial at the FVG, second partial at the relative equal lows, stop stays at cost, remainder runs to the rejection-block low.
- **Timestamps:** [0:57:15](https://youtu.be/fhBnnU44A6E?t=3435) stop to cover costs, partial below short-term low; [0:58:04](https://youtu.be/fhBnnU44A6E?t=3484) 75–80% off at mid gap; [2:04:31](https://youtu.be/fhBnnU44A6E?t=7471) two partials booked; [2:07:28](https://youtu.be/fhBnnU44A6E?t=7648) let the market take you out.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** First time in the series he states explicit % partial sizes for the mid-gap model.

=== [2024 #31 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Micro Vs. Mini \ September 19, 2024 (ID XiZHGOeVAOk)] — Opening Range Gap (RTH) — huge gap handling, quadrants and midpoint draw  (aliases ICT uses: opening range gap, "mid gap", upper quadrant level, ORG)
- **Kind:** rule / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - The opening range gap is the space between the prior day's settlement/last RTH print (he says "414", i.e. 4:14 PM ET close, caption) and the 9:30 ET opening print. Two data points, two "arrays".
  - If today opens ABOVE yesterday's settlement, the bias/initial draw is LOWER toward the midpoint of that gap (50%, "mid gap" = consequent encroachment of the gap). It does not need to get there that day or that morning; it is only the bias/framework.
  - He claims an approximately 70% tendency ("built-in 70% advantage", "70% strike rate") for price to gravitate to the middle of the opening range gap within the first 30 minutes of the session (he says "first 10... sorry, first 30 minutes").
  - He draws quadrants of the gap: the upper quadrant level (75% of the gap measured from the gap low to the opening high) is the first logical reaching point on a drop, then the mid gap (50%).
  - A very large gap (several hundred handles) changes the plan: it "gives the market a chance to give the public a reason to chase" — price is likely to make a drop first (initial high of day), then run above that initial high to hit buy stops, then drop for the deeper retracement. With a small gap (he says 40–60 handles) the first fair-value-gap short would already have been an entry for him; with this gap he waits.
- **How to identify:** compare the 9:30 open with the prior settlement; draw the gap box; mark 75% / 50% (and 25%); mark the initial high of the day made during the 9:30–10:00 opening range.
- **How to use:** use the gap midpoint as the first draw; do not demand that it trades there. Use the 75% level as the interim/partial level (he describes it as a logical place for a partial, "if you were in a day that didn't have this multiple-100-handle opening gap you could take this as a partial and then aim for the upper quadrant level").
- **Example in this video:** NQ December (micro and mini), 15-second and 1-minute charts, Sept 19, 2024. Open gapped up several hundred handles. Mid gap stated as 19844.5 on the micro and 19843.5 on the mini (about one full handle apart because of the two contracts). Price opened, dropped initially, then ran the initial high (he marks a 19926 level at one point), then retraced.
- **Timestamps:** [0:00:26](https://youtu.be/XiZHGOeVAOk?t=26) midgap shown · [0:05:38](https://youtu.be/XiZHGOeVAOk?t=338) micro vs mini mid gap levels compared · [0:24:58](https://youtu.be/XiZHGOeVAOk?t=1498) "the draw is mid gap in the first 30 minutes" · [0:56:36](https://youtu.be/XiZHGOeVAOk?t=3396) opening range (9:30–10:00) behavior after a very large gap higher · [1:08:44](https://youtu.be/XiZHGOeVAOk?t=4124) the two-array logic (settlement vs 9:30 open)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He explicitly distinguishes the "opening range gap" (prior close to open) from the "opening range" (9:30–10:00). The prior close time is given as "414" in captions (4:14 PM ET) — treat as caption-derived. He does not state the mirror case (open below settlement) but the logic is symmetrical.

=== [2024 #32 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ CFDs Vs. Futures \ September 20, 2024 (ID Vo8WKYKtzIU)] — Opening range gap — "discount" gap, size thresholds, and CFD bridging  (aliases ICT uses: opening range gap, ORG, settlement price (4:14 PM ET) to 9:30 open)
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Futures: the gap is between the prior day's settlement price at 4:14 PM ET (he also says "4:15 or 4:14") and today's 9:30 AM ET opening price. Futures still trade 4:15–5:00 (he says another 45–46 min, "closes at 5:00") and overnight, but the RTH-chart view (regular trading hours) shows the clean gap.
  - If yesterday's 4:14 settlement is higher than today's 9:30 open, it is a DISCOUNT opening range gap (today opens below the prior close). The settlement line is the high of the ORG and the 9:30 open is the low.
  - A small ORG is about 20 handles and is "limp/anemic"; he does not like less than 40 handles. With under 40 handles he uses other tools for the morning or opts out until 10:00 and then trades a Silver Bullet (short mention).
  - You can anticipate the gap near 9:29 (electronic hours): see whether the open will be above or below settlement and guess whether it will be wide.
  - The opening range is the first 30 minutes (9:30–10:00 ET), an algorithmic interval ("anything less than this they're making up stuff"). He says he will teach more uses in the 2024 mentorship and the book, and won't reveal everything before then.
  - For CFD traders: CFDs trade around the clock with no settlement break and no 4:15–6:00 gap, so there is no gap function; bridge it by marking the 4:14 PM ET candle's close (that is the "settlement") and the 9:30 open on the CFD chart. The same two price points give the same information.
- **How to identify:** RTH chart; find the 4:14 PM ET close, the 9:30 open; draw two bold horizontal lines extended; label premium or discount.
- **How to use:** gap size determines morning approach (small gap = harder morning, pay attention to time and yesterday's levels); the previous day's gap and whether it was traded to its midpoint matter.
- **Example in this video:** NQ December micro, Thu Sep 19 settlement (caption reads "2,55", i.e. about 20,055) vs Fri Sep 20 open ("20,035 even", caption) = 20 handle discount gap. Yesterday's gap "barely made its way toward the upper quadrant" and never traded to its midpoint/CE, leaving a vacuum of price below.
- **Timestamps:** [0:07:09](https://youtu.be/Vo8WKYKtzIU?t=429) prior 4:14 settlement candle shown · [0:08:00](https://youtu.be/Vo8WKYKtzIU?t=480) discount opening range gap · [0:09:38](https://youtu.be/Vo8WKYKtzIU?t=578) opening range 9:30–10:00 · [0:20:11](https://youtu.be/Vo8WKYKtzIU?t=1211) under 40 handles → other tools or wait for 10:00 Silver Bullet · [1:04:26](https://youtu.be/Vo8WKYKtzIU?t=3866) CFD chart: mark 4:14 and 9:30
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** In M24-031 he put the small-gap level at 40–60 handles (a first-FVG entry would be fine); here he sets about 40 as the floor below which he changes approach. Prices are caption-derived, so verify.

=== [2024 #32 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ CFDs Vs. Futures \ September 20, 2024 (ID Vo8WKYKtzIU)] — Buy-side raid at the open → trap, then drop to the opening range gap low  (aliases ICT uses: knock out shorts, trap buyers above old high, rake them across the coals)
- **Kind:** example / pattern
- **What ICT says:** at 9:30 the market rallied and took out short-term buy-side liquidity, stopping out anyone short and trapping buyers who bought the break above the old high; price then traded back toward sell-side (sell stops below). The pattern played out as: open → swing up to the high of the ORG (yesterday's 4:14 settlement) → trades through it for more liquidity → back down through the opening price (low of the ORG) → one more pass for buy-side → drops again toward the ORG low.
- **How to identify:** pre-session high wicked at the open, followed by failure to continue.
- **How to use:** do not buy breakouts; look to short from an uptick into a PD array after the raid.
- **Example in this video:** micro NQ 1m, 9:30–9:50 ET Sept 20.
- **Timestamps:** [0:15:23](https://youtu.be/Vo8WKYKtzIU?t=923) the raid and trap · [0:26:35](https://youtu.be/Vo8WKYKtzIU?t=1595) open → high → open low → buy side again
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** same theme as the 031 breakout-buyer trap.

=== [2024 #33 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ How To Avoid Seek & Destroy \ Tape Reading \ September 23, 2024 (ID Qg3O1GkJDeQ)] — Opening range gap — unfilled portion as the draw (premium gap)  (aliases ICT uses: unfilled portion of the opening range gap, mid gap, new day opening gap)

=== [2024 #37 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Premarket Concepts \ September 30, 2024 (ID l7eu9yvUUtg)] — Opening range gap / new week opening gap as entry confluence  (aliases: ORG, NWOG, yellow box, purple dashes)

=== [2024 #40 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Tape Reading NQ \ October 02, 2024 (ID YxpHUwZhu80)] — RTH opening gap midpoint (CE) in the first 30 minutes  (aliases: "opening range gap", "mid gap", "opening gap is the initial bias")
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - The opening gap is the initial bias. In the first 30 minutes after the open, "70% of the time" price trades to the midpoint (consequent encroachment) of the gap. If you gap down, the primary focus is up to the midpoint of the gap. He repeats the 70% number and calls it a 70% strike rate.
  - Size filter: an opening range gap of at least 40 handles (NQ) is his minimum interest for the first 30 minutes. A larger gap (e.g. 75 handles) makes him more interested in the day, and he would use most of his available leverage.
  - After the midpoint is hit, it often acts as the turning point to the primary sell-side.
- **How to identify:** Compare the 4:00 PM close (ET) and 9:30 AM open and mark the gap and its 50%. (Defined as the RTH opening gap in other lectures.)
- **How to use:** Gap down means expect a first-30-minute run up to the gap midpoint. Gap up means the reverse (inferred).
- **Example in this video:** NQ Wed 10-02: gap-down open. The midpoint sat at 19965. Price traded up to it in the first 30 minutes, then reversed and dropped to the primary sell-side below.
- **Timestamps:** [1:23:46](https://youtu.be/YxpHUwZhu80?t=5026) mid gap at 19965, 70% · [1:24:33](https://youtu.be/YxpHUwZhu80?t=5073) opening gap as initial bias · [1:25:23](https://youtu.be/YxpHUwZhu80?t=5123) 40-handle minimum · [1:32:16](https://youtu.be/YxpHUwZhu80?t=5536) recap, 70% strike rate
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** The "gap up" mirror is inferred. He only demonstrates a gap down.

=== [2024 #41 — October 03, 2024 NQ Market Review (ID Xyiw_IFE9yc)] — RTH opening range gap and its midpoint  (aliases: "opening range gap", "mid gap", "settlement")
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - On a chart set to RTH, price delivery is 9:30 ET until 4:14 PM ET. The previous day's RTH settlement (at 4:14 ET, as he states it) to the 9:30 open is the opening range gap. The rectangle drawn between those two prices, together with the time, is the opening range gap and the opening range.
  - The midpoint of the gap is the key level. 70% of the time the gap gets filled to halfway. He pushes back on a Twitter study claiming less, saying he has data back about 40 years and has lived it.
  - Practical use: an opening gap down means the first-30-minutes bias is bullish (up to the midpoint). He calls this a nice scalping opportunity.
  - Next, price often reaches to the previous day's settlement as well.
- **How to identify:** Mark the previous RTH settlement price and the 9:30 open. Measure the 50% (CE).
- **How to use:** Gap down at the open means expect the first-30-minute run up to the gap midpoint. After that, look for a reversal if the higher-timeframe bias is bearish.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Thu 10-03, gap-down open. Price traded right back up to the mid-gap, pulled into a 1m FVG, found support at the mid-gap, rallied to previous settlement, formed another FVG, then cleared the buy-side and spent time there (he reads that as buy-stop accumulation by smart money so they can sell short). The market then broke lower.
- **Timestamps:** [0:04:23](https://youtu.be/Xyiw_IFE9yc?t=263) RTH lens and gap definition · [0:05:11](https://youtu.be/Xyiw_IFE9yc?t=311) 70% claim and defense · [0:06:00](https://youtu.be/Xyiw_IFE9yc?t=360) buy-stop accumulation then short
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Same 70% claim as M24-040. He is defensive about it here.

=== [2024 #42 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ October NFP NQ \ October 04, 2024 (ID qcYYm1iNCIM)] — Opening range gap: ETH vs RTH, midpoint, and gap-closure scenarios  (aliases: "opening range gap", "mid gap", "gap closure half", "settlement")
- **Kind:** rule / definition
- **What ICT says:**
  - Chart sessions: electronic trading hours (ETH) run 6:00 PM to 5:00 PM New York time. Regular trading hours (RTH) run 9:30 AM to 4:14 PM. The opening range gap is always defined by RTH: previous RTH settlement (4:14 PM) to the 9:30 open. Toggle between the two when planning.
  - Mid-gap (50%, consequent encroachment) is reached about 70% of the time within the first 30 minutes (9:30-10:00). He restates his 40 years of data (32 years watching the markets, bought historical data before he started trading) against a Twitter study that said otherwise.
  - Standard day: the gap is the initial bias. Scalp to the mid gap if you want. At mid gap, decide whether price continues in the gap's direction or fully closes the gap. If it closes a premium gap, use the bottom of the gap as resistance and expect a move down in multiples of the gap size, two to three standard deviations.
  - With a small gap, wait for the first presented FVG.
  - NFP scenario (this day): ETH price was sitting at the top of an enormous potential gap with relative equal highs just above. Possible script: open at 9:30, run up to those highs to accumulate buy stops (street money chases), then smart money sells into the flood of willing buyers and drives down to the mid gap, three-quarters, or full closure. The primary buy side may not appear on an RTH chart, so check ETH.
  - Conditional read: if the open pushes down to the mid gap early and then climbs all the way back up, that is not a turtle soup short setup. It suggests price is going much higher (consolidate, accumulate, higher). He would prefer a small drop first to make traders think lower and then a rally to the obvious buy side.
  - He says the mid-gap move may take longer than 30 minutes on a news day, and he "is not holding it to" the 70% on NFP.
- **How to identify:** Toggle ETH/RTH. Measure the RTH settlement to open, then the 50%. Note buy-side liquidity on ETH that is not visible on RTH.
- **How to use:** First-30-minute bias toward mid gap. After that, decide on full closure vs continuation using higher-timeframe bias and liquidity.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 10-04: large gap-up in the making. Price rallied from 8:30, opened, ran, then dropped to the mid gap before 10:00. ICT said "not a tick more" at the half of an FVG, then aggressive delivery to mid gap, the 70% strike rate by 10:00.
- **Timestamps:** [1:06:01](https://youtu.be/qcYYm1iNCIM?t=3961) ETH vs RTH view · [1:07:46](https://youtu.be/qcYYm1iNCIM?t=4066) mid gap script · [1:10:18](https://youtu.be/qcYYm1iNCIM?t=4218) session hours · [1:11:06](https://youtu.be/qcYYm1iNCIM?t=4266) smart money sells into buy stops · [1:13:32](https://youtu.be/qcYYm1iNCIM?t=4412) premium gap, 2-3 SD targets · [1:24:50](https://youtu.be/qcYYm1iNCIM?t=5090) 40 years of data · [1:28:01](https://youtu.be/qcYYm1iNCIM?t=5281) reading a mid-gap tag then rally
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Adds the ETH/RTH toggle and the 2-3 standard deviation (gap-size multiple) projection beyond M24-040/041. The "7%" jab in the transcript is a sarcastic aside, not a statistic.

=== [2024 #43 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ NQ High Resistance Liquidity Conditions Tape Reading \ October 07, 2024 (ID DzJpkxI057g)] — Opening Range Gap (RTH opening gap) — high, midpoint (CE), quadrants, as initial draw  (aliases: "opening range gap", "mid gap", "new week opening gap" mentioned separately)
- **Kind:** rule | refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - "Always starting the opening range with that initial bias": the first 30 minutes of the session he frames price toward the opening range gap midpoint, which he calls the "mid gap" (the consequent encroachment of the gap). It is "always the initial draw for the first 30 minutes."
  - The gap here was a discount-side gap: price had been trading below Friday's settlement ("we kept lower than Friday's settlement price"), so the mid gap (CE of the discount opening gap) is the upward objective. Full gap closure is above the mid gap; he also mentions upper and lower quadrants of the gap and the previous Friday settlement as further levels.
  - Captions give the opening range gap high at "2222.5", the mid gap near "2,179.5"/"21795" (garbled; consistent magnitude would be NQ ~20,xxx but the caption digits are unreliable — caption unclear). Do not use these numbers literally.
  - He keeps his bias to one side of the market: because he does not want to see it as a shorting opportunity, "if it goes lower it's going to go without me."
  - He notes price "traded back to the lower quadrant of the opening range gap" and later says any movement above the mid gap takes price to the new week opening gap low and its CE.
- **How to identify:** Mark the gap between the previous RTH close (settlement) and the 9:30 open; split into quadrants with the midpoint (CE) as the central target; note which side of the gap price is on relative to Friday's settlement.
- **How to use:** Use the mid gap as the initial draw on liquidity for the first 30 minutes; only take longs (in this bias) after a specific model sequence (see next concept); do not sell just because price drops below the gap or trades lower, unless other information provides a bearish read.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 7 Oct 2024. Bias: reach for the mid gap of the opening range gap. Price instead trades below, tags the minor sell side, bounces, spends the first 30 minutes (9:30–10:00) not reaching it, then later in the session rallies to the mid gap after tagging a volume imbalance low.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:01:39](https://youtu.be/DzJpkxI057g?t=99) — opening range gap high noted; "initial bias" starts here.
  - [0:09:53](https://youtu.be/DzJpkxI057g?t=593) — full gap closure vs. mid gap; "always the initial draw for the first 30 minutes."
  - [0:54:19](https://youtu.be/DzJpkxI057g?t=3259) — mid gap = CE; beyond it the next references are the new week opening gap low and its CE.
  - [1:01:44](https://youtu.be/DzJpkxI057g?t=3704) — keeps focus on the mid gap/CE of the discount opening gap; filters shorts.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He says it does not need to be reached within the first 30 minutes for the bias to be valid; "by 10:00 ... I'm filtering": a failure to hit the target early does not flip the bias.

=== [2024 #44 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ NQ Tape Reading \ October 15, 2024 (ID HMn46vLjKvY)] — Opening Gap Size Filter (NY opening gap / RTH opening range gap size)  (aliases: "anemic gap", "opening range gap")
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** In the 2024 mentorship he taught that a small RTH opening gap implies a lackluster, low-impact morning session, no easy run from entry to target. Today's gap (previous close 4:14 p.m. settlement vs. open) was "anemic" (numbers around 20631.75 vs ~20662.75 stated, caption unclear which is which).
  - Minimum threshold he likes: about 45–50 handles (caption "4550"); preferred: 75 handles or more.
  - With a 75+ handle gap: "70% likelihood it goes back to half the gap" (the CE of the gap); he'd go in right at the opening bell, not even waiting for the first FVG.
  - With a tiny gap: no excitement/displacement at the open, so the morning delivery is 50/50. Sit still and wait for 10:00 (10:00–11:00 Silver Bullet) for an FVG that gives liquidity in line with bias; if it isn't clear, do nothing. Defer trading to lunch macro, PM session or last hour (e.g. come back at 1:30, watch the first 30 minutes into 2:00, trade PM; or sit down ~2:45 and trade the last hour).
- **Example in this video:** Oct 15 NQ: gap from the 4:14 p.m. close to the 9:30 open was too small to trade the opening; he waited for 10:00.

  - [0:40:15](https://youtu.be/HMn46vLjKvY?t=2415) — 45–50 handle minimum, 75 preferred, 70% to half-gap
  - [0:41:53](https://youtu.be/HMn46vLjKvY?t=2513) — wait until 10:00 Silver Bullet, otherwise do nothing
  - [0:42:41](https://youtu.be/HMn46vLjKvY?t=2561) — 50/50 morning, shift to lunch/PM/last hour

=== [2024 #46 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ News Release Tape Reading \ October 17, 2024 (ID z2t62hgjLXo)] — Opening Range Gap 50% (mid gap) probability, and Judas swing scenario planning for 9:30  (aliases: "RTH opening gap", "gap closure", "premium gap")
- **Kind:** rule | refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - Reference points: yesterday's 4:14 PM ET RTH settlement (he says "4:14 p.m. eastern time ... in regular trading hours" — the last RTH reference) versus the 9:30 opening price; set charts to RTH so the opening range gap shows. Price pre-open was at roughly 20,545 (caption "4 545"), about two handles from settlement.
  - Drop a Fib on the gap: the 50% level is a "70% likelihood in the first 30 minutes" that price trades there. (This is the one quantified claim.)
  - If price gaps higher at the open (a premium gap), the likelihood of coming down to 50% of the gap is high; that would run below the lows of the 8:30 range and into the inefficiency below, and retail would be reluctant to be long on that; then he could switch gears and work toward the higher-time-frame buy-side draw.
  - What he wants before the open: price staying close to the 8:30 range, "hugging around it," not running up; then a small initial Judas swing higher at 9:30 taking a short-term high left before the bell, then a drop to a discount to trade to half the gap range.
  - Counter-scenario: if price gets above 600 (caption; likely 20,600) before the open, he'd be less likely to expect a full or half gap closure before higher; instead the Judas swing would be the run between ~600 and ~660 at the open, sweeping that, and then a sell-off to a gap-closure event: mid gap or 3/4 of gap closure.
  - He does not know the opening price until 9:30, so he gives the scenarios; the opening price "tells the story" for which scenario applies, and sets his draw on liquidity and setup.
  - Also: Judas swing "9:30 into 9:45" is the window he expects.
  - He stresses to students that he is not saying "up is right and down is right"; his stated preference is that price does not run up yet; if it does, he is wrong, and he would not chase.
- **How to use:** Compute the RTH settlement-to-9:30-open gap; 50% line is the expected first-30-minute target; adjust by where price is at the open and whether it has already run to the next liquidity.
- **Timestamps:** [0:44:14](https://youtu.be/z2t62hgjLXo?t=2654) — premium gap in the making; [0:46:36](https://youtu.be/z2t62hgjLXo?t=2796) — 50% = 70% likelihood first 30 minutes; [0:47:23](https://youtu.be/z2t62hgjLXo?t=2843) — if >600 pre-open, less likely to fill; [0:49:00](https://youtu.be/z2t62hgjLXo?t=2940) — Judas swing 600–660 then sell-off to mid-gap / 3/4 closure.
- **Nuance:** Garbled price digits; treat levels as approximate. The "70%" figure is stated once; no source or test cited.

=== [2024 #47 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Premarket Tape Reading NQ Futures \ October 21, 2024 (ID CQk-5lAirn0)] — Opening Range Gap (previous RTH settlement to 9:30 open): how to mark it
- **Kind:** definition | how-to
- **What ICT says:**
  - Switch the chart from electronic trading hours (ETH) to regular trading hours (RTH) to find the previous day's settlement. On the 1-minute chart, the last RTH candle is the 4:14 ET candle (it settles at 4:14; there is no 4:15 candle because RTH is closed). On a 5-minute chart the 4:10 candle is displayed instead, which is why he tells you to drop to the 1-minute chart to read the exact close/open when marking any gap (new week or new day).
  - Previous day for Monday is always the previous Friday (unless Friday was a holiday; then use the last settlement).
  - Label convention: always label by the day you are trading, not the date of the data: on Monday it is "Monday's opening range gap high/low", not "Friday's." Until the 9:30 first print you do not know which side is high/low: if the market opens BELOW the prior settlement, the settlement is the opening range gap HIGH and the 9:30 open is the gap LOW (discount gap). If price travels back above it before 9:30 (here he notes it would have to traverse over 100 handles), relabel: the settlement becomes the low and the 9:30 first print the high.
  - Trading sessions context: ETH resumes Sunday 6:00 p.m. ET; Friday ETH stops at 5:00 p.m. ET.
- **How to identify:** Previous day RTH close (1m, 4:14 ET) to today's 9:30 first print.
- **How to use:** Mark the settlement now; pre-open label it provisionally; once the 9:30 open prints, set the FIB across the gap and take the 50% level (see below).
- **Example in this video:** NQ, Monday 21 Oct 2024: Friday's RTH settlement line (caption value ~20,494.25) annotated in black as "Monday's opening range gap high"; pre-market price ~20,39x (caption: "20,39 even") below it, so it is a prospective discount gap.
- **Timestamps:** [0:05:08](https://youtu.be/CQk-5lAirn0?t=308) — ETH vs RTH toggle; [0:05:55](https://youtu.be/CQk-5lAirn0?t=355) — use the 1-minute chart, 4:14 last candle; [0:10:57](https://youtu.be/CQk-5lAirn0?t=657) — labeling convention and relabel scenario.
- **Nuance / caveats:** Price digits in the captions are inconsistent; do not rely on them. Reinforces the Oct 7 and Oct 17 videos ("mid gap" = 50% of this gap).

=== [2024 #47 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Premarket Tape Reading NQ Futures \ October 21, 2024 (ID CQk-5lAirn0)] — New Week Opening Gap (NWOG) — drawing it  (aliases: "new week opening gap", "Monday gap")
- **Kind:** definition | how-to
- **What ICT says:** NWOG is the gap between Friday's ETH settlement (the last electronic print Friday, 5:00 p.m. ET) and the first print Sunday at 6:00 p.m. ET. Since the market settled at one price and started at another, the lower value is the NWOG low and the higher is the NWOG high. Draw it by copying a horizontal line (ctrl-drag in TradingView), put it on the opening price, and flip low/high. This week it was very small: caption reads "484¼ to 496¼" with CE 490¼ (digits caption-derived; likely 20,484.25–20,496.25). Add a line segment at the consequent encroachment and use a contrasting color. The NWOG here encapsulated the Friday RTH settlement price.
- **How to use:** It is a reference/PD array; the CE of the gap is used. Also "Monday's opening range gap" (RTH) is a separate and generally more relevant reference for the opening session.
- **Timestamps:** [0:07:31](https://youtu.be/CQk-5lAirn0?t=451) — Sunday 6 p.m. open; [0:08:32](https://youtu.be/CQk-5lAirn0?t=512) — drawing NWOG; [0:09:20](https://youtu.be/CQk-5lAirn0?t=560) — size and CE.

=== [2024 #47 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Premarket Tape Reading NQ Futures \ October 21, 2024 (ID CQk-5lAirn0)] — Opening Range Gap 50% rule — discount gap = bullish first 30 minutes  (aliases: "mid gap", "half gap", "gap closure", "70% likelihood")
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - If the market opens at 9:30 BELOW the previous RTH settlement (a discount gap), the bias for 9:30–10:00 ET is bullish, expecting at least 50% of the gap's range to be traded to. He states there is a "70% likelihood of half the gap being traded to during the first 30 minutes of trading, 9:30 to 10:00 Eastern."
  - The 50% level does not make the bias bearish afterward: "it just means that 70% of the time that's what's usually unfolding." If it does not reach 50% within 30 minutes, he still looks for it later; this is an immediate bias to study/tape-read, not a guarantee.
  - Implementation: take the first 9:30 print as the gap low, drop the Fib to the previous settlement (gap high), read the 50% level. (He says this is for a gap lower; the converse for a premium gap is implied but not restated here.)
  - Beyond 50%: price could go to a full gap closure or 3/4 gap closure; the 50% level is the minimum expectation.
  - "I'm never ever ever going in there on the basis of simply 'I'm going to go short because we open lower'" — worst thing to do, retail-minded.
  - Hypothetical he builds: assume the next tick opens at ~20,408 (caption "408", also rendered "20,480" — caption unclear; likely 20,40x given pre-market ~20,39x), annotated as Monday's opening range gap low; the Fib to the gap high (caption "494 and A4" ≈ 20,494.25) gives a 50% level near the top of the London relative equal highs, so the mid gap AND the London session high buy side agree as the draw.
- **How to identify:** (a) 9:30 open below prior RTH settlement; (b) measure the gap; (c) 50% level; (d) check what session highs lie in the path.
- **How to use:** Be long-biased until the 50% is traded; look for a partial near the first session high/relative equal highs, but aim for the draw above if the relative equal highs sit close to the 50% level, because the other half of the gap remains (3/4 or full closure). Use "any fair value gap, the first presented fair value gap" for the entry; if it wins, you're done; if it loses, don't do it again, sit still.
- **Example in this video (hypothetical, not a live trade):** NQ 21 Oct 2024: if price opens ~20,40x, 9:30–10:00 bias is long; first target the 50% level; pre-open he suggests a "modest little tiny Judas swing lower" taking out a short-term 1-minute low after 9:30, then a rally to attack the London session highs/mid gap. If instead price sells off first and takes out the London low, he'd look for a change in direction and then target the London session high.
- **Timestamps:** [0:26:21](https://youtu.be/CQk-5lAirn0?t=1581) — hypothetical open and gap labeling; [0:27:09](https://youtu.be/CQk-5lAirn0?t=1629) — 70% likelihood, 9:30–10:00; [0:29:32](https://youtu.be/CQk-5lAirn0?t=1772) — bullish until 50% traded; [0:31:09](https://youtu.be/CQk-5lAirn0?t=1869) — confluence of mid gap and London highs; [0:36:46](https://youtu.be/CQk-5lAirn0?t=2206) — not bearish afterwards, just usual 70% behavior; [0:39:08](https://youtu.be/CQk-5lAirn0?t=2348) — scenario if sell-off first.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Same 70%/50% statement as the Oct 17 video (M24-046). Here he adds the explicit "never short just because we opened lower" prohibition and the idea that this alone "could be your entire model."

=== [2024 #48 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ How ICT Picks Winning FVG's & Orderblocks \ October 25, 2024 (ID svYZKOrWPRo)] — New Week Opening Gap (NWOG) and consequent encroachment  (aliases: "NWOG", "new week opening gap CE")
- **Kind:** definition / example
- **What ICT says:** NWOG is the difference between Friday's 4:59 PM ET close and Sunday's 6:00 PM ET open. The halfway point (dotted line) is its consequent encroachment. Keep only the current NWOG on the chart; you do not need every NWOG or NDOG ("kitchen sink"). He says he codified the use of the gap and did not invent Sunday's gap. A draw on liquidity can be a session high or low (previous PM session, London, Asian), a single high, or an opening gap level.
- **Example in this video:** price failed to reach the CE of the NWOG initially, dropped outside the gap, rallied to the NWOG high, and then dropped to a random level before a bullish rally. A short-side Model 2022 (shift in market structure, FVG, retrace up into premium/OTE) was delivered back down to the CE (three times) and then at 9:30 "all hell breaks loose" upward through the buy stops (this pre-9:30 sequence is described quickly and the captions are unclear in spots).
- **Timestamps:** [1:29:29](https://youtu.be/svYZKOrWPRo?t=5369) keep only the current NWOG; [1:31:09](https://youtu.be/svYZKOrWPRo?t=5469) NWOG definition and CE; [1:32:44](https://youtu.be/svYZKOrWPRo?t=5564) the Model 2022 trap delivering to CE.
- **Nuance / caveats:** he notes the model 2022 (bearish) looked classic but was a trap against the bullish run. It supports "bias first".

=== [2024 #49 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ High Resistance & Low Resistance Conditions \ October 28, 2024 (ID 3YfetuXVhOE)] — Clustered New Week / New Day Opening Gaps as draw on liquidity  (aliases: NWOG/NDOG clusters, "black hole", "Event Horizon on steroids")
- **Kind:** rule / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - NWOG = Friday settlement to Sunday 6 PM open. Compare the current NWOG with the last five NWOGs (or last five NDOGs). If any two overlap (even one level of one gap encroaching the range of another is enough), that is a "cluster".
  - Open (unfilled) overlapping gaps act as a magnet: no buying/selling occurred there, "natural value areas". More overlapping gaps mean a higher probability price is repriced there. Whether a gap has been traded through/filled in the past "matters not".
  - The cluster is a draw (right direction), not a requirement to reach it ("just a magnet draw on price"; the stadium-directions analogy: get close to the stadium and then find the signs).
  - A trader shorting from above takes the highest level of the cluster first (NWOG high); for longs, the lowest level.
  - Works as a session bias for any school of thought, and no moving averages, VWAP or HTF top-down needed.
- **How to identify:** current NWOG high / CE / low plus the prior week's NWOG sitting inside the current one (here in its lower half); price is above both.
- **How to use:** use as bias/draw; first (nearest) level is the target for a beginner. Combine with the opening-range-gap fill (below) for a 3/4 or full gap closure or more.
- **Example in this video:** Oct 28 2024 NQ: current NWOG is large (Friday settle to Sunday 6 PM open); last week's NWOG (purple) sits in its lower half. Price hit neither the current NWOG CE nor the low that day; the NWOG high was first touched at 11:43. His X post that morning called the draw down into this area.
- **Timestamps:** [0:22:13](https://youtu.be/3YfetuXVhOE?t=1333) no news so move comes clean pre-market · [0:23:49](https://youtu.be/3YfetuXVhOE?t=1429) layout of the two NWOGs · [0:39:03](https://youtu.be/3YfetuXVhOE?t=2343) how to choose which gap / any two overlapping · [0:39:50](https://youtu.be/3YfetuXVhOE?t=2390) black-hole effect · [1:29:34](https://youtu.be/3YfetuXVhOE?t=5374) clustering defined

=== [2024 #49 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ High Resistance & Low Resistance Conditions \ October 28, 2024 (ID 3YfetuXVhOE)] — Opening Range Gap consequent encroachment fill (70% in first 30 minutes)  (aliases: RTH opening gap half-gap, half-gap fill)
- **Kind:** rule / statistic claim
- **What ICT says:**
  - Opening range gap = previous day settlement to 9:30 ET first print. Its CE (half) is traded to about 70% of the time in the first 30 minutes ("seven times out of ten").
  - A minute or two before 9:30 you can estimate where the mid-gap will be.
  - If gap-CE fill is also in the direction of a cluster of NWOG/NDOG, expect 3/4 gap, full closure, or more (the NWOG is beyond). You do not just stop at CE: "there is a draw still lower".
  - If it misses in the first 30 minutes but fills later (e.g. 11:00) that is still fine, though less valuable on a high-resistance day.
- **Example in this video:** Oct 28 NQ opened with a big gap up from Friday settlement; price opened inside an inversion FVG, traded down aggressively, and filled the half gap in 19 minutes (within the first 30), then did not reach the NWOG high until 11:43.
- **Timestamps:** [1:14:10](https://youtu.be/3YfetuXVhOE?t=4450) half-gap + cluster stack · [1:18:26](https://youtu.be/3YfetuXVhOE?t=4706) how to measure · [1:33:35](https://youtu.be/3YfetuXVhOE?t=5615) filled in 19 minutes · [1:46:29](https://youtu.be/3YfetuXVhOE?t=6389) "even in high resistance it trades to mid-gap"

######## 2026 LECTURES ########

=== [2026 #2 — NonFarm Payroll October 2026 Lecture (ID 59Elm8myGEc)] — Unrealized dealing range / "Event horizon" (50% of a projected range)  (aliases ICT uses: event horizon, mid-range reading, finite specific arrays, "phantom zone")
- **Kind:** new coinage / entry model component
- **What ICT says:**
  - "Event horizon" is his name for the midpoint (50%) of a dealing range that has not been fully realized yet: one end is a real, already-formed high (here the short-term high made after the NFP release), the other end is an old daily high that price has not reached (the June 22, 2026 daily high of the December NQ contract, 31,285).
  - It does not matter whether price ever gets to the old high. It is a forecast: "we're predicting, not reacting to price."
  - Both ends are "finite, specific arrays" that cannot be hidden; the middle of the range is the level to hunt for shorts because a price run into premium of that range is expected to be sold.
  - On its own, the event-horizon level means nothing. "Isolated levels are useless"; it needs a convergence of several smart-money concepts to be viable.
  - The market-efficiency logic: the new leg higher will leave buyers' sell-stops (sell-side liquidity) resting below the post-news low. Shorting from the event horizon gives willing sellers a lower target to pay out into.
- **How to identify:**
  1. Mark the 8:30 news release. Wait roughly 15-20 minutes into the session (do not guess ahead of it).
  2. Wait for a short-term high to form after the first rip and a retracement.
  3. Wait for price to take that short-term high out (a shift in market structure on the lower timeframe); only then can the unrealized range from that new high to the old daily high be measured. (He anticipates it before price gets there.)
  4. Draw a fib with the 50% (equilibrium) of that range; the 0.5 is the event horizon.
  5. Make sure the fib sits "on air" (anchored exactly on highs/lows, not on candle bodies).
- **How to use:**
  - Look for shorts at/near the event horizon when the daily draw is higher (he was bullish for the day but was fading a news-driven intraday run). He is "contrarian" intraday: he scalps against the grain of the daily range.
  - Stop loss goes above the entry array (above the gap's upper boundary / CE zone), not far above the level.
  - Set an extreme final take-profit (see Targets below) and bank partials along the way.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Dec contract, 1m, Friday of NFP week (October 2026 NFP; exact date not stated). At 8:30 NFP prints and price rips higher (price had been drawing up toward the old daily high; 9:30 opening came later). A short-term high forms at 8:38 (about 31,146.75, caption-garbled "838" and "31146.75 ... the high of that candlestick"). Price retraces, then rallies again through that high. Projecting the 8:38 high to the old daily high 31,285 gives a 50% level (his "event horizon") of about 31,216 (arithmetic from his numbers; he does not say the figure). Price trades up to the event horizon, where he sells.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:07:30](https://youtu.be/59Elm8myGEc?t=450) — explains the unrealized dealing range from the first high to the June 22 daily high
  - [0:10:42](https://youtu.be/59Elm8myGEc?t=642) — "if I know this level and this level, I want the middle of that range: event horizon"
  - [0:36:23](https://youtu.be/59Elm8myGEc?t=2183) — recap: two ways to use the high; mid-range reading is the event horizon
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He stresses this is "a volatility-based, news-driven, once-in-a-while scenario", not an every-day Silver Bullet-style setup, and "it's not going to appear every single time." Intended for advanced students who already have models; he says newer students should not try to copy it. Note: it relies on a prior directional draw (higher NQ prices).

=== [2026 #3 — NQ AM Session Review 09/30/2026 (ID kRShZKZlvJ4)] — RTH Opening Range Gap (Monday's / Tuesday's) as a draw on liquidity  (aliases ICT uses: regular trading hours opening range gap, "first minute fair value gap"-style gap between prior RTH close and 9:30 open)
- **Kind:** example only / rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Earlier in the week (Monday/Tuesday) he stated price would likely rise at least into the CE of Monday's RTH opening range gap; today (Wednesday) it did that and went on to close the whole gap.
  - Tuesday also has its own RTH opening range gap, shown on the 1-minute chart as a gray shaded zone from the gap to the opening price; price was "extended beyond" it.
  - Each session is read in relation to the previous session; ICT says this is what he has been suggesting "in terms of each session and how it relates to the previous session."
  - The chart is switched to regular trading hours to see these gaps clearly.
- **How to identify:** Set chart to RTH; gap between the prior day's RTH close and the 9:30 open; use its CE (50%) as the first target and the far edge as the full-fill target.
- **How to use:** As the target/draw for a long: aim for the CE, expect large-range candles once price clears the lower portion of the gap's remaining unfilled half.
- **Example in this video:** NQ daily: small consolidation between lows and a high. 1m: Monday's RTH ORG is the large orange-shaded zone above price. At the 9:30 open price took out a low, rallied, hit Monday's gap CE, pulled back into a "weird consolidation," then rallied to close the entire gap.

  - [0:00:00](https://youtu.be/kRShZKZlvJ4?t=0) — daily view; prior call that price would go to Monday's gap CE
  - [0:02:28](https://youtu.be/kRShZKZlvJ4?t=148) — switches to RTH to show Monday's and Tuesday's gaps
  - [0:05:52](https://youtu.be/kRShZKZlvJ4?t=352) — 9:30 open takes the low, rallies through the CE of Monday's gap
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Caption says "early trading hours"; context makes clear it means regular trading hours (RTH). He says the gap was filled completely, "I didn't require it do the entire thing" for his own trade.

=== [2026 #4 — NQ December Asian Session Execution 09/29/2026 (ID 7M56e9O06Ko)] — Event Horizon (Measurement Method)
- **Kind:** refinement
- **What ICT says:** When two pools of liquidity exist, measure the midpoint between them (event horizon); price can be taken off there as a partial, staggering exits.
- **How to identify:** Identify two clear liquidity pools; measure the distance between them.
- **How to use:** Exit partials at the event horizon to fund the position without requiring the final target.
- **Example in this video:** Event horizon measured between upper and lower liquidity pools; partials taken near midpoint (~725 area).
- **Timestamps:** [0:15:16](https://youtu.be/7M56e9O06Ko?t=916) explains event horizon concept, [0:26:27](https://youtu.be/7M56e9O06Ko?t=1587) execution "if we can get five contracts off there."
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Allows staggered profit-taking without single dependency on terminus target.

=== [2026 #4 — NQ December Asian Session Execution 09/29/2026 (ID 7M56e9O06Ko)] — Opening Range Gap (Monday's RTH Open)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** The high and low of the opening range from Monday's regular trading hours (RTH); used as reference level for support and target zones.
- **How to identify:** Identify the first candle(s) of Monday's regular trading hours (9:30 ET) and mark the high/low.
- **How to use:** Price running into these levels often encounters liquidity; use as target zones and support levels.
- **Example in this video:** Monday's RTH opening range gap low (~18530 area) used as target on the way up; also as support on pullbacks.
