- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Must be identified correctly (Monday RTH, not Asian session or previous day).

=== [2026 #5 — NQ December Commentary 09/29/2026 (ID I7fIf1eEz0w)] — Opening Range Gap (Magnetic Attractor)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** The high and low of the opening range (here, 9:30–11:00 AM for RTH); acts as a magnet attracting price repeatedly. Even if price fails to reach it on the first attempt, it will gravitate back to it.
- **How to identify:** Mark the opening range: for RTH open, use 9:30 ET and the first 1–2 hours of trading.
- **How to use:** Expect price to revisit opening range levels multiple times during the day; use as support, resistance, and target zones.
- **Example in this video:** Tuesday's RTH opening range gap low at ~18430; price drifted lower during NY lunch, then gravitated back up to the opening price (9:30 ET opening, anchored 30,708.75 on ES basis).
- **Timestamps:** [0:03:28](https://youtu.be/I7fIf1eEz0w?t=208) "today's regular trading hours opening range gap," [0:06:42](https://youtu.be/I7fIf1eEz0w?t=402) "So, while we didn't get to it here, look what it did. It gravitated right back up into it there."
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Acts as a magnet even if initially missed; will be revisited intra-day.

=== [2026 #6 — NQ AM Session Low Of Day Long Review (ID UVVmS0de0g0)] — Regular Trading Hours (RTH) Opening Range Gap
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** The gap between Friday's 4:14 PM ET close (RTH settlement) and Monday's 9:30 AM ET open (RTH resume). This gap is the primary structural reference for intraday trading. Must be graded (divided into 8ths and 4ths with Fibonacci) to identify key levels (octant, quadrant, half, three-quarter closure points).
- **How to identify:** On daily chart with RTH only (not electronic hours): mark the close at 4:14 PM previous Friday; mark the open at 9:30 AM current trading day. The void between is the RTH opening range gap.
- **How to use:** Use Fibonacci to divide the gap into 8 equal points (octants). Lowest octant and lowest quadrant are failure points (if price can't even touch these, it's weak). Use standard deviations of the gap width for downside targets; -1 and -2 SD levels below the gap define key support zones.
- **Example in this video:** NQ opened lower on 09/28 (discount gap). Lowest quadrant and octant levels were identified around 18430 area. Two SD of the gap drop to ~18370 area, aligning with Thursday's daily low at 18370.
- **Timestamps:** [0:06:36](https://youtu.be/UVVmS0de0g0?t=396) "discount regular trading hours opening range gap," [0:07:26](https://youtu.be/UVVmS0de0g0?t=446) "grade that by simply having the fib on it," [0:26:08](https://youtu.be/UVVmS0de0g0?t=1568) standard deviations explained.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Do not confuse with "opening range" (first 30 minutes of session, 9:30–10:00). RTH opening range gap is overnight gap only, not intraday.

=== [2026 #10 — How New Students Should Start Part 5 \ Back Testing Properly (ID xnbtp_j81lI)] — RTH Opening Range Gap: discount/premium definition, mid gap, and -1.0 fib projections
- **Kind:** definition | rule | refinement (new: standard-deviation projections)
- **What ICT says:**
  - Use the regular trading hours (RTH) chart, not electronic. Reference points: the previous day's RTH closing price (final print 4:14 p.m. ET on the 1-minute chart) and the 9:30 a.m. opening price. Between them is the opening range gap. Opening lower than the settlement = DISCOUNT opening range gap; opening higher = PREMIUM opening range gap. Where it opens at 9:30 determines the type.
  - Watch how the consequent encroachment (mid gap) is traded to after the open.
  - New: Fib standard-deviation projections off the gap. Draw the fib from the open to the settlement and add -1.0: the level hits the high of the AM session; draw from the settlement to the open and add -1.0: it hit the AM session low "to the tick" (also a further "duplicated down" projection of the 69-handle range delivered the lunch-macro lower low — caption unclear).
  - RTH relative equal highs and lows (smooth areas formed in the last 15–20 minutes of the previous day's trading, "previous market on close macro") must be observed in RTH view because the gap will refer back to them; it doesn't matter if electronic-hours price (London/Asia) already traded through them.
  - Record the range from low to target and what it offered; assume you took a trade idea there.
- **How to identify:** Chart set to RTH; previous day last 1m candle 4:14 p.m. close vs 9:30 open.
- **How to use:** Expect the CE (mid gap) to be traded to; project -1.0 fibs from the gap for AM-session extremes; use RTH-smooth highs/lows as references; log the gap size in handles and the time the CE was delivered.
- **Example in this video:** NQ: discount opening range gap of 69 handles. At 9:30 first candle wicked but did not take out the low; a second attempt, then price rallied to the RTH CE (mid gap) just 5 minutes after 9:30. The AM session high was nailed by the -1.0 projection; the AM session low (caption: 29,386.25, at about 11:12 a.m. ET) was nailed initially by the -1.0 projection from settlement to open, then swept by a later lower low; price subsequently worked inside the gap with a lunch-macro draw toward 10:00-hour lows and delivered to a "perfect standard deviation" (69 handles) lower — "to a tick."
- **Timestamps:** [0:24:47](https://youtu.be/xnbtp_j81lI?t=1487) — RTH, 4:14 p.m. settlement; [0:25:33](https://youtu.be/xnbtp_j81lI?t=1533) — discount vs premium gap; [0:26:22](https://youtu.be/xnbtp_j81lI?t=1582) — 69-handle gap, CE hit within 5 minutes; [0:27:11](https://youtu.be/xnbtp_j81lI?t=1631) — -1.0 projection hits AM high; [0:27:57](https://youtu.be/xnbtp_j81lI?t=1677) — -1.0 hits AM low; [0:28:45](https://youtu.be/xnbtp_j81lI?t=1725) — RTH smooth highs/lows.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** This is the first stated use of -1.0 (standard deviation) projections for the opening range gap in these notes; whether he applies the same projection up and down on each day is not fully clear from captions. He says doing this trains "turtle soup" (pattern recognition of stop runs).

=== [2026 #13 — NFP Review & New Student Encouragement (ID -Qag-99u_BM)] — Regular trading hours (RTH) opening range gap  (aliases: "opening range gap", "RTH ORG")
- **Kind:** definition + example
- **What ICT says:** The gap between the RTH settlement (4:14 p.m. ET the previous day) and the 9:30 a.m. ET open the next day. This is not the electronic overnight data, which he distinguishes from regular trading hours. The 9:30 open price is the high of the gap (here a gap-down), the prior close is the other edge, and the midpoint is the consequent encroachment (CE).
- **How to identify:** Mark Thursday's 4:14 p.m. ET RTH settlement, mark Friday's 9:30 opening price, and shade between them (he used bright yellow).
- **How to use:** If price trades back inside the gap, use it as a premium array on the way up, expecting premium-sensitive selling. The expected path is a run up into the 9:30 opening price (the high of the gap), then a move lower that takes out the CE. The gap's high edge acts as resistance.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Fri Aug 28. Thursday RTH settlement = 29,521.25 (4:14 p.m.). Friday 9:30 open sits above it. After price traded back below the low formed in the NDOG, he looked for a run up into the 9:30 open and then a move lower through the CE.
- **Timestamps:** [0:07:17](https://youtu.be/-Qag-99u_BM?t=437) — Thursday 4:14 p.m. ET settle and 9:30 Friday open explained; [0:08:05](https://youtu.be/-Qag-99u_BM?t=485) — shades the gap and defines it; [0:08:53](https://youtu.be/-Qag-99u_BM?t=533) — premium-array expectation, CE, targets.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He says it is "inside the gap", so it uses that area as a premium array. Tied to his conviction after the low was taken out. The 4:14 settlement time is as heard in the captions (caption slightly unclear).

=== [2026 #13 — NFP Review & New Student Encouragement (ID -Qag-99u_BM)] — NDOG used as a liquidity reference (new day opening gap)  (aliases: "orange box")
- **Kind:** example only
- **What ICT says:** The low that price ran through on NFP "formed randomly" inside Thursday's new day opening gap (the orange box on his chart). Price took that low out and then continued lower.
- **How to use:** Marked in advance, the NDOG low was a visible liquidity point.

=== [2026 #14 — How New Students Should Start Part 4 \ Pillars Of Daily Log Entries (ID L81eMQhmXmc)] — Pillar 2 — RTH opening range gap and consequent encroachment delivery  (aliases: "regular trading hours opening range gap", "RTH ORG", "CE", "middle of the inefficiency")
- **Kind:** rule / study method
- **What ICT says:** On TradingView, use a 1-minute chart and switch the lower-right toggle from ETH (electronic trading hours) to RTH. The gap is between the previous day's 4:14 p.m. ET final print (the close of that 1-minute candle) and the 9:30 a.m. ET opening price. Draw a rectangle across the gap with the "mid level" option turned on (set it in the favorites) so the middle (consequent encroachment, CE) is shown. Log how price pulls back to half the gap and how quickly. In the example it does it almost immediately (the 9:31 candle open trades right down to it, "second minute after 9:30"). He estimates roughly a 70% likelihood it reaches the CE by 10:00 a.m. but says "don't hold me to that": keep daily records and calculate your own statistics at year-end.
- **How to identify:** RTH-only view; previous 4:14 p.m. close; 9:30 open; rectangle with mid-line.
- **How to use:** Anchor a target at the CE. If looking for lower prices, take any old fair value gap or bearish order block (once you learn them) and target the CE; no need to expect the whole gap to close. Annotate the screenshot with observations such as "third time this week it went to the RTH ORG CE by 10 o'clock", and the entry idea (e.g. an inversion fair value gap higher up selling down to the CE).
- **Example in this video:** 1-minute chart; the gap lies below the open (a gap-down); price opens at 9:30 and by 9:31 trades down to the CE; short idea from an inversion FVG up top to the CE.
- **Timestamps:** [0:12:33](https://youtu.be/L81eMQhmXmc?t=753) — pillar 2 introduced; [0:13:22](https://youtu.be/L81eMQhmXmc?t=802) — ETH/RTH toggle, 4:14 p.m. final print; [0:14:13](https://youtu.be/L81eMQhmXmc?t=853) — rectangle with mid level; [0:15:50](https://youtu.be/L81eMQhmXmc?t=950) — pullback to half the gap, 70% by 10:00 claim; [0:16:40](https://youtu.be/L81eMQhmXmc?t=1000) — anchor a target, annotate observations.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** The 4:14 p.m. time and the "70%" are his stated figures; the 70% is explicitly not to be trusted without your own stats.

=== [2026 #15 — My Model On NQ NFP Friday (ID Khj1veL49r8)] — Short from the RTH opening range gap high (9:30 open) with stacked PD arrays  (aliases: "high of the opening range gap", "daily volume imbalance", "daily bias on the balance sell side inefficiency", "new week opening gap high")
- **Kind:** example only (entry model application)
- **What ICT says:** Price traded up into the high of that day's RTH opening range gap, i.e. the 9:30 opening price. That level coincides with (a) the high of a daily buy-side imbalance / sell-side inefficiency (as heard: "daily bias on the balance sell side inefficiency", caption unclear), (b) the upper portion of a daily volume imbalance he shaded gray and has been discussing for about three weeks, and (c) the high of the new week opening gap (purple shaded area). Price "didn't want to take the liquidity out of up here" (the relative equal highs stayed intact), so he expected it to come down for the stops below. He later notes it was also the high of the daily volume imbalance (blue area) and gets "a lot of reaction there".
- **How to identify:** RTH ORG high at the 9:30 open; overlapping higher-timeframe imbalance (daily volume imbalance / daily inefficiency) and NWOG high; relative equal highs left untouched above.
- **How to use:** Short from the premium array, stop above, target the sell side below and the first presented FVG of the previous day. See stop/management below.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Fri Aug 28 after 9:30. Price rallies to the 9:30 open (gap high) and stalls; relative equal highs above remain untouched. Price then declines through the NWOG low and the RTH ORG low, into the NFP low and the NDOG.
- **Timestamps:** [0:04:58](https://youtu.be/Khj1veL49r8?t=298) — expects to take out the ORG low and the NFP-formed low in Thursday's NDOG; [0:05:46](https://youtu.be/Khj1veL49r8?t=346) — ORG high / daily imbalance / liquidity left up above; [0:06:34](https://youtu.be/Khj1veL49r8?t=394) — NWOG high (purple) and aggressive acceleration wanted; [0:19:22](https://youtu.be/Khj1veL49r8?t=1162) — also the high of the daily volume imbalance (blue).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** The video begins mid-trade (captions start 0:03:54), so the exact entry price is not given. Video 13 states the short was taken at the first high of the 10:00 hour.

=== [2026 #16 — Trading NFP Thursday High Resistance Liquidity Runs (ID xpH0wDANtmM)] — New Week Opening Gap (High Resistance Liquidity Run)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** The gap between Friday's RTH close and Monday's RTH open (similar to daily RTH gap, but spans the weekend). This gap is a significant liquidity magnet. On NFP week Thursdays (high volatility days), price often runs from intraday highs down to this new week gap low, targeting stop-losses above the gap high. This is called a "high resistance liquidity run" because price must overcome significant resistance (prior highs, relative equal highs) to reach the target.
- **How to identify:** Mark Friday's RTH close; mark Monday's RTH open; the void between is the new week opening gap.
- **How to use:** Use the low of this gap as a short target; use the high of this gap as stop-loss placement (just above it) for shorts.
- **Example in this video:** Monday's gap was targeted for a short entry; price ran from intraday highs down to this gap low throughout the day.
- **Timestamps:** [0:00:02](https://youtu.be/xpH0wDANtmM?t=2) "new week opening gap low," [0:00:52](https://youtu.be/xpH0wDANtmM?t=52) "relatively equal lows."
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** NFP week Thursdays see aggressive liquidity runs; use caution (position sizing, early profit-taking).

=== [2026 #17 — How New Students Should Start Part 3 (ID MB7clEZvF7s)] — New Week Opening Gap (NWOG) matrix: definition and how to build it
- **Kind:** definition | how-to
- **What ICT says:**
  - NWOG = difference between the Sunday 6:00 p.m. ET open (every Sunday) and the previous Friday's last print (ETH 4:59 p.m. on the 1-minute chart). If a holiday prevents trading on Friday, use Thursday.
  - Keep a rotating display of at least the last five weeks' NWOGs on a layout with nothing else on it; label each gap by its date and keep colors consistent. (He suggests labeling with the date, e.g., "NWOG Aug 30", any format works.)
  - How to draw (TradingView): switch to electronic trading hours (ETH); use the calendar "go to date" to jump to the previous Sunday at 18:00 (military time 1800 = 6 p.m. ET); zoom in; anchor the rectangle at the Friday close at 4:59 p.m. (the last print, down-close candle: anchor to the close) and drag up to the Sunday 6:00 p.m. opening price. Repeat going back week by week (example weeks: Sunday Aug 30; Friday Aug 21 4:59 p.m. close to Sunday Aug 23 open).
  - Manage the objects via the object tree: rename each (e.g., "NWOG Aug 23"), toggle visibility, copy the layout for each quarter and delete old ones; hiding old ones keeps them in the list for later.
  - Reading the matrix: hovering over a gap highlights it; use consequent encroachment of the gap; price "meandering around an empty space between two key PD arrays" can be read relative to the nearest NWOGs.
- **Example in this video:** Layout with five NWOGs and first presented FVGs; on 2 Sep price was in no-man's land between a previous Monday first presented FVG and Tuesday's first presented FVG (and Wednesday's), far from an NDOG and with another far below.
- **Timestamps:** [0:31:57](https://youtu.be/MB7clEZvF7s?t=1917) — NWOG definition, Sunday 6 p.m. vs Friday close, holiday rule; [0:32:46](https://youtu.be/MB7clEZvF7s?t=1966) — keep five rotating weeks; [0:33:35](https://youtu.be/MB7clEZvF7s?t=2015) — drawing via ETH + calendar; [0:35:12](https://youtu.be/MB7clEZvF7s?t=2112) — anchor to Friday 4:59 p.m. close, Sunday 6 p.m. open; [0:37:36](https://youtu.be/MB7clEZvF7s?t=2256) — object tree, rename; [0:39:12](https://youtu.be/MB7clEZvF7s?t=2352) — quarterly copies.
- **Nuance / caveats:** Reconciles with Oct 2024 videos in this batch (NWOG = Friday ETH settlement to Sunday 6 p.m. open). He says the matrix is for students; he himself uses paper journals.

=== [2026 #18 — How New Students Should Start Part 2 (ID djabvhihIM8)] — RTH Opening Range Gap (settlement vs 9:30 open) — large-gap handling  (aliases: ORG, "opening range gap", overnight gap, RTH OG)
- **Kind:** rule / refinement (re-application of the concept from [[L26-019]])
- **What ICT says:**
  - The gap runs from the prior day's regular-trading-hours settlement (captioned "4:14 p.m. Eastern", cash session end; the transcript garbles it as "9:14 p.m." on the open side) to the 9:30 ET open. Here Monday's settlement was about 29,511.5 (caption "0.9511 and a half") and price opened far below, a discount gap of "400 handles or more".
  - He grades the gap with fib levels from the 9:30 open up to the settlement: lines are counted off from the open ("one, two, three, four") with the fourth being the gap's midpoint / consequent encroachment. Normally price trades to the gap midpoint "with 70% likelihood by 10:00 a.m."; on this day it had not happened yet, so he treated it as "deferred delivery" and said price could skip it entirely.
  - On days with a huge gap you "always" should at least give price an attempt to trade into the gap. It may be moot (the market can be too weak and keep selling), but if you try and get stopped out that is okay, because a stop-out is usually followed by an easier setup.
  - Because of the size of the gap and "a lot of open air space up there", he says he is "heavier-handed on the longs": long entries are more likely to yield an exaggerated run in your favor than shorts on a day with that big a gap. He says this does not mean it cannot go lower.
  - The ORG and the 9:30 open line are drawn on the chart so that opening-range-gap levels can be compared directly with the daily array levels.
  - Alternative scenario he voiced pre-open: price could first run higher to sweep the early buy side (the stops of breakout sellers), then drop to try the lower octant of the daily array and bounce.
- **How to identify:** prior RTH settlement line; 9:30 open; measure open-to-settlement; midpoint (CE) = target price is statistically drawn to by 10:00.
- **How to use:** a gap above price after a big overnight drop is a bias toward long entries; use ORG fib levels as objectives; if the gap closure does not get a chance to start by mid-morning, do not fade it blindly ("I don't want to fade that on a day like today").
- **Example in this video:** NQ, open below Monday's ~29,511.5 settlement after an overnight run of about 500 handles lower; hypothetical longs from ~29,0xx–29,13x with the buy-side pools (initial intraday high, equal/"smooth" highs) and 29,212 as objectives; ORG midpoint not reached in the first hour.
- **Timestamps:** [0:01:50](https://youtu.be/djabvhihIM8?t=110) settlement and gap size · [0:26:32](https://youtu.be/djabvhihIM8?t=1592) always attempt the gap · [0:40:34](https://youtu.be/djabvhihIM8?t=2434) why heavier on longs · [0:56:42](https://youtu.be/djabvhihIM8?t=3402) gap midpoint, 70% by 10:00
- **Nuance / caveats:** The 70% figure is the same as in [[L26-019]] (it says half the gap fills by 10:00 about 70% of the time); here he phrases it as the CE of the gap.

=== [2026 #18 — How New Students Should Start Part 2 (ID djabvhihIM8)] — Confluence of daily array grading with ORG levels → "low hanging fruit" objective  (aliases: layered key levels, octant / quadrant grading)
- **Kind:** rule / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - If there is an inefficiency on the daily timeframe (here the gray BISI box from Part 1) and you grade it with fib levels (quadrants = 25%, octants = 12.5%), and one of those levels agrees exactly with a level on the opening range gap grading, that level becomes an obvious objective: "two levels lining right up at 29,212".
  - He cares more about reaching that shared level than about reaching the gap midpoint, even though the midpoint is also likely, because of NFP week, a day after significant consolidation, and a big overnight run lower: "I want to have a low hanging fruit objective because it's part of a consolidation profile day."
  - Once at that level: partial there, then watch whether price shows "unction" (interest) to keep going or gives up and breaks lower.
  - At the end he stresses you do not need order-flow tools or "inside the candle" data: open, high, low, close and time form the whole story ("you can read that book by its cover").
- **How to identify:** daily BISI graded in 8 parts; ORG graded from open to settlement; look for a price where one level from each lines up. He describes it as the "lowest octant / lower quadrant" of the ORG coinciding with the daily array's level; caption mapping of which octant is which is unclear. 29,212 is the same number as the 50% line of the daily BISI in [[L26-019]] (29,211.25) and sits inside the upper half of that array per this video.
- **How to use:** target = the shared level; stop = below the structure of the entry; first partial at or just before it; trail the remainder to the next level (here Wednesday Aug 26's first presented FVG and the octant inside it).
- **Example in this video:** hypothetical longs from 29,067.5 onward targeting 29,212; the runner partial was filled at 29,213 (~1:05:51) and the last contract went to the octant within Wed Aug 26's first presented FVG.
- **Timestamps:** [0:54:17](https://youtu.be/djabvhihIM8?t=3257) two things layer at same level · [0:57:30](https://youtu.be/djabvhihIM8?t=3450) why 29,212 is the objective · [1:05:51](https://youtu.be/djabvhihIM8?t=3951) partial at 213 · [1:22:23](https://youtu.be/djabvhihIM8?t=4943) "no-brainer", read the candle
- **Nuance / caveats:** Reinforces that stacking higher-timeframe PD array levels with the RTH gap levels is the high-probability filter he taught in [[L26-019]].

=== [2026 #19 — How New Students Should Start Part 1 (ID BWEPtHZ1Y2Y)] — RTH Opening Range Gap (previous settlement) and the half-gap statistic  (aliases: RTH OG, regular trading hours opening range gap)
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Before the 9:30 open, draw a line at the previous regular-trading-hours settlement (Friday's close here), labeled "RTH opening range gap settlement". Keep chart time on New York time.
  - At 9:30: if the open is above that price, the line becomes "RTH OG low"; if the open is below it, the line becomes "RTH OG high". So the word "settlement" turns into low or high depending on the opening price.
  - If price opens below the settlement it is a discount gap (premium gap if it opens above); the gap generally tries to fill.
  - Stated statistic: if there is a gap, half the gap gets filled by 10:00 AM about 70% of the time (the first 30 minutes after the open).
  - Plan: draw a fib from the open to the settlement; half gap is the 50% point of that range. Alternative path: price first runs the nearby sell-side (a minor low), then tries half gap, and a trader might leave a runner for full closure.
  - Keep a template/workspace that holds only RTH opening range gaps and a running tally.
- **Example in this video:** Friday's RTH settlement was about 29,487.5 ("487 half"); at ~8:39 price was ~29,420 (caption says "47 handles higher" — digits unclear). At 9:30 price opened below settlement (discount gap) and rallied immediately through the gap, past full closure ("gap closure and more"), heading to the high of the daily BISI box.
- **Timestamps:** [0:41:46](https://youtu.be/BWEPtHZ1Y2Y?t=2506) annotation rule · [1:36:58](https://youtu.be/BWEPtHZ1Y2Y?t=5818) 70% half-gap by 10:00 · [1:42:51](https://youtu.be/BWEPtHZ1Y2Y?t=6171) the 9:30 move
- **Nuance / caveats:** Two draws "in contention" for the opening range (9:30–10:00) were: the minor sell-side low and the RTH OG high. A new student will not know which one; the job is to observe which one price goes to at 9:30.

=== [2026 #19 — How New Students Should Start Part 1 (ID BWEPtHZ1Y2Y)] — Event horizon (partial-profit level)  (aliases: halfway to the draw)
- **Kind:** new coinage / rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Grade the distance between the most recent low (price just made) and the next draw-level (e.g., a daily quadrant line). The midpoint of that is the "event horizon" and the place to take a partial. "How can I make sure I take something away and reward myself for participation?"
  - For a beginner on a demo or paper trade: if you hold three micros, take one off at that level; the partial "pays" the transaction and removes the need to be right about the final target.
  - Rationale: price has been moving continuously one way for about half an hour with no significant run on the short-term opposite liquidity, so halfway is a reasonable place.
- **Example in this video:** hypothetical short from the volume imbalance in the daily SIBI above; low taken; draw level 29,371; partial at the halfway point (event horizon). After that, as price retraced, he watched the wick midpoint and rolled the stop down.
- **Timestamps:** [1:08:22](https://youtu.be/BWEPtHZ1Y2Y?t=4102) · [1:09:11](https://youtu.be/BWEPtHZ1Y2Y?t=4151) "event horizon" · [1:10:00](https://youtu.be/BWEPtHZ1Y2Y?t=4200) three micros · [1:27:06](https://youtu.be/BWEPtHZ1Y2Y?t=5226) answering "partials are stupid"
- **Nuance / caveats:** He counters people who say taking partials is stupid: here two partials plus a lowered stop paid roughly 30 handles (stop lowered to 29,412 locks ~30 handles from the hypothetical entry) and later "32 more handles" (caption).

=== [2026 #23 — NQ High Of Day Short Review (ID P8Pn8DHFSW8)] — Opening Range Gap (aliases: RTH opening range gap, ORG)
- **Kind:** definition + rule + example
- **What ICT says:** Gap between the previous day's 4:14 PM ET close (end of RTH) and the 9:30 ET open of the next day. Premium gap = opens higher; discount gap = opens lower. Divide the gap into octants and consequent encroachment (midpoint). 70% probability of trading to the midpoint (consequent encroachment) within first 30 minutes (by 10:00 ET).
- **How to identify:** Find the close price of previous RTH day and the open at 9:30 ET next day. Measure the difference. Use fib from high to low of gap; mark octants (upper quadrant, upper percentile, consequent encroachment, lower percentile, lower quadrant).
- **How to use:** In a premium gap (opens higher), expect price to retrace to at least the gap midpoint as low-hanging fruit. Don't demand full gap closure. For intraday: take first partial at the low formed before the gap, then manage for midgap. Entry signals: watch how bodies behave relative to octants.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Aug 23, opens at 9:30 ET at higher level than Aug 22 close (premium gap). Consequent encroachment (gap midpoint) = yesterday's 4:14 PM close. Price is expected to trade down to this level by 10:00 ET. ICT shorts into the gap's octants; targets the midpoint; later pushes for new day opening gap.
- **Timestamps:** [0:37:50](https://youtu.be/P8Pn8DHFSW8?t=2270) — defining ORG; [0:41:02](https://youtu.be/P8Pn8DHFSW8?t=2462) — 70% rule and octants; [0:50:47](https://youtu.be/P8Pn8DHFSW8?t=3047) — naked chart with fib on gap
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Don't demand full gap closure; that's greedy. Two partials (one at prior day's low, one at gap midpoint) are often sufficient for a profitable session.

=== [2026 #23 — NQ High Of Day Short Review (ID P8Pn8DHFSW8)] — New Week Opening Gap (aliases: NWOG, new week gap)
- **Kind:** definition + rule
- **What ICT says:** Difference between Friday's 4:14 PM ET close (end of RTH) and Sunday 6 PM ET open (start of new week in electronic markets). Project this gap across entire week. Keep last five weeks on chart but only mark gaps relevant to current price action.
- **How to identify:** Track Friday close to Sunday 6 PM open difference.
- **How to use:** Price often respects new week opening gap in early morning rally/decline. If price runs above it early, it signals strong directional bias. Mark it as a level not to be taken for granted.
- **Example in this video:** NQ rallies into new week opening gap early morning; doesn't reject it, suggesting bullish pressure. Later when bearish, price respects this level during retracements.
- **Timestamps:** [0:42:42](https://youtu.be/P8Pn8DHFSW8?t=2562) — identifying NWOG; [0:44:17](https://youtu.be/P8Pn8DHFSW8?t=2657) — how it guides price
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Keep workspaces separate for new week gaps only; don't clutter trading chart.

=== [2026 #23 — NQ High Of Day Short Review (ID P8Pn8DHFSW8)] — New Day Opening Gap (aliases: NDOG)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Similar logic to new week opening gap but from previous day 4:14 PM to current day 6 PM ET (restart). Use this as floor/ceiling depending on bias.
- **How to identify:** Previous RTH close to 6 PM open differential.
- **How to use:** Stop-loss placement. Target zone during reversals.
- **Example in this video:** NQ short trade stopped out just above new day opening gap low; ICT wanted to put limit just above this level to get full gap closure.
- **Timestamps:** [0:58:00](https://youtu.be/P8Pn8DHFSW8?t=3480) — stop loss at NDOG
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** None stated.

=== [2026 #25 — PreMarketSession NQ Consolidation August 25, 2026 (ID ieJSy4YsVgk)] — New Week Opening Gap Short Entry in Pre-Market
- **Kind:** example only
- **What ICT says:** (Demonstrated, not generalized) After London trends up overnight, the new week opening gap (Sunday 6 PM open to Friday 4:14 PM close from prior week) can be sold short when price fails to hold above it in early pre-market. Entry: just above the NWOG high (where it first rejected). Stop-loss: just above the upper quadrant or NWOG high anchor. Targets: Obsidian, sell-side liquidity, octants.
- **Example in this video:** NQ opened above NWOG at 9:30 on Sunday (premium gap). Failed to hold Monday-Tuesday pre-market. ICT shorts into the high, places stop above NWOG upper quadrant, targets Obsidian and then sell-side liquidity. Manages with partials and runner.
- **Timestamps:** [0:00:01](https://youtu.be/ieJSy4YsVgk?t=1) — entry setup; [0:01:51](https://youtu.be/ieJSy4YsVgk?t=111) — NWOG reference; [0:06:45](https://youtu.be/ieJSy4YsVgk?t=405) — watching order flow into gap
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** This entry is specific to pre-market consolidation after trending London. Not a rule for all trading.

=== [2026 #26 — Monday Review On NQ & PreMarket Session Rules Revisited (ID grcLMbjs-zs)] — New Week Opening Gap (NWOG) and New Day Opening Gap (NDOG) used with the daily array  (aliases: NWOG, NDOG)

=== [2026 #30 — Trade Management & Removing The Need To Be Right (ID F8s_vfK9HaM)] — New Week Opening Gap (NWOG) as a draw and Monday framework  (aliases ICT uses: "Sunday's opening", "terminus draw", "black hole")

  - Monday framework for experienced traders: if price opens and rallies away from the NWOG, and never trades back to it during Sunday evening (Asia) or London, and there is no runaway price action higher or lower, then on Monday it is "very easy" to aim for a run to the NWOG. He calls it a "no-brainer type thing".

- **How to identify:** NWOG not yet traded back to in Sunday evening / Asia / London, price displaced away from it, no runaway trend.
- **How to use:** short or long toward the gap (direction opposite the move away), take partials before the gap, and treat the gap high / low as the terminus.

=== [2026 #33 — PPI Market Review August 13, 2026 (ID RVHX56v-gUo)] — Event Horizon (Fibonacci 50% of a Multi-Day Range)
- **Kind:** definition + example
- **What ICT says:** Measure from one daily high to another (e.g., Tuesday's high to Monday's high), find the midpoint (50% Fibonacci level), and project it forward. That level often acts as a ceiling on strong bullish days or a floor on bearish days. It's a psychological equilibrium point where momentum often fades briefly before continuing.
- **How to identify:** Select two daily highs/lows separated by a few days. Apply Fibonacci; the 0.5 level = event horizon.
- **How to use:** Partial-taking target. Resistance on intraday rallies. If price blows through it cleanly, expect continued momentum. If it stalls there, it might retrace.
- **Example in this video:** Tuesday's high to Monday's high, 50% = event horizon. Price reaches it intraday, hesitates, then blows through on continued strength. ICT calls this out as a reference level.
- **Timestamps:** [0:08:03](https://youtu.be/RVHX56v-gUo?t=483) — measuring event horizon; [0:08:54](https://youtu.be/RVHX56v-gUo?t=534) — price action at/through it
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Works better on intraday charts after a strong directional break. Less reliable on choppy days.

=== [2026 #41 — Part 2 High Precision Secrets To Intraday Price Action (ID BD7kqmgdBU8)] — RTH opening range gap (ORG): definition, grading and the 70% / 10:00 statistic  (aliases: opening range gap, "regular trading hours opening range gap", half gap)
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - The ORG runs from the 9:30 ET opening price to the previous session's regular-trading-hours final print/settlement at 4:14 pm ET. On this day: high = 9:30 open at 28,576.75, low = Thursday's 4:14 pm print at 28,324.75 (a gap up).
  - Add the levels in the data window to confirm both prices are exact (he toggles RTH and then back to electronic hours to read the prices).
  - Grade the gap: high, upper octant, upper quadrant, consequent encroachment (CE, the half gap), lower quadrant/octant, low. Above the CE is premium, below is discount.
  - Half gap (CE) "70% of the time" gets traded to by 10:00 am ET. Here it traded to the CE at 9:43.
  - The time dimension: 9:30 open is the "tethered" level, so horizontal levels are price relative to time. He also draws a first-hour dealing range (9:30–10:30 high and low) as a second time-based range.
- **How to identify:** gap between prior RTH close (4:14 final print) and 9:30 open; confirm with RTH/ETH toggle.
- **How to use:** the graded levels tell you where PD arrays should form and which levels they should react from; then check where the bodies (not wicks) are relative to those levels.
- **Example in this video:** NQ-like prices, Friday: price returns to the 9:30 open, trades below and up again, falls short of it, then opens lower and trades right into it; a gap in premium acts as a breakaway; it trades down through the CE at 9:43 and fills the gap to the low at 28,324.75, closes beyond it after hours.
- **Timestamps:** [0:09:39](https://youtu.be/BD7kqmgdBU8?t=579) setting the 9:30 high and the RTH low · [0:11:12](https://youtu.be/BD7kqmgdBU8?t=672) CE and the 70% by 10:00 statistic · [0:12:01](https://youtu.be/BD7kqmgdBU8?t=721) first-hour dealing range
- **Nuance / caveats:** he has used two "last price" references over the years: the high of the 4:00 candle (if it is higher than the close) from his 1990s S&P days, and the 4:14 final print; see the shortcut model below.

=== [2026 #41 — Part 2 High Precision Secrets To Intraday Price Action (ID BD7kqmgdBU8)] — Opening range gap extensions: -0.2, -0.5, -1.0 (standard deviation)
- **Kind:** rule / projection
- **What ICT says:**
  - Measure from the 9:30 open (gap high) down to the previous settlement (gap low), then use negative fib extensions below the gap low: -0.2 ("negative 20", a very low threshold just outside the gap), -0.5 (the "50" level), and -1.0 (one standard deviation). He says he taught this "a couple of years" ago.
  - The market came "real close" to the -1 projection, and the -0.5 level created a small FVG that price traded up into and then sold off from.
  - Measurements do not need to be perfect ("I ain't got time for that").
- **How to use:** these are the exit levels for the runners (see scale-out below). The -0.2 level is a place to take a partial because price can dip below the gap low and then rip back up.
- **Timestamps:** [0:21:07](https://youtu.be/BD7kqmgdBU8?t=1267) -50 and -1 projections · [0:46:37](https://youtu.be/BD7kqmgdBU8?t=2797) -0.2, -0.5 and -1 revisited
- **Nuance / caveats:** the earlier segment mentions a "negative 50" retraction level and "negative one", while the later one adds the -0.2; whether he is using the extension in percent or in range multiples is not spelled out.

=== [2026 #47 — Chain Of Custody Of Price With RTH ORG (ID V5crdCw0AsY)] — RTH Opening Range Gap (RTH ORG)  (aliases ICT uses: regular trading hours opening range gap, opening range gap, Monday's RTH opening range gap)
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - The RTH ORG for a given day is bounded by two prices: the 9:30 opening price of regular trading hours (the open of the 9:30 candle) and the last print of the prior RTH session (Friday's last print at 4:14 p.m. ET for Monday's gap; the candle's close is used - he dropped the fib on the higher value, the close at 28,291.25 vs open 28,290.75, per captions).
  - "Those are the only two reference points you really need" when looking at regular trading hours; the CME website offers other data, "it's not a big deal."
  - On Monday the high of the gap was the 9:30 opening price and the low was Friday's last print; the blue box's low is "all the way down there."
  - If price gaps up and you are bearish, full gap closure is the draw.
  - Some students argued about using 4:14; he insists the 9:30 open to the final printed RTH price is correct.
- **How to identify:** switch to the RTH (regular hours) chart; mark the 9:30 candle open and the prior session's last print (4:14 p.m. ET candle close); box between them.
- **How to use:** (1) the box edges and midpoint define a dealing range; (2) grade it into octants/quadrants; (3) trade PD arrays that form on those levels in the direction of the draw (full gap closure).
- **Example in this video:** Monday RTH: last print Friday 4:14 p.m. ET about 28,291; Monday 9:30 open higher (about 28,5xx; implied by 9:3x prices near 28,521-28,536). Bearish bias; draw = fill of the entire gap down to Friday's last print.
- **Timestamps:** [0:04:01](https://youtu.be/V5crdCw0AsY?t=241) — RTH ORG introduced for Monday; [0:04:52](https://youtu.be/V5crdCw0AsY?t=292) — only two reference points (9:30 open and last RTH print); [0:06:28](https://youtu.be/V5crdCw0AsY?t=388) — high = 9:30 open, low = Friday 4:14 last print
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He mentions that the RTH ORG is separate from the daily suspension block (previous video's levels): you can use it alone and don't need to confer with the daily BISI to find the draw. Instrument not named (NQ implied).

=== [2026 #47 — Chain Of Custody Of Price With RTH ORG (ID V5crdCw0AsY)] — Layering the daily bias and daily inefficiency with the RTH ORG  (aliases ICT uses: "ring in" the daily levels, what takes precedence)

=== [2026 #52 — Reviewing High Resistance Liquidity Run Conditions - July 21, 2026 (ID e6XwTK4hVqI)] — Simplified Trading Model for Beginners (Half-Gap Rule)
- **Kind:** teaching technique
- **What ICT says:** (Demonstrated with Caleb) A beginner trader should start with a very simple model: (1) Look at the opening range gap (9:30 open to 4:14 previous close), (2) Identify the direction (gap up = look for shorts to half-gap; gap down = look for longs to half-gap), (3) Wait for a PD array to be traded to (hourly BISI, relative equal lows, etc.), (4) Enter short/long into that array, with target = half-gap (consequent encroachment of ORG). This removes decision-making and focus on simple mechanics.
- **How to identify:** Mark the opening range gap, calculate consequent encroachment (midpoint).
- **How to use:** Entry signal. When a PD array is traded to and direction aligns with gap direction, enter. Target = gap midpoint.
- **Example in this video:** ORG gap = 450 handles. Half-gap (CE) = 225 handles below open (approx). Caleb aims to short into the hourly BISI, target half-gap. Simple, mechanical, no overthinking.
- **Timestamps:** [0:00:47](https://youtu.ye/e6XwTK4hVqI?t=47) — explaining the model; [0:03:12](https://youtu.ye/e6XwTK4hVqI?t=192) — executing shorts to half-gap target
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** This model requires a "respectable amount of range" (30–40 handles minimum) to yield reasonable profits. If gap is too small, pass.

=== [2026 #53 — Trading In High Resistance Liquidity Run Conditions - July 21, 2026 (ID tp0qkiWCctw)] — Opening Range Gap & Gap Midpoint (Half-Gap)
- **Kind:** rule · entry model
- **What ICT says:** At market open (9:30 ET for NQ), price often gaps up or down. This gap is the area between the previous close and the new opening candle's low (if gapped up) or high (if gapped down). The midpoint of this gap is a powerful magnet; price often retraces into it as a secondary objective after the first target (relative equal lows or structural lows). "Find the mid-gap. There's your target. Aim for that. You might miss it, but it gives you a simple framework to start with."
- **How to identify:** On the 1-minute open candle (9:30), mark the high (if gap up) or low (if gap down). Draw a horizontal line at the midpoint. In this video, ICT marks the half-gap multiple times around 0:13:23–0:21:18 and later around 0:49:02.
- **How to use:** Use as a secondary target after taking partials at the first objective (relative equal lows or sell-side liquidity). Helps with position management: take one partial here, hold for further range. Provides psychological anchor—a hard level to aim for rather than "as low as possible."
- **Example in this video:** Opening gap: 9:30 open at ~18480 (approx), previous settlement lower; huge premium gap marked around 0:02:02. Half-gap calculated and marked as a secondary objective around 0:13:23. Reached and confirmed around 0:49:02 and 0:51:25.
- **Timestamps:** 
  - [0:02:02](https://youtu.be/tp0qkiWCctw?t=122) — "So there's a relative equal lows... enormous gap. It's a premium gap."
  - [0:13:23](https://youtu.be/tp0qkiWCctw?t=803) — Marking half-gap: "I'm going to remove all risk here... take one off underneath those relative equal lows... if it takes that out and fills me... then I'm going to bring the stop down."
  - [0:49:02](https://youtu.be/tp0qkiWCctw?t=2942) — Half-gap finally hit: "Half gap down here is what we're looking at."

=== [2026 #54 — NQ Futures Trade Review - July 20, 2026 (ID 2L88yH3LKcs)] — Event Horizon / Midpoint Between PD Arrays
- **Kind:** definition · price level
- **What ICT says:** The midpoint between two key price delivery arrays (FVGs, order blocks, highs, lows, etc.). This is a zone where price often stalls or reverses because it's the geometric center of a range. "Event horizon. It's a midpoint between two key PD arrays."
- **How to identify:** Identify two key levels (e.g., a swing high and swing low, or two gaps). Calculate the midpoint between them. That's the event horizon.
- **How to use:** Use as a secondary target or stall zone. Expect bodies to struggle to cross through event horizons without a reversal or consolidation.

=== [2026 #56 — NQ Futures Weekly Range Market Wizardry (ID NPU4gsM9jqA)] — New Week Opening Gap (NWOG) / Premium & Discount
- **Kind:** definition · gap · liquidity reference
- **What ICT says:** When a new week starts (Sunday 6 PM ET or Monday pre-market), the settlement price from Friday may differ from the opening price of the week. This creates a gap. If the gap is "premium" (above Friday's close), price will likely retrace back down into that gap mid-week. If "discount" (below Friday's close), price will likely rally back up into it. The gap defines the weekly trading range boundaries. "So, if price is bearish and we went lower, we're trading back up in, it's reasonable. It's reasonable for price to trade up into that on Friday of last week and then open on Monday or Sunday or whatever you want to look at it lower than where we closed on Friday. So, we have a new week opening gap." (0:05:43)
- **How to identify:** On the daily chart, mark the Friday RTH close. Mark the Monday/Sunday opening price. If opening > close, gap is premium. If opening < close, gap is discount.
- **How to use:** Premium gaps typically act as resistance (inversion FVG); price will retrace into them mid-week. Discount gaps act as support; price will rally into them. Use the gap as a weekly target or waypoint.
- **Example in this video:** Monday opens in premium relative to Friday's close, indicating a weekly bearish bias (down move expected). (0:05:43–0:07:25)
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:05:43](https://youtu.be/NPU4gsM9jqA?t=343) — "So we have a new week opening gap."
  - [0:06:35](https://youtu.be/NPU4gsM9jqA?t=395) — "If we're bearish this is going to act like an inversion fair value gap."

He wants a walk through the low and acceleration to the event horizon; if price closes below it, that would indicate a run toward the "545s" (price level, caption partial). First partial: one contract at event horizon; the rest left on the book as limit orders; once at event horizon the stop moves to just below the low of the inversion FVG that sits over the suspension block.
- **How to use:** event horizon = partial target; closing below = run to the next major pool.
- **Example in this video:** targets: 8:30 lows then event horizon, then ~545 area.
- **Timestamps:** [0:05:39](https://youtu.be/fFi2QEApVGM?t=339) — event horizon, ballpark; [0:16:16](https://youtu.be/fFi2QEApVGM?t=976) — at event horizon between an old low and an old low
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** In L26-002 the event horizon is the 50% of an unrealized dealing range for fading a rally; here it is the 50% between two lows for continuation to the downside.

=== [2026 #63 — Part 2 \ How To Probe Low Probability RTH Opening Ranges 07/14/2026 (ID bz4QNPcpmWE)] — Targets: RTH opening range gap CE, upper half, NWOG that did not fully close  (aliases ICT uses: unfinished business, decidedly weak, consequent encroachment of the opening range gap)

=== [2026 #64 — How To Probe Low Probability RTH Opening Ranges 07/13/2026 (ID rtIcbG6twrI)] — Preparing the RTH opening range gap CE before 9:30 (manual fib)  (aliases ICT uses: RTH ORG, half gap, mid gap, regular trading hours opening range gap CE)
- **Kind:** rule (process) / how-to
- **What ICT says:**
  - With a large gap between Friday's settlement (RTH close, about 30,048.5 per captions) and the pending Monday open, prepare before the bell: put the fib on the exact Friday settlement level, drop it down toward the 9:32/9:33 mark on the time axis (not at 9:30, so you can see the candle open), enable "show all levels," make the CE line black and thick.
  - When the 9:30 opening price prints, drag the fib's other anchor to it; the 50% is the "RTH ORG CE" (label it so you do not lose sight of it).
  - Prefer drawing manually to scripts: scripts can only crunch the data they're given, the platform's data sometimes looks wrong, and doing it by hand keeps you involved ("as the author, if it doesn't jive with me, I won't use it").
  - A huge gap tends to attract an attempt to fill; he thinks the market is predisposed to draw toward the purple Friday-settlement line and toward buy-side near the 30,048.5 level, though it doesn't need to get there.
  - He estimates about 70% likelihood that price rallies to the CE of the RTH ORG around 10:00 ("that's usually the statistics"; caption unclear).
- **How to identify:** RTH ORG = prior-day RTH settlement (last print) to the 9:30 open; CE = 50%.
- **How to use:** CE as the first target for the gap draw, expect to approach it by about 10:00; a probe long uses the stop under the CE of the wick, etc.
- **Example in this video:** Monday 7/13: Friday settlement ~30,048.5; Monday's open lower (opening price implied about 29,714.5 given the 7/14 CE of ~29,881.5, my arithmetic from L26-063); RTH ORG CE annotated.
- **Timestamps:** [0:10:33](https://youtu.be/rtIcbG6twrI?t=633) — Friday settlement colored purple; [0:12:09](https://youtu.be/rtIcbG6twrI?t=729) — how I prepare: fib on the level; [0:13:46](https://youtu.be/rtIcbG6twrI?t=826) — CE of RTH ORG labeled; [0:14:37](https://youtu.be/rtIcbG6twrI?t=877) — drag to the opening price, manual preference; [0:17:02](https://youtu.be/rtIcbG6twrI?t=1022) — 70% likely to go up by 10:00
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** The 70% figure is his stated approximate statistic (caption unclear whether it refers to price reaching the CE). Consistent with L26-047 about RTH ORG definitions.

=== [2026 #65 — Market Review & NQ Futures Trade Recap 07/08/2026 (ID JmAJfuSkY1g)] — New Day Opening Gap (aliases: NDOG, opening gap)
- **Kind:** definition + rule
- **What ICT says:** The difference between settlement price (RTH close at 4:59 ET previous day) and RTH open at 9:30 ET next day (actually 6:00 PM ET previous day to 9:30 ET next day in NQ's continuous session). NDOG has consequent encroachment (the wick extension). If price fails to leave a body at or above NDOG CE, that's bearish. If it does and closes above, that's bullish.
- **How to identify:** Mark previous day's RTH close and current day's 6:00 PM open (electronic open); also mark 9:30 RTH open. NDOG CE is the wick into one of these.
- **How to use:** NDOG acts as a bearish or bullish bias filter. Failed body above NDOG CE = no bullish intent. Successful close above = bullish intent. Also used as PD array discount or premium source for targets.
- **Example in this video:** NQ on 07/08, NDOG was set from 07/07 4:59 ET close to 07/08 6:00 PM ET open. Price rallied into NDOG at 9:30 open but failed to leave a body above NDOG CE, signaling bearish.
- **Timestamps:** [0:07:14](https://youtu.be/JmAJfuSkY1g?t=434) (NDOG defined) · [0:08:50](https://youtu.be/JmAJfuSkY1g?t=530) (NDOG CE: doesn't leave body above = bearish) · [0:09:37](https://youtu.be/JmAJfuSkY1g?t=577) (part of MMSM, NDOG failure = selloff).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** NDOG is a higher-timeframe filter; failure on 1m doesn't mean rejection of entire NDOG; must watch for closes and body placement.

=== [2026 #65 — Market Review & NQ Futures Trade Recap 07/08/2026 (ID JmAJfuSkY1g)] — Opening Range Gap (aliases: ORG, RTH opening range gap)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** The difference between RTH open at 9:30 ET and RTH close at 4:59 ET the same day. This is a daily structure. 70% likelihood of trading to the 50% midpoint (consequent encroachment) within 9:30-10:00 ET.
- **How to identify:** Mark 9:30 open and 4:59 close on daily; plot Fibonacci levels; focus on 50% midpoint.
- **How to use:** Targets, support in deep discounts, entry confirmation when price holds or rejects at ORG levels.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 07/08, ORG from 9:30 open. Price ran into new day opening gap at 10:00, failed, then dropped. Later, rallied back and used ORG 50% as support.
- **Timestamps:** [0:16:02](https://youtu.be/JmAJfuSkY1g?t=962) (ORG definition, contrasted with NDOG) · [0:16:53](https://youtu.be/JmAJfuSkY1g?t=1013) (ORG 50% = 70% likelihood by 10:00 ET).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** NDOG is part of ORG; ORG is the 9:30–4:59 structure.

=== [2026 #67 — Analysis Delivering On NQ (ID N3qz13Hl-gg)] — Event Horizon (aliases: midpoint, 50% level, halfway point)
- **Kind:** measurement tool
- **What ICT says:** Event Horizon is the midpoint between two price levels (e.g., high and low of an inefficiency, or two historical swing extremes). Price often pauses or reverses at this midpoint. It's a powerful confluence when combined with other factors.
- **How to identify:** Find two price levels (a low and a high, or two equal lows); mark the price midpoint between them.
- **How to use:** Use as a pause/reversal zone. If price is moving toward a low and hits Event Horizon on the way, expect a bounce or consolidation. Also use as a partial profit target.
- **Example in this video:** Michael noted they were "at event horizon between the low of this to the high of that inefficiency." Price was pausing there before continuing lower.
- **Timestamps:** [0:07:56](https://youtu.be/N3qz13Hl-gg?t=476) (at event horizon, expecting further movement lower after close below).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Event Horizon is a technical level, not an order-flow level; it's useful for timing entries/exits within a larger move.

=== [2026 #70 — Missed Entry How To Navigate The Same Trade Idea (ID EZB4vBUFdHw)] — Partials to relatively equal lows and event horizon; scaling out contracts  (aliases ICT uses: best-case scenario, event horizon, runner)

=== [2026 #71 — Futures Market Review \ Lecture On Shadows Validating PD Arrays (ID pq9WuZ9q4Bg)] — RTH opening range gap (historical origin and use)  (aliases: "premium gap opening", "regular trading hours opening range gap")
- **Kind:** definition / history
- **What ICT says:** If the market opens above the prior day's close, that is a premium gap (an RTH opening range gap higher). Early in his career he took the idea from Larry Williams' "open vs yesterday's close" observations and reformulated it: look for a fill; if bullish, even half of the gap being filled is enough to see price go higher; if bearish, want price to break through the gap and then use the gap range to measure projections lower. He says this is not part of the current model but influenced him as a technician.
- **How to use:** Monday NQ: opened and traded up into the gap but could not reach its midpoint (CE), then broke lower, which he used for the short.
- **Timestamps:** [0:46:47](https://youtu.be/pq9WuZ9q4Bg?t=2807) — description; [0:10:14](https://youtu.be/pq9WuZ9q4Bg?t=614) — Monday gap and CE.

=== [2026 #72 — Enigma FVG Projections & Protractions & Review \ June 27, 2026 (ID rbNhlJSXDaA)] — Event horizon (CE between two liquidity pools)  (aliases: "event horizon", "consequent encroachment", "midpoint")
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:** Between two pools of liquidity (an old high and another old high/low reference), the midpoint of the range is the consequent encroachment, which he also calls the "event horizon". A close above that midpoint gives confidence that price will draw to the opposite pool; it is not a guarantee. On DXY after June 7: a close above the CE between the minor buy side and the old high, and the next day (Wednesday) price traded higher.
- **How to use:** CE close as confirmation of the draw; used with a stated terminus (101.977).
- **Timestamps:** [0:05:44](https://youtu.be/rbNhlJSXDaA?t=344) — CE or event horizon; [0:01:41](https://youtu.be/rbNhlJSXDaA?t=101) — the plan from June 7.

=== [2026 #73 — Futures Commentary & Motivational Lecture \ June 24, 2026 (ID hxdAOfcp6gE)] — Event horizon (50% of a range between two lows / old high and target)  (aliases ICT uses: "event horizon", "consequent encroachment of the range", "halfway point")
- **Kind:** definition / rule / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - Anchor a fib on a low and drag to the next low (or from an old high to the target), delete all levels in the settings, and keep only the 0.5 level. That halfway point is the event horizon.
  - It is a "very strong candidate" for price to reach; price may simply touch it and turn around. He uses it to build structure for predetermined partials and to weigh the magnitude and velocity of a price run.
  - A close beyond it (below, in a short) increases confidence that the low/target behind it will be taken out. If it fails to close beyond, treat it as a possible bounce or reversal.
  - Event horizons can be nested: draw one from the first low and another from the second; treat the zone between the two levels as an inefficiency/gap ("good enough for government work"). A close below both raises the confidence that both lows will be taken out.
  - At the event horizon, "it's an event on the horizon you must make a decision about": where to move the stop, and whether to take a partial. He says you should take partials ("pay the trader"); don't demand target-or-nothing, because nothing is easy to hit.
  - Halfway is "always a really good rule of thumb."
- **How to identify:** two reference points (lows/highs/old high and target); midpoint between them.
- **How to use:** use as first target and partial level; adjust stop when reached; read closes beyond it as confirmation; grade the range with octants/quadrants if desired (below).
- **Example in this video:**
  - GBPUSD (short): objective reached; lowest of two relative equal lows is the next level; he says not to demand a target below it and to take something off at that low or at the CE of its wick.
  - NQ long (Monday): old short-term high anchored at roughly 30,869.75 to the target (minor buy side/ATH area) at 30,975.50; midpoint is the event horizon at about 30,922.75 (he had it on 30,923 by eye; "the actual tick is 922.75"). Price reached it, and then could not trade to the 30,975.50 minor buy side before breaking lower.
- **Timestamps:** [0:05:42](https://youtu.be/hxdAOfcp6gE?t=342) — he is asked about "my event horizon concept"; [0:07:16](https://youtu.be/hxdAOfcp6gE?t=436) — only the 0.5 level, "very strong candidate"; [0:08:05](https://youtu.be/hxdAOfcp6gE?t=485) — nested event horizons; [0:27:26](https://youtu.be/hxdAOfcp6gE?t=1646) — NQ midpoint 30,922.75; [0:28:16](https://youtu.be/hxdAOfcp6gE?t=1696) — decision point, partials, "pay the trader".
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** In L26-072 he used the same word for the CE of a range between two liquidity pools with a "close above it" confidence rule. Here it is also a partial/stop checkpoint. Same math (0.5), more management.

=== [2026 #74 — Teaching Calub Complex Opening Range Delivery \ June 22, 2026 (ID k2QLuxMIZio)] — Event Horizon (halfway point between a 1-hour minor BSL pool and an old high)  (aliases ICT uses: "event horizon", spoken in captions also as "either/any horizon")
- **Kind:** new coinage (as used here; the term may have been defined in an earlier video)
- **What ICT says:**
  - Event horizon is "the halfway point between the 1-hour minor buy-side liquidity pool and then the old high" — i.e. the midpoint (50%) of that span.
  - It is the first objective after entry: "rip right on into event horizon, and then accelerate to minor buy side, which is up here from the hourly chart" (the 1-hour minor buy-side liquidity he had tweeted about that morning).
  - He plans to take one contract off at/just below event horizon and then move the stop up.
  - Later he notes bodies are not "laying on" the halfway point yet: price has wicked just above it a couple of times but not closed above it.
- **How to identify:** take the 1-hour minor buy-side liquidity pool and the old high above/near it; compute the midpoint.
- **How to use:** first partial / scale-out level, with the stop trailed after it; full target is the 1-hour minor BSL (and he explicitly is "not going to aim for the full new all-time high", though "it could do it").

Price reached event horizon (wicks above, bodies not yet on it) but the orders ended up closed out through his own TP-drag error rather than the planned partial structure.
- **Timestamps:** [0:02:44](https://youtu.be/k2QLuxMIZio?t=164) — definition of event horizon and initial stop placement; [0:04:23](https://youtu.be/k2QLuxMIZio?t=263) — "take one off at event horizon... then navigate the stop up"; [0:08:19](https://youtu.be/k2QLuxMIZio?t=499) — bodies not yet laying on the halfway point.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** caption wording is garbled ("any horizon", "either horizon") but clearly refers to the same level. If the coinage is new to the reader, flag it as an ICT 2026 midpoint-of-range target.

=== [2026 #75 — Futures Market Commentary \ June 18, 2026 (ID bUN_f8U_1Eg)] — Event Horizon (aliases: midpoint, old low to next target)
- **Kind:** measurement tool
- **What ICT says:** Event Horizon is the midpoint between two price levels. When used in daily analysis, it's often the midpoint between an old low and the next target/inefficiency below. This midpoint is a pause zone or bounce point; price often pauses here before continuing to the next target.
- **How to identify:** Find an old low and a target below it; calculate the midpoint price.
- **How to use:** Use as an intermediate pause/bounce zone. If you're short and price is at Event Horizon, expect a pause or bounce before continuation to the next target.
- **Example in this video:** Bitcoin: Michael used old low to next target; Event Horizon is the midpoint between them.
- **Timestamps:** [0:19:50](https://youtu.be/bUN_f8U_1Eg?t=1190) (event horizon concept: old low down to next target or pool of liquidity).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Event Horizon is a technical level, less critical than institutional liquidity levels.

=== [2026 #76 — Futures Commentary \ June 09, 2026 (ID 18vX-vv2bsg)] — New Day Opening Gap (NDOG) / New Week Opening Gap (NWOG) (aliases: opening gap, weekly gap, settlement gap)
- **Kind:** definition + targeting tool
- **What ICT says:** NDOG is the gap between RTH close (4:59 ET previous day) and RTH open (9:30 ET next day). NWOG is the gap between week close and week open. Michael uses these as reference levels and as targets for reversals. When price approaches these gaps, it often acts as inversion FVGs (weak commitment).
- **How to identify:** Mark the settlement price (4:59 ET close) and the next day's RTH open (9:30 ET); the gap between them is NDOG. Mark Friday close and Monday open for NWOG.
- **How to use:** Use NDOG/NWOG as targets and bounce zones. On the 5m or 1m chart, use the gradient to show the NDOG levels.
- **Example in this video:** Crude oil: Michael graded the new week opening gap (settlement price to next week open). On the 5-minute chart, price utilized the gap and launched lower; it was a target zone.
- **Timestamps:** [0:03:19](https://youtu.be/18vX-vv2bsg?t=199) (new week opening gap: high and last week settlement price) · [0:04:08](https://youtu.be/18vX-vv2bsg?t=248) (shows how NWOG is graded on 5m chart; used as launching price level).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** NDOG/NWOG act as institutional reference points; use them as targets or bounce zones, not as hard support/resistance.

=== [2026 #86 — Market Alchemy - Trading ATH \ May 14, 2026 (ID wprXVW1pW9Y)] — RTH Opening Range Gap (discount opening range gap) and its retest as discount sensitivity  (aliases: "regular trading hours opening range gap", "discount array", "trampoline")
- **Kind:** definition + rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - The RTH opening range gap is the space between the close of the prior session's last candle (the 4:15 PM ET settle) and today's 9:30 open. A gap lower is a "discount opening range gap": price opened below where it settled the day before, i.e. it is being "offered at a discount".
  - Classic expectation: a premium/discount gap tries to fill; the gap closure happens either way, whether you short it or buy it.
  - But if you trade against the higher-timeframe freight train (here a violent bull run), shorting the fill is not a trade for a new trader.
  - Better use: if price overcomes the gap (a candle goes up through/over it), then the very next candle opens and trades back down to it, and the gap now "acts as a discount array". In a bull market, that first return into the gap is the original first run into the range and should act as discount sensitivity: price uses it like a trampoline, drops in, then runs energetically away.
  - If you miss that move, do not chase candle by candle; anchor with a vertical line at the open so you keep time in view.
- **How to identify:** compare the 4:15 PM ET close of the previous day with the 9:30 open; mark the gap box between them (open as the low, previous close as the high on a gap-down day).
- **How to use:** long on the first retest into the gap after price has overcome it, with the higher-timeframe bias bullish; use the gap's height as the unit of measurement for upward projections (next concept).
- **Example in this video:** NQ opens at 9:30 below the prior close (gap-down). Price immediately overcomes the gap on one candle, the very next candle opens and trades back down into it, and it bounces energetically. ICT also notes he traded the "full mini, not the micro".
- **Timestamps:** [0:01:56](https://youtu.be/wprXVW1pW9Y?t=116) — defines today's RTH opening range gap and expects upside draw; [0:10:35](https://youtu.be/wprXVW1pW9Y?t=635) — discount gap versus 4:15 PM settle; [0:13:45](https://youtu.be/wprXVW1pW9Y?t=825) — retest of the overcome gap acts as a discount array; [0:14:32](https://youtu.be/wprXVW1pW9Y?t=872) — "trampoline".
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** restates and extends his opening-gap teaching: the gap is used (a) as support after being overcome and (b) as a ruler for projections.

=== [2026 #86 — Market Alchemy - Trading ATH \ May 14, 2026 (ID wprXVW1pW9Y)] — Opening Range Gap "standard deviations": the gap height as a ruler with a time window (Market Alchemy)  (aliases: "multiplication table", "replications / standard deviations", "projection")
- **Kind:** new coinage / entry-timing model
- **What ICT says:**
  - Draw a Fibonacci tool from the opening price (9:30 open, the low of the gap) to the prior close (top of the gap): that height is "one standard deviation". Project it upward multiple times (1, 2, 3 ... 7, 7.5, 8).
  - Think of a multiplication table: the vertical axis is price multiples, the horizontal axis is time. First go up the vertical axis (count the multiples), then when price enters the time window (here 11:30) look horizontally; the right multiple "unlocks like the tumblers in a lock".
  - He estimates the reachable count with a thumb: he holds his thumb out until it spans the gap height, then steps it up the screen; roughly 6.5 to 7.5 deviations.
  - Testing on the day: 6.5 and 7 were already exceeded before 11:30, so they could not be the turning level. 7.5 (caption: "2.5") was reached just before 11:30 (wonderful but not the turn). 8 deviations is the next level: price turned there at the start of the first hour of the lunch window.
  - Earlier teaching restated: when the market is consolidating, measure the opening range gap at 0.25 and 0.50 (graded levels). Everything is "graded" because the market is algorithmic.
  - An independent projection: the 10:00 to 11:30 run measured with a Fibonacci projection also points to the same vicinity.
  - ICT says this level-by-time technique is "not found anywhere else", and that parts are being held back for his written books but he will teach it on the channel.
- **How to identify:** (1) gap box = 9:30 open to prior 4:15 PM close; (2) project multiples upward; (3) the candidate multiple is the one not yet exceeded before 11:30; (4) watch candle bodies at that multiple inside 11:30–12:30.
- **How to use:** as the profit-taking or reversal-short zone on strong up days: level (multiple) plus time (lunch window) together define the turning point, and a short is taken from there (see Inversion FVG short).
- **Example in this video:** NQ 14 May 2026: gap = open to prior 4:15 close; 8 deviations is where price stalls in the first hour of the lunch window; he calls it "a turning point randomly in the first hour of the 2-hour lunch period" and challenges "talk to me about not picking a daily high".
- **Timestamps:** [0:07:28](https://youtu.be/wprXVW1pW9Y?t=448) — multiplication-table analogy; [0:27:56](https://youtu.be/wprXVW1pW9Y?t=1676) — Fibonacci from the open to the close of the gap; [0:30:16](https://youtu.be/wprXVW1pW9Y?t=1816) — 0.25 and 0.50 grading in consolidations; [0:33:25](https://youtu.be/wprXVW1pW9Y?t=2005) — thumb measurement; [0:35:02](https://youtu.be/wprXVW1pW9Y?t=2102) — test 6.5, 7, 7.5, then 8.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** counts are approximate in the captions (caption unclear around "2.5"/"7.5" and "6.5 to 7.5"). He says you will need to practise daily: it feels cumbersome at first. The Market Alchemy title is not explicitly explained; this section is the main new material.

=== [2026 #86 — Market Alchemy - Trading ATH \ May 14, 2026 (ID wprXVW1pW9Y)] — Event Horizon (50% of the fib drawn between two lows)  (aliases ICT uses: "event horizon")

- **What ICT says:** after the first partial at the minor sell-side low, he drew a fib between that low and the next low and "50% is this line... that's event horizon". It is the second take-profit area when trading against higher-timeframe order flow. Price "took the profit below that", i.e. his exit filled at event horizon.
- **How to identify:** fib between two liquidity points (here two lows); 50% line.
- **How to use:** second scale-out level; the final target is just beyond the relative equal lows.
- **Example in this video:** short from the iFVG, partial at minor sell-side, event horizon partial, remaining contracts at the relative equal lows (one tick beyond); price then ran lower without him.
