=== [2026 #89 — ICT 2026 Futures Review & RTH ORG Commentary \ April 29, 2026 (ID tbEzAhdv_Ak)] — Regular Trading Hours Opening Range Gap (ORG) and Quadrants / Octants
- **Kind:** definition + use
- **What ICT says:** When US markets open at 9:30 AM ET, price often gaps relative to yesterday's close. This gap is the "opening range gap" (ORG). The gap divides into four quadrants (25% sections). Expect price to eventually fill the gap and test at least the lower quadrant (if gapped down) or upper quadrant (if gapped up). If price gaps down, expect initial trade up to ~25% of the gap (lower quadrant), then expect it to drop further below the gap (gap-and-go).
- **How to identify:** Mark the previous close, mark the open; draw the gap high/low. Divide into four equal segments (quadrants). Identify the octant (1/8) levels within.
- **How to use:** If gap down: expect run to lower quadrant, then run lower (below the gap). If gap up: expect run to upper quadrant, then expect potential reversal or continued run. On FOMC: use the gap as a reference structure; price may oscillate within the gap range, testing quadrants multiple times.
- **Example in this video:** NQ April 29, 2026. Gap down at 9:30 (Friday's close was higher). Price traded up into the lower quadrant (did not reach 25% of gap), then came back down and was testing both lower and upper quadrants as market makers whipsawed retail.
- **Timestamps:** [0:04:38](https://youtu.be/tbEzAhdv_Ak?t=278) — opening range gap explained · [0:06:13](https://youtu.be/tbEzAhdv_Ak?t=373) — octant and quadrant levels identified · [0:17:53](https://youtu.be/tbEzAhdv_Ak?t=1073) — Friday's ORG as reference
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** On FOMC days, the gap may not follow typical rules; be flexible. Upper octant and lower octant are the 1/8 marks; trading within these is precision price action.

=== [2026 #90 — ICT 2026 Trading MNQ Futures & CFD US100 \ April 28, 2026 (ID jz0RbL1oVK8)] — Event Horizon (Gap Between Liquidity Pools)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Event horizon is the area between two liquidity points (e.g., between a gray pool and a sell-side liquidity pool, or between two relative equal lows). It marks a transition zone. When price pierces through event horizon with conviction, the next liquidity target becomes more likely. It is a measurement tool for partial profit-taking and conviction building.
- **How to identify:** Identify two sequential liquidity points (gray pool low, then the next sell-side pool, or two relative equal lows); the gap between them is the event horizon.
- **How to use:** Use event horizon as a partial-profit-taking zone. When price reaches event horizon, take 20–50% of position. Track whether price moves through event horizon quickly (strong) or slowly (weak). Speed of movement = confidence in the continuation.
- **Example in this video:** MNQ April 28. Event horizon was marked between the gray pool midpoint and the sell-side liquidity pool at ~27,069. Price needed to break through this area with heaviness (stretched out candles, extended bodies). When it did, ICT took 2–3 lots off the 10-lot position.
- **Timestamps:** [0:24:04](https://youtu.be/jz0RbL1oVK8?t=1444) — event horizon identified · [0:27:20](https://youtu.be/jz0RbL1oVK8?t=1640) — speed through event horizon as signal
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Do not demand a measured move through event horizon; speed and heaviness matter more than hitting exact price.

=== [2026 #90 — ICT 2026 Trading MNQ Futures & CFD US100 \ April 28, 2026 (ID jz0RbL1oVK8)] — Opening Range Gap (ORG) & Quadrants / Octants
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** At the 9:30 AM ET open, the market creates a gap relative to yesterday's close. This gap is divided into quadrants (25% sections). The opening price at 9:30 and the high/low of the ORG are reference points. On April 28, the 9:30 opening was at 27,184.25 (MNQ); this became a magnet on price during the session.
- **How to identify:** Mark yesterday's close, mark the 9:30 open; the gap between them is the ORG. Divide into quadrants.
- **How to use:** Use the opening price as a reference; when price returns to it, expect either a bounce or a break-through depending on structure. In this session, price aimed to return to the 9:30 open on the short move.
- **Example in this video:** MNQ April 28. 9:30 open at 27,184.25; lower quadrant at 27,227.75. Price moved down from pre-market highs to eventually target the 9:30 open level.
- **Timestamps:** [0:00:49](https://youtu.be/jz0RbL1oVK8?t=49) — quadrant level reference · [0:04:51](https://youtu.be/jz0RbL1oVK8?t=291) — opening price as magnet
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** On volatile days (high geopolitical uncertainty), the ORG may be less reliable; use it as reference but remain flexible.

=== [2026 #91 — ICT 2026 Futures Review \ April 24, 2026 (ID 2OVAvl4H3IY)] — Opening Range Gap (ORG) Consequent Encroachment as Reference
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** The opening range gap (9:30 AM ET opening price vs. prior close) has a consequent encroachment (the CE of the gap). This CE becomes a reference for the entire day. If price drops below the ORG CE with conviction, sell-side is likely to be hit hard. If price stays above ORG CE, buy-side is being protected.
- **How to identify:** Mark the 9:30 open; mark the ORG high/low; calculate the CE.
- **How to use:** Use ORG CE as a major reference point. Watch whether price respects or violates it. If broken, the next target is often relative equal lows below.
- **Example in this video:** NQ April 24. ORG CE was at ~27,080. As price moved lower, this level was a key reference. When broken with conviction, the next sell-side pool became the target.
- **Timestamps:** [0:01:12](https://youtu.be/2OVAvl4H3IY?t=72) — ORG and CE defined · [0:06:06](https://youtu.be/2OVAvl4H3IY?t=366) — ORG as endless supply of setups
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** On news-driven days (like FOMC), ORG may be less reliable.

=== [2026 #94 — ICT 2026 Futures Market Review \ April 21, 2026 (ID PCMv3J6_9Lg)] — RTH Opening Range Gap (Regular Trading Hours)
- **Kind:** definition + rule + filters
- **What ICT says:** The gap between the previous day's close (or end of RTH) and the next day's 9:30 ET open. These gaps are pools of liquidity. You only need the **three most recent RTH gaps** to do intraday trading without bias or higher-timeframe bias. Use them hierarchically: today's gap, yesterday's gap, Friday's gap. Mark both the high and low of each gap as price targets.
- **How to identify:** Chart tool: mark the zone between yesterday's close (4:00 PM ET) and today's 9:30 AM ET open. The high of the gap is the "RTH opening range gap high"; the low is the "RTH opening range gap low". Also mark the "consequent encroachment" level—the midpoint or lowest wick touching the gap.
- **How to use:** When targeting a move, use the RTH gaps as sequential targets: first target = today's RTH opening range gap (high or low, depending on direction); if taken, next target = yesterday's RTH opening range gap; if taken, next target = Friday's RTH opening range gap. When price reaches a gap, expect it to stop, pause, or reverse at that level. Mark the gap on a notepad to keep the chart clean. Use the octant or quadrant (dividing the gap into 8 or 4 zones) for finer entry points.
- **Example in this video:** ES, April 21, 2026. Three gaps: (1) Tuesday RTH (9:30 ET open to Friday close), high ~26,800+; (2) Monday RTH, high ~26,800+; (3) Friday RTH, high 26,695 (the target ICT tweeted in the morning). Price rallies, falls short of ATH, then sells off. First stop: Monday/Tuesday RTH gap high (~26,800). Then sold off through both those gaps. Second stop/target: Friday RTH gap low (~26,695). Price reached it, reversed (the octant level stopped it perfectly). Also dug into Friday's gap with a specific octant level (the 1/8 mark).
- **Timestamps:** [0:03:51](https://youtu.be/PCMv3J6_9Lg?t=231), [0:08:36](https://youtu.be/PCMv3J6_9Lg?t=516), [0:11:00](https://youtu.be/PCMv3J6_9Lg?t=660), [0:25:20](https://youtu.be/PCMv3J6_9Lg?t=1520), [0:31:47](https://youtu.be/PCMv3J6_9Lg?t=1907)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Opening range gaps from older days (more than 3 days back) are old data and less reliable. The three-gap rule works because it captures the current market structure; older gaps are stale. Use a notepad, not chart annotations, to track the levels—it keeps you focused on price action, not visual clutter.

=== [2026 #96 — ICT 2026 Lecture:  Trading ATH In The ICT PD Array Matrix \ April 20, 2026 (ID r-ztz7kReYc)] — RTH Opening Range Gap at highs: gap high = prior RTH settlement; octants and CE as read of intent  (aliases: "RTH ORG", "discount gap", "lowest octant")
- **Kind:** rule / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - RTH opening range gap = Friday's settlement (RTH close at 4:14 PM ET candle) versus Monday's 9:30 open. Monday opened lower: a discount gap near all-time highs. The gap high is Friday's settlement; he labels the gap box "RTH ORG" and its midpoint is consequent encroachment.
  - Expectations at ATH when bullish: price can trade above the gap high and then use the gap as a discount PD array ("something to support price"), possibly making a higher ATH; or price can fill to half (CE) and on to full closure.
  - Gap-fill tendency: price tends to go all the way to full closure, but if still bullish "you have to at least yield" to the possibility it trades above the high and uses the gap for support.
  - He was not interested in trading the first run into half gap, being unsure of full closure, and he would not have entered at full closure either: he would wait to see if it uses the high.
  - Reading the morning: a candle went to CE of the gap and closed below the gap's lowest octant (an eighth-division line, caption unclear). Because bodies would not even sit near the lowest octant, let alone above it, he read that as unlikely to run for full closure, and perhaps the extreme high of the gap, so the session high might be in.
  - Same gap logic as in the L26-086 video (RTH opening range gap and its overcome-then-retest behaviour).
- **How to identify:** prior day RTH settlement vs 9:30 open; box; midpoint; octant lines.
- **How to use:** at ATH a discount gap can be bought only if price proves it by exceeding the gap high and holding the gap as support; a sale idea starts when bodies refuse to approach the gap's upper range.
- **Example in this video:** MNQ, Mon 20 April 2026: Friday 4:14 PM settle vs 9:30 open lower; the first candle goes to CE and closes below the lowest octant; ICT interprets as the high of the morning.
- **Timestamps:** [0:16:07](https://youtu.be/r-ztz7kReYc?t=967) — RTH gap and discount gap; [0:19:19](https://youtu.be/r-ztz7kReYc?t=1159) — CE, full closure, or use as discount array; [0:20:55](https://youtu.be/r-ztz7kReYc?t=1255) — close below the lowest octant.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** "octant"/"quadrant" use is thin; same octant term appears in L26-074.

=== [2026 #96 — ICT 2026 Lecture:  Trading ATH In The ICT PD Array Matrix \ April 20, 2026 (ID r-ztz7kReYc)] — Prior Friday's RTH opening range gap CE as a target level (two-key-level framework)
- **Kind:** rule / example
- **What ICT says:** the gap from Thursday's 4:14 PM ET settle to Friday's 9:30 open: its consequent encroachment (50%) is a key level (he coloured it deep blue, bold and dashed). He does not need the high and low of that gap. The framework going into the day was two levels: (1) 50% of last Friday's RTH opening range gap, and (2) 20% of the weekly range (TGIF); "specifically anchored the majority to TGIF". The short idea had a best-case target there.

=== [2026 #97 — ICT 2026 Futures Market Review \ April 18, 2026 (ID 9nmjg331xGQ)] — Event Horizon
- **Kind:** definition + calculation
- **What ICT says:** The midpoint between two significant price levels, calculated by (Level A + Level B) ÷ 2. Event horizon is a price magnet—a level where smart money often holds price temporarily before continuing higher or lower. It's not a target; it's an observation point. When price reaches event horizon, pause and ask: does it have support/resistance there? Can it continue? If price moves through event horizon decisively with strong bodies, the move is likely to continue to the next target.
- **How to identify:** Identify two key levels (e.g., Thursday's daily high at 26,563 and all-time high at 26,859). Calculate midpoint: (26,563 + 26,859) ÷ 2 = 26,711. Mark it on the chart.
- **How to use:** Don't trade at event horizon; use it as a reference. When price is below it, event horizon acts like support (price wants to reach it). When price is above it, it acts like resistance (price wants to push past it to the target). If price stalls at event horizon, it may be a sign of weakness or consolidation.
- **Example in this video:** ES, April 18, 2026. Event horizon = 26,711 (between Thursday's 26,563 high and ATH 26,859). Price ran up from the morning low, reached 26,711, and continued higher to 26,859. Event horizon was a way station, not a reversal point.
- **Timestamps:** [0:04:40](https://youtu.be/9nmjg331xGQ?t=280), [0:10:11](https://youtu.be/9nmjg331xGQ?t=611), [0:14:56](https://youtu.be/9nmjg331xGQ?t=896), [0:30:48](https://youtu.be/9nmjg331xGQ?t=1848)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Event horizon can be calculated from any two levels (daily highs, RTH gaps, key wicks, all-time highs). Use it when you need a midpoint reference. It's different from relative equal highs or balanced price ranges—it's purely mathematical, not based on historical price touching.

=== [2026 #98 — ICT 2026 Futures Market Review \ April 17, 2026 (ID X-xYcsOG9Yg)] — RTH Opening Range Gap Exploration & Standard Deviation
- **Kind:** daily structure + extension rule
- **What ICT says:** When the market is in an uptrend (longer-term bias bullish), it can "explore" (temporarily dip) below the RTH opening range gap to a standard deviation level. The standard deviation is calculated from the opening range gap midpoint, extended downward by 0.5 standard deviations. This level is a magnet during the morning session. Once price reaches the -0.5 standard deviation, it often reverses and returns to the opening range gap high or above. This is a structured exploration, not a breakdown.
- **How to identify:** 
  1. Mark RTH opening range gap high and low.
  2. Calculate midpoint (event horizon).
  3. Calculate the range of the gap (high - low).
  4. Extend downward by 0.5 × range to find the standard deviation level (-0.5σ).
  5. Price will often test this level in a bullish day before reversing.
- **How to use:** When price drops below the opening range gap in a bullish market, don't panic. It's an exploration to the standard deviation level. Place limit orders at the -0.5σ level, expecting a bounce. Once price bounces from -0.5σ and returns to the opening range gap high, it's a strong bullish signal (it's confirmed the structure and is now ready to run to the next target).
- **Example in this video:** MNQ, April 17, 2026. RTH opening range gap was high 26,xxx, low Wednesday's 4:14 PM settlement. Price dipped below the gap to approximately -0.5σ, hit, and reversed back up to opening range gap high, then continued higher to 26,711 (event horizon) and eventually 26,859 (all-time high).
- **Timestamps:** [0:18:14](https://youtu.be/X-xYcsOG9Yg?t=1094), [0:19:03](https://youtu.be/X-xYcsOG9Yg?t=1143)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** The -0.5σ standard deviation is a mapped level before price reaches it (not after). ICT mentions "I mapped this out before we went down there"—meaning he calculated the level in advance and annotated it on the chart.

=== [2026 #98 — ICT 2026 Futures Market Review \ April 17, 2026 (ID X-xYcsOG9Yg)] — Event Horizon (Intraday Management)
- **Kind:** reference + midpoint calculation
- **What ICT says:** The midpoint between two significant levels (daily high and all-time high, for example) is an intraday management tool. Price often gravitates toward event horizon during the mid-morning or afternoon session. When price is below event horizon, it's attractive for longs (price wants to reach it). When price is above event horizon, it's a consolidation zone (price is between event horizon and the next target). Use it as a reference, not a hard target.
- **How to identify:** Calculate (Level A + Level B) ÷ 2 = event horizon. For April 17, event horizon was (26,563 + 26,859) ÷ 2 = 26,711.
- **How to use:** When trading intraday, know your event horizon. It's a psychological midpoint. Price often runs to it, consolidates, then continues. Use it to scale partials or add to positions depending on where you are relative to it.
- **Example in this video:** Event horizon 26,711 became a visible holding point and reference level during the afternoon session.
- **Timestamps:** [0:09:31](https://youtu.be/X-xYcsOG9Yg?t=571), [0:21:29](https://youtu.be/X-xYcsOG9Yg?t=1289)

=== [2026 #99 — ICT 2026 Entries & Drills Part 2 \ April 16, 2026 (ID eft9_3ekDCY)] — RTH Opening Range Gap (ORG) and the 70% CE tendency  (aliases: regular trading hours opening range gap, opening gap, "the 9:30 gap", half gap, CE of the gap)
- **Kind:** rule (reinforcement of a known concept, with new framing for beginners)
- **What ICT says:**
  - The gap is framed only on regular trading hours: the previous day's RTH settlement price (he says "4:14 p.m." ET, the last RTH print/candle) to the 9:30 ET opening price. He insists it is always framed on RTH, not on the 24-hour session.
  - If a gap forms at the 9:30 open, there is about a 70% probability that price makes an attempt to trade back to half the gap (the CE of the gap). It does not have to reach it, and it does not have to reach full closure, though it can.
  - Best-case targets for the drill: the half gap first, then 3/4 gap closure, then full gap closure (the RTH settlement price = "opening range gap low" when the gap is a premium gap).
  - Even after price trades through the gap, "always allow for return back to consequent encroachment of the opening range gap".
  - The 9:30 opening price itself is a level that can be re-traded and revisited at any time ("retested, rebooked, repriced").
- **How to identify:** compare the 9:30 opening price with the previous RTH settlement. Open above settlement = premium gap (bearish bias for the drill). The CE is the midpoint between the two prices.
- **How to use:**
  - Pre-stage the chart before the open: no indicators, just a line pre-drawn at the previous settlement and a second line/tool parked near price. When the 9:30 candle opens, drag the second anchor to the candle's open price and read off the CE so you can state the level immediately (his example CE read as "370 and 3/4").
  - Premium gap = look only for shorts, only for things that will participate in going lower. Use the gap's lower pools of sell-side liquidity as the draw.
  - Before the open, do not treat a pending price as the open price. Wait for the opening bell.
- **Example in this video:** 16 Apr 2026 (Thursday). Roughly ten minutes before the open price is inside a small sloppy range, above the prior RTH settlement, so a premium opening gap is expected. The CE is staged ahead of the bell. At 9:30 the gap opens higher ("premium gap opening's up here"); he wants price to return to the gap, with two sell-side pools below as the draw, the nearer one higher probability. He shorts (demo on TradingView), price trades down to the sell side, then later runs through the gap low area and on to the negative 0.5 projection, before coming back up to the CE of the gap.
- **Timestamps:** [0:05:29](https://youtu.be/eft9_3ekDCY?t=329) 70% probability of trading to CE of the gap, not necessarily full closure · [0:07:23](https://youtu.be/eft9_3ekDCY?t=443) anchoring CE to the 9:30 candle open and tweeting it · [0:13:01](https://youtu.be/eft9_3ekDCY?t=781) "half of the gap ... 70% of the time it makes an attempt to trade to half gap" · [0:55:19](https://youtu.be/eft9_3ekDCY?t=3319) student plan: shorts only in premium gaps in first 30 minutes, aim for half gap, best case 3/4 or full
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** frames the 70% as a statistical tendency to be confirmed by the student over about 20 days of observation, not a guarantee. He says repeatedly it need not close fully. The "4:14 p.m." settlement reference is stated by him; treat the exact timestamp as his convention for the last RTH print.

=== [2026 #99 — ICT 2026 Entries & Drills Part 2 \ April 16, 2026 (ID eft9_3ekDCY)] — Opening Gap Bias Rule for the Drill  (aliases: "we only want to look for shorts")
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** if the 9:30 open is higher than the previous day's RTH settlement, that sets the drill bias bearish; only look for shorts. The mirror (open lower than settlement) is implied but not spelled out in this video.
- **How to identify:** 9:30 open versus 4:14 p.m. ET prior-day settlement.
- **How to use:** anchor the short on an old FVG from before the session or one that forms during the opening range, and aim for the gap's CE and sell-side liquidity. He prefers a pre-session FVG over one formed inside the opening range, because the latter is sometimes favorable and sometimes not (he says he has tried to teach people not to lean on those so much).
- **Example in this video:** the FVG he used was a pre-session one formed in the 7:00 to 9:30 window; he notes bodies were being shared (tight, overlapping) inside it.
- **Timestamps:** [0:22:42](https://youtu.be/eft9_3ekDCY?t=1362) open above settlement sets bias bearish, only look for shorts · [0:23:28](https://youtu.be/eft9_3ekDCY?t=1408) pre-session FVG (7:00 to 9:30) as anchor
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** daily context added to the bias: after about nine consecutive up-close days with ES and NQ at all-time highs, a retracement was "healthy"; a possible short-term or intermediate-term high, so both the gap and the daily context pointed lower ([0:26:20](https://youtu.be/eft9_3ekDCY?t=1580)).

=== [2026 #99 — ICT 2026 Entries & Drills Part 2 \ April 16, 2026 (ID eft9_3ekDCY)] — Opening Range Gap Negative 0.5 Projection  (aliases: negative 0.5 standard deviation of the ORG, algorithmic run, "measuring the gap")
- **Kind:** new coinage / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - When price fills the gap, reaches the gap low and goes through it, the question is where to aim next. Measure the whole opening range gap (9:30 opening price to the RTH gap low) with the Fibonacci tool and toggle the negative 0.5 level. That level projects outside the gap and is a standard-deviation style measurement for the next run.
  - He calls that run algorithmic, based on the 9:30 opening price and the RTH ORG low.
  - In practice the body often finishes right on or just above the level and the wick goes a tad below it, and then price reacts.
  - It is not found in Wyckoff or older technical analysis, in his view.
- **How to identify:** fib drawn across the whole gap, with -0.5 beyond the far end (below the gap low for a premium gap).
- **How to use:** target for a short that has traded through the gap low; after reaching it, expect a return to the CE of the ORG (his stated outcome in this video).
- **Example in this video:** gap high = 9:30 open, gap low = RTH settlement. Price trades down through the gap low, reaches the -0.5 projection (wicks a little lower), then retraces to the CE of the gap. He says it "wasn't perfect" but was worthwhile, and it found "no lower" before returning to the CE.
- **Timestamps:** [0:36:32](https://youtu.be/eft9_3ekDCY?t=2192) fib drawn on the gap, -0.5 projection · [0:37:21](https://youtu.be/eft9_3ekDCY?t=2241) "where do you aim for" after going through the low · [0:55:19](https://youtu.be/eft9_3ekDCY?t=3319) body on the level, wick slightly below
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** presented as the "next lesson" after the basic half-gap drill (two to four weeks into practising it).

=== [2026 #99 — ICT 2026 Entries & Drills Part 2 \ April 16, 2026 (ID eft9_3ekDCY)] — Opening Range Gap Octants and Quadrants  (aliases: lower octant, full gap closure level)
- **Kind:** refinement (chart tool)
- **What ICT says:** he overlays octant (eighths) and quadrant levels on the ORG, then removes them to reduce clutter. A "lower octant" of the gap is a level he wants price to reach after the sell side is taken ("if we can touch that same low and carry us into the lower octant level, that'll be sufficient"). At one point bodies did not touch the lower octant and only the wick went through it.
- **How to use:** progression of targets inside the gap: sell-side liquidity first, then the lower octant, then the full gap closure (RTH settlement).
- **Timestamps:** [0:20:13](https://youtu.be/eft9_3ekDCY?t=1213) wants price to wilt into the lower octant of the gap · [0:27:53](https://youtu.be/eft9_3ekDCY?t=1673) bodies didn't touch the lower octant, wick went through
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** exact placement of "lower octant" is not stated numerically (caption unclear; likely the 12.5% level nearest the gap low).

=== [2026 #101 — ICT 2026 Entries & Drills \ April 15, 2026 (ID KASpfAd1MnI)] — Regular Trading Hours Opening Range Gap (RTH ORG) and 9:30 open as references
- **Kind:** definition / example
- **What ICT says:**
  - The previous day's RTH close (the 4:14 PM ET candle close/settlement, as he says) and today's 9:30 open define the RTH opening range gap; the 9:30 open is the gap high when it opens above settlement; the settlement can be relabelled as RTH ORG low.
  - The 9:30 opening price acts as a level (reclaim/hold above or below) and its CE is a drill target.
  - First reads at the open: no gaps = sit still.
- **Example in this video:** at 9:30 price opens above the prior settlement; ICT labels the 9:30 open as ORG high and later targets the ORG CE for a short ([0:06:45](https://youtu.be/KASpfAd1MnI?t=405); [0:33:25](https://youtu.be/KASpfAd1MnI?t=2005)).
- **Timestamps:** [0:06:45](https://youtu.be/KASpfAd1MnI?t=405); [0:09:16](https://youtu.be/KASpfAd1MnI?t=556).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** the 4:14 PM wording is as captioned.

=== [2026 #103 — ICT 2026 Futures Opening Range Tape-Reading \ April 14, 2026 (ID G9mVorSyvR0)] — Opening Range Gap (NWOG / NDOG)  (aliases: RTH opening range gap, weekly opening gap)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Gaps formed at session opens (electronic open at 6 p.m. ET or 9:30 RTH). If a gap is not closed by near-open action, it becomes a reference level. Regular trading hours (RTH) opening range gaps are marked and tracked for potential fill or reversal.
- **How to identify:** Mark the gap at open. Track whether bodies close the gap or bodies stay above/below.
- **How to use:** If bodies stay above a gap, that's bullish (don't go back for fill quickly); if bodies move into it, that's retracement (potentially weakness).
- **Example in this video:** Sunday evening NQ opening: large premium gap opening (way below previous close). This became NWOG to reference. Monday low retracted into prior week's 30% (TGIF), then never returned to the gap—bodies stayed above, showing no fill intended near-term.
- **Timestamps:** [0:04:42](https://youtu.be/G9mVorSyvR0?t=282) regular trading hours opening range gap annotated.
- **Nuance / caveats:** Gaps are reference points, not automatic fill targets.

=== [2026 #104 — ICT 2026 Futures Opening Range Tape-Reading \ April 13, 2026 (ID Il_DM4T7nzg)] — Opening Range Gap (RTH Opening Range Gap / New Week Opening Gap)  (aliases: NWOG, gap closure)
- **Kind:** definition + entry context
- **What ICT says:** Mark the gap formed at 9:30 ET (RTH open) or at Sunday 6 p.m. ET (electronic/new-week open). The high and low of that gap are reference levels. A "full gap closure" means price trades all the way back through the gap and out the other side (below the low, if it opened with a premium gap above prior close). Consequent encroachment of that gap (the midpoint) is often a first test. Bodies closing above or below that midpoint tell you whether the move is committed.
- **How to identify:** Find the bar at 9:30 or 6 p.m. ET. Mark the high and low. Measure the midpoint. Track whether bodies breach and hold above/below it.
- **How to use:** If looking for bullish, you want to see bodies stay above the gap's midpoint and then full gap closure + continuation above the prior close (RTH close from previous Friday, 4:14 p.m. ET). If full gap closure doesn't happen with strength, that's a warning sign. Gaps can serve as support/resistance for future retracements.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Sunday opening: large premium gap below Friday close (around 18,340 vs prior 18,530). Monday 9:30 opened even lower. Throughout the morning, price tested the consequent encroachment (gap midpoint), then bodies moved above it. Full gap closure (return to Friday's 4:14 p.m. close level of 18,400+) and continuation above it was the bullish outcome being watched.
- **Timestamps:** [0:01:14](https://youtu.be/Il_DM4T7nzg?t=74) gap opened at Sunday; can't trade it (gap risk). [0:02:04](https://youtu.be/Il_DM4T7nzg?t=124) RTH opening range gap marked, consequent encroachment level. [0:47:23](https://youtu.be/Il_DM4T7nzg?t=2843) full gap closure as bullish confirmation. [0:58:19](https://youtu.be/Il_DM4T7nzg?t=3499) RTH close level (4:14 ET Friday).
- **Nuance / caveats:** Gaps formed in uncertain/event-driven openings (geopolitical, war, peace news) have lower probability early on; bodies' behavior (reluctance, hesitation) is the real signal, not gap location alone. "Reluctance" (price not doing what it "should") is a canary-in-the-coal-mine warning.

=== [2026 #104 — ICT 2026 Futures Opening Range Tape-Reading \ April 13, 2026 (ID Il_DM4T7nzg)] — Event Horizon  (aliases: midpoint split, equilibrium target)
- **Kind:** target method
- **What ICT says:** When you have two key levels (e.g., high of inversion FVG and high of new week opening gap), split the difference (midpoint). This is often a reversal zone or partial-profit target. It comes from the idea that "octave" or "gradient" levels have algorithmic significance.
- **How to identify:** Mark two highs or two lows. Measure the midpoint between them. Apply Fib if needed.
- **How to use:** As a partial profit target or a place to watch for reversal/congestion.
- **Example in this video:** High of new week opening gap + inversion FVG high, split in half = event horizon. Price stops there or reverses. Use as a target on bread and butter entries.
- **Timestamps:** [0:35:26](https://youtu.be/Il_DM4T7nzg?t=2126) split difference = event horizon.
- **Nuance / caveats:** Not guaranteed; just probabilistic.

=== [2026 #105 — ICT 2026 Futures Weekend Review \ April 11, 2026 (ID RY67bW2UhxY)] — Continuous Contract vs. Front Month Contract (aliases: contract rollover adjustment, settlement price toggle)
- **Kind:** refinement
- **What ICT says:** The continuous contract shows price as if contracts roll seamlessly; it smooths gaps that occur at rollover dates. But ICT does not use this toggle to find fair value gaps. He uses it to reveal volume imbalances that exist on one contract but are hidden on the other due to the gap. Both closing prices must agree (e.g., 25,333.00 on both); when they don't match exactly, the toggle is useful. The settlement price toggle also changes what you see; if you have settlement price toggled on/off differently than ICT, you won't see the same suspension blocks or volume imbalances.
- **How to identify:** In TradingView, the continuous contract has a toggle button for rollover adjustment (lower right, gray hue). The front month contract shows only settlement price toggle. Check both; compare the PD arrays across them.
- **How to use:** Start analysis on continuous contract to get historical context unobstructed by rollover gaps. Identify breaker blocks and key levels. Then compare how the same price action uses those levels on the front month. If one contract's suspension block aligns better with bodies closing, use that as your key reference. Document both in your notes as alternates.
- **Example in this video:** MNQ June (front month) vs. MNQ continuous: when toggling the continuous adjustment off/on, a suspension block appears with a volume imbalance that the front month doesn't show cleanly. The consequent encroachment of the continuous is 25,495 (10.5 handles above front month's 25,474.5). This 20-handle spread reveals whether traders on each contract are using the same level.
- **Timestamps:** [0:05:28](https://youtu.be/RY67bW2UhxY?t=328) shows the toggle button; [0:13:20](https://youtu.be/RY67bW2UhxY?t=800) explains why both levels matter; [0:18:03](https://youtu.be/RY67bW2UhxY?t=1083) contrast with spotty price action on front month only.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Some traders have the settlement price toggle on when ICT doesn't; this is why they don't see suspension blocks. If you don't see a volume imbalance or suspension block in your chart, check both toggles. The continuous contract function is NOT for finding new fair value gaps; it is for finding volume imbalances that wouldn't be visible otherwise and for ensuring you have clean historical price data before contract rollovers.

=== [2026 #105 — ICT 2026 Futures Weekend Review \ April 11, 2026 (ID RY67bW2UhxY)] — Opening Range Gap & 70% Rule (aliases: 9:30 gap, regular trading hours opening range gap, RTHORG)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** Every morning at 9:30 ET, the market opens with a price; 70% of the time in the first hour, price will trade to the consequent encroachment (midpoint) of the gap created by the overnight move. This is your highest-probability first-hour draw. If you only trade the opening range gap for the first hour, you'll have a significant edge.
- **How to identify:** Settlement price at 4:15 PM ET → opening price at 9:30 ET = opening range gap. Calculate the midpoint (consequent encroachment).
- **How to use:** This is your session bias. Premium gap → expect fill of midpoint on downside. Discount gap → expect fill on upside. Do not trade beyond this until you confirm 70% is working in your hands.
- **Example in this video:** April 11 market review: MNQ opened with a huge premium gap; price was expected to fill down to consequent encroachment, which it did repeatedly through the morning.
- **Timestamps:** [0:33:17](https://youtu.be/RY67bW2UhxY?t=1997) states 70% figure; [0:54:08](https://youtu.be/RY67bW2UhxY?t=3248) compares opening price to RTH opening range gap.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** The 70% applies to the first hour (first 30 minutes for newer traders). This is the foundation of session bias; everything else is optional.

=== [2026 #105 — ICT 2026 Futures Weekend Review \ April 11, 2026 (ID RY67bW2UhxY)] — Event Horizon (aliases: midpoint of a large range, void, ether level)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** When a range (premium to discount, old high to old low, PD array) is very wide, the midpoint often appears to be empty—a void. But price gravitates to it with uncanny precision. This "event horizon" is where the market holds balance; algorithms use it as a target.
- **How to identify:** Measure a large range (e.g., daily suspension block high to low). Find the exact midpoint. Draw it as a reference.
- **How to use:** Use as a pullback target, reversal zone, or continuation staging point. If price overshoots it, expect it to come back.
- **Example in this video:** MNQ daily chart: event horizon level between the low of suspension block and the upper quadrant; price bounced in this area, used it as support, then continued.
- **Timestamps:** [0:06:02](https://youtu.be/RY67bW2UhxY?t=362) introduces event horizon conceptually.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** This level is not marked on the chart; it's pure geometry. Some traders miss it because it "doesn't exist" visually.

=== [2026 #106 — ICT 2026 Tape-Reading Opening Range \ April 10, 2026 (ID Hiq47zliJ_Y)] — Consequent Encroachment / Midpoint Draw (aliases: half gap, 50% level, mid-level draw)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** The midpoint of the opening range gap is the consequent encroachment; price draws to this level in 70% of first-hour trades. This is the lowest-probability miss in ICT's methodology. If you're watching the market at 9:30, the consequent encroachment is your first target to watch.
- **How to identify:** (Settlement price at 4:15 PM + Opening price at 9:30 AM) ÷ 2 = Consequent Encroachment.
- **How to use:** This is your first-hour bias. Premium gap → target consequent encroachment on downside. Discount gap → target upside. Once price reaches it, look for the next level or consider taking profit.
- **Example in this video:** Friday: CPI weak; opening range gap consequent encroachment was the draw for the first hour.
- **Timestamps:** [0:06:26](https://youtu.be/Hiq47zliJ_Y?t=386) sets up consequent encroachment; [0:10:05](https://youtu.be/Hiq47zliJ_Y?t=605) shows price reaching it.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** On decoupled days, this level may be delayed or missed; don't force it.

=== [2026 #110 — ICT 2026 Opening Range Tape Reading \ April 07, 2026 (ID gLt8kAG7noQ)] — Opening Range Gap (ORG)
- **Kind:** definition | rule
- **What ICT says:** Yesterday's regular trading hours (RTH) open and close establish the RTH opening range gap for the next day. The gap between 4:14pm ET close and 9:30am ET open forms the structure. If 9:30 open is below the prior close, it's a discount gap (bullish setup likely).
- **How to identify:** Compare 4:14pm ET close to 9:30am ET open; note the high and low of the gap range and subdivide by octants (1/8ths) and quarters.
- **How to use:** Use ORG levels as support/resistance and potential rally/selloff targets. The 50% and 70% retracement levels are common targets.
- **Example in this video:** NQ opened 9:30 at a discount (below prior close); immediately rallied to ~70% of the ORG and failed, indicating weakness. Then traded back into and below the ORG low, confirming further downside potential.
- **Timestamps:** [0:03:29](https://youtu.be/gLt8kAG7noQ?t=209), [0:33:38](https://youtu.be/gLt8kAG7noQ?t=2018), [0:34:25](https://youtu.be/gLt8kAG7noQ?t=2065)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** On high-uncertainty days (like this one with geopolitical risk), price action is "wild and woolly" and less precise. ORG analysis is still valid but requires more patience to see clean execution.

=== [2026 #111 — ICT 2026 Market Review \ April 06, 2026 (ID 5ycp9oGKPms)] — Premium Gap vs. Discount Gap & Opening Range Gap (ORG) Targets
- **Kind:** definition | rule | entry model
- **What ICT says:** Premium gap = open above prior close. Discount gap = open below prior close. On a premium gap day, the first protocol is to expect price to trade back into the ORG (back towards or below the opening price, seeking the low of the gap). On a discount gap, expect price to extend higher first, then consolidate. For premium gaps: first target is the opening price itself (9:30); second target is the midpoint (consequent encouragement); full gap closure is the best-case scenario but often won't occur (use a "low-hanging fruit objective"). Time window: 9:30 AM to 10:00 AM (first 30 minutes). Probability: ~70% of the time, at least to the midpoint.
- **How to identify:** Compare prior day's close (4:14 PM ET) to 9:30 AM ET open price. Gap = the difference. Subdivide by octants and quadrants. Mark as "premium" (higher) or "discount" (lower).
- **How to use:** Use ORG levels as natural S/R. Expect partial or full gap retracement within 30 minutes. Stops above (for shorts) or below (for longs) the ORG extremes. Targets at the midpoint or at full closure depending on market structure and time of day.
- **Example in this video:** NQ opened at a premium (above prior close). ICT expected downside penetration to the opening price or the midpoint within the first 30 minutes. This was achieved by ~9:50 AM.
- **Timestamps:** [1:23:28](https://youtu.be/5ycp9oGKPms?t=4808), [1:25:06](https://youtu.be/5ycp9oGKPms?t=4906), [1:25:55](https://youtu.be/5ycp9oGKPms?t=4955), [1:26:49](https://youtu.be/5ycp9oGKPms?t=5009), [1:27:35](https://youtu.be/5ycp9oGKPms?t=5055)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** On macro-uncertainty days (geopolitical risk, FOMC, NFP), the 70% probability may decline, but the setup remains valid for multiple days or intraday.

=== [2026 #112 — ICT 2026 Market Commentary \ April 06, 2026 (ID m_ND0oO67cU)] — Opening Range Gap (ORG) Application on Premium Gap Openings
- **Kind:** rule | entry model | refinement
- **What ICT says:** On a premium gap opening (open above prior close), the first protocol is to watch if price can make it back to the opening price (9:30) within the first 30 minutes. This is the minimum expectation. If bulls want to run higher, they need to at least defend the 9:30 level and start sending it higher from there. Conversely, if they can't hold 9:30, expect downside toward consequent encouragement (the midpoint of the ORG).
- **How to identify:** Compare prior-day close to 9:30 open. If open is higher (premium gap), mark the 9:30 price level.
- **How to use:** On premium gap days, watch the 9:30 level closely. If price closes above it, bulls are defending; if it closes below, bears are winning. Use 9:30 as a pivot for intraday entries.
- **Example in this video:** NQ: "This is a 9:30 opening price. We went below it here. We came right back to it now. So bulls, if they really want to send it higher, this would be the minimum expectation here."
- **Timestamps:** [0:15:22](https://youtu.be/m_ND0oO67cU?t=922), [0:16:11](https://youtu.be/m_ND0oO67cU?t=971), [0:20:09](https://youtu.be/m_ND0oO67cU?t=1209), [0:20:56](https://youtu.be/m_ND0oO67cU?t=1256), [0:22:31](https://youtu.be/m_ND0oO67cU?t=1351), [0:23:18](https://youtu.be/m_ND0oO67cU?t=1398), [0:24:05](https://youtu.be/m_ND0oO67cU?t=1445), [0:24:51](https://youtu.be/m_ND0oO67cU?t=1491)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** On event-driven days with geopolitical uncertainty, this 70% probability may not hold; the market can be delayed or reversed by sudden news.

=== [2026 #113 — ICT 2026 1st Hour Dealing Range \ March 28, 2026 (ID 6DuByzKLDsc)] — RTH Opening Range Gap (RTH ORG) and its consequent encroachment
- **Kind:** definition / rule of thumb
- **What ICT says:**
  - The gap is between the prior RTH settlement price (the 4:14 PM ET candle close on Thursday, in this example; he calls it "4:14's regular trading hours settlement price") and the 9:30 ET open.
  - The side above is labelled RTH ORG **high**, the side below is RTH ORG **low**. In the example the 9:30 open was below the prior close, so the prior close is the ORG high and the open is the ORG low (sell-side delivery with the opening price gap).
  - Putting a fib across the two points gives 50%, the consequent encroachment of the gap.
  - Rule: in the first 30 minutes (9:31–10:00 ET) always allow for price to try to reach the midpoint of the opening range gap; he states a 70% likelihood.
  - The gap only exists on the RTH chart. On an electronic trading hours (ETH) chart overnight price action fills the space, so "make sure you're looking at the right chart".
- **How to identify:** compare 4:14/4:15 PM ET prior-day RTH close vs 9:30 AM open on a futures chart (use RTH session settings).
- **How to use:** expect an attempt at the gap CE between 9:31–10:00; but he was reluctant to short the retracement from the gap on Friday (holiday-type conditions, TGIF) and wanted more evidence.
- **Example in this video:** MNQ Friday: gap down at 9:30; price did not reach the gap CE ("gap and go"); the first retracement up to the gap he had marked before it happened but did not trade.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:36:06](https://youtu.be/6DuByzKLDsc?t=2166) — previous RTH close at 4:14 PM Eastern, RTHORG high/low labels
  - [0:38:37](https://youtu.be/6DuByzKLDsc?t=2317) — 70% likelihood of midpoint attempt between 9:31 and 10:00
  - [0:39:27](https://youtu.be/6DuByzKLDsc?t=2367) — gap never traded to, gap and go
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** the 70% is his stated tendency, not a guarantee; it did not trigger here.

=== [2026 #114 — ICT 2026 Market Commentary \ March 27, 2026 (ID z6AxPV2NQ9U)] — Opening Range Gap (ORG) / RTH Opening Range
- **Kind:** definition / context
- **What ICT says:** The opening range gap is the gap between the pre-market or previous day's close and the 9:30 ET RTH open. It can be a gap up (bullish) or gap down (bearish). This gap's size and location relative to prior structure matters. A "deep deep discount opening range gap" means we opened much lower than expected relative to prior price. The low of this opening range and its high are key liquidity points.
- **How to identify:** 
  - Pre-market close or previous day settlement.
  - 9:30 RTH open candle.
  - Measure the gap between them.
  - Identify the high and low of the opening range for the day (typically the 9:30 candle or first few minutes).
- **How to use:** 
  - If it's a deep discount, shorts may be cautious (too good to be true).
  - If it's a gap up into resistance, longs are chasing.
  - Use the opening range high/low as pivotal support/resistance.
  - The liquidity inside the gap is a target for price to visit.
- **Example in this video:** 0:03:08 — "opening price at 9:30 there" (opening range gap low). The opening range high is also identified, and ICT explains that price must clear it to confirm bullish action.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:00:30](https://youtu.be/z6AxPV2NQ9U?t=30) — "I have deep deep deep deep discount opening range gap here"
  - [0:03:08](https://youtu.be/z6AxPV2NQ9U?t=188) — opening price identified; trading hour opening range gap defined
  - [0:19:05](https://youtu.be/z6AxPV2NQ9U?t=1145) — "opening range gap low, which is at opening price at 9:30"
- **Nuance:** Opening range gaps are session-specific. On Friday, opening gaps are less reliable guides due to weekend gap risk.

=== [2026 #117 — ICT 2026 Market Commentary \ March 19, 2026 (ID w2zTfhjScDg)] — Event Horizon (Midpoint Targeting Strategy)
- **Kind:** rule / entry model
- **What ICT says:** When you have two sell-side liquidity pools (two lows), you can draw a fib between them; the midpoint is event horizon. Use it as a partial exit point when you're bearish and expecting continuation lower. Take 25% off at event horizon on a long downtrend; let the balance run to the lower target. You can also use it as an adding-in point if you're adding to a short.
- **How to identify:** Identify two lows (or one old low and one new lower low); draw fib or measure midpoint.
- **How to use:** As a partial exit (especially if you're nearing the end of a move), or as a point to add to the short if you believe lower prices will follow. On a long-established downtrend, take 25% minimum at event horizon to bank some profit.
- **Example in this video:** Bitcoin daily (hypothetically short): two sell-side pools; event horizon is midpoint. If short Bitcoin, take 25% off at event horizon, then let the runner go to the lower target around 49,577 or lower.
- **Timestamps:** [0:27:14](https://youtu.be/w2zTfhjScDg?t=1634), [0:34:22](https://youtu.be/w2zTfhjScDg?t=2062), [0:42:30](https://youtu.be/w2zTfhjScDg?t=2550)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Event horizon changes in meaning once the range resets; if you break the lower low and treat that as a new range, the old event horizon no longer applies—you measure from the new lows.

=== [2026 #118 — ICT 2026 Market Commentary \ March 17, 2026 (ID AxLXI00L8zM)] — Opening Range Gap (RTH)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** The gap (or lack thereof) between the close of the previous session and the open of the regular trading hours session. On March 17, there was an RTH opening range gap with buy-side and sell-side liquidity defined by the gap's low and high.
- **How to identify:** Compare the prior close to the current open; if there's a gap, mark it and identify buy-side (low) and sell-side (high) liquidity pools.
- **How to use:** As targets for price; buyers often seek to fill the gap or access the sell-side.
- **Example in this video:** NQ March 17 RTH opening range gap; sell-side is the high of the gap, minor buy-side is slightly below.
- **Timestamps:** [0:06:29](https://youtu.be/AxLXI00L8zM?t=389), [0:44:13](https://youtu.be/AxLXI00L8zM?t=2653)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** During rollover, these gaps bleed over from the prior contract; watch for artificial price runs filling them.

=== [2026 #121 — ICT 2026 New York Lunch Algorithmic Theory \ March 11, 2026 (ID C90xGr3kW8Y)] — Event Horizon (new coinage)  (aliases: midpoint between two liquidity pools)
- **Kind:** new coinage / definition
- **What ICT says:** Event horizon is the 50% midpoint between two pools of liquidity. Anchor a fib from one pool to the next and show only the 50% level. He says nobody has this in their books and he learned it from nobody. Used as an intermediate objective on the way to the next pool: he wanted price to reach it before rolling his stop just above a pool of liquidity and then trying for the next pool.
- **How to identify:** Two liquidity pools (e.g., the relative equal lows at 24,892.50 and the next pool below at what he calls "54 quarter", presumably 24,854.25). Fib 24,892.50 to 24,854.25, set to show only 50%.
- **How to use:** Target/partial reference; stop management (roll stop to just above the pool once event horizon is hit); stay near the pool region.
- **Example in this video:** MNQ Wed Mar 11: draw 24,892.50 → 24,854.25 (midpoint is about 24,873.4); later price dived to a candle low of 24,873.75 at the 1:30 PM turn (he notes 24,873.25 on one candle, "slightly higher" than the midpoint, "drawing toward that midpoint").
- **Timestamps:** [0:15:13](https://youtu.be/C90xGr3kW8Y?t=913) — defines event horizon; [0:16:01](https://youtu.be/C90xGr3kW8Y?t=961) — how he planned to manage it; [0:17:40](https://youtu.be/C90xGr3kW8Y?t=1060) — price reaction near midpoint
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Price values partly rely on auto-captions ("54 quarter"); verify against the chart.

=== [2026 #122 — ICT 2026 Market Commentary \ March 10, 2026 (ID 54kzPj03Rkg)] — Regular Trading Hours Opening Range Gap (RTH ORG)
- **Kind:** rule / definition
- **What ICT says:** RTH ORG is defined by comparing the previous day's settlement price (4:14 p.m. ET) to today's 9:30 a.m. ET opening. If opening is higher = premium gap; if lower = discount gap. Can be divided into quadrants and octants (eighths) to create reference levels. Previous 5 days' RTH ORGs remain relevant reference points even after price has moved away.
- **How to identify:** On daily chart, mark previous day's 4:14 p.m. closing and current day's 9:30 a.m. opening. Draw line between them. When market opens above settlement = premium; below = discount.
- **How to use:** On intraday charts (1-min, 5-min), algorithmic price action will respect these levels, particularly quadrant and octant subdivisions. Bodies of candlesticks should respect these support/resistance levels. If they do, it's algorithmic. Use as bounce/fail references for order flow confirmation.
- **Example in this video:** Thursday's RTH ORG high was used as octant reference on Tuesday intraday. Candlestick bodies stopped precisely on this octant level multiple times. When price refused to go above it after driving up, it indicated algorithmic rejection of further upside.
- **Timestamps:** [0:09:25](https://youtu.be/54kzPj03Rkg?t=565) — explaining settlement price anchors; [0:14:07](https://youtu.be/54kzPj03Rkg?t=847) — showing octant levels in use; [0:15:43](https://youtu.be/54kzPj03Rkg?t=943) — confirming Thursday's gap becomes Tuesday's discount array
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** RTH ORG uses regular trading hours only (9:30–4:14 ET), not electronic hours. Must distinguish between Monday's gap (using Friday settlement) and Tuesday's gap (using Monday settlement). When price trades above an RTH ORG, it becomes a discount array for future references if you're bullish.

=== [2026 #124 — ICT 2026 Micro Emini S&P 500 Live Trade Execution \ March 09, 2026 (ID clG2IDpEcjE)] — Targets Using RTH Opening Range Gap Levels
- **Kind:** rule / example only
- **What ICT says:** Targets = known liquidity levels. Examples: RTH ORG high (if opening is below it), consequent encroachment (CE) of RTH ORG, intraday high, previous highs. He states: "I wouldn't need it to trade the consequent encouragement [he means consequent encroachment, or CE] of the RTH opening range gap; this is easy, low-hanging fruit."
- **How to identify:** Mark on your chart: previous day's settlement (4:14 p.m. ET close), today's 9:30 a.m. open = RTH ORG. Divide by Fibonacci (eighths) for gradient levels. Intraday highs and lows = liquidity.
- **How to use:** Plan targets BEFORE entry. Partial profits at easier targets (first high, CE of ORG), hold remainder to second or third target (buy-side liquidity pools). Example: $50 target = cover commissions and profit. $100 = bank a second win. Trailing to higher targets = let winners run.
- **Example in this video:** Target 1 = previous high (covered commissions at ~$50). Target 2 = RTH ORG high. Trade delivered to both on the day.
- **Timestamps:** [0:03:23](https://youtu.be/clG2IDpEcjE?t=203) — mentioning consequent encroachment as first target; [0:09:06](https://youtu.be/clG2IDpEcjE?t=546) — scaling out as targets are hit
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Don't over-complicate target selection. Use obvious levels (previous highs/lows, RTH ORG, order block resistance). If price gets there and stalls, take the profit; don't be greedy.

=== [2026 #126 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ February 28, 2026 (ID FBxxYUvNaY4)] — RTH Opening Range Gap (RTH ORG) preparation and 70% midpoint rule  (aliases: opening range gap, RTH settlement, discount/premium gap)
- **Kind:** rule / procedure
- **What ICT says:**
  - Reference price: the previous day's RTH settlement, the 4:14 PM ET close (he also notes you can use the 5:00 PM ET settlement; taught last year). Toggle to the RTH chart session; the gap only appears once the first RTH print at 9:30 occurs.
  - Pre-market routine: note the 4:14 PM settlement (after the close or first thing in the morning); about 30 minutes before the open (around 9:00) compare where price is. If price is well below, label the settlement price RTH ORG high in advance (it is highly unlikely to trade above it before 9:30); you are in for a discount opening range gap (open below the settlement). Above it is a premium gap. He writes "RTH ORG settlement" on his notepad.
  - Once the 9:30 open prints, drag the fib between the open and the settlement price: 50% is the CE.
  - Statistical idea: there is a 70% likelihood price trades to the midpoint of the gap between 9:30 and 10:00. There will be days it never goes there (opens and just drops/goes up). He frames it as a probability inside a narrative, not a guarantee, comparable to his 70% "Tuesday London session makes the weekly high (if bearish) or low (if bullish), else Wednesday" rule.
  - Full gap closure is always possible, but the easier objective is usually the closer liquidity.
  - He calls "too clean" a high that sat one tick above the CE of the gap (nothing else to compare it to on the chart), visible by eye on the price axis without drawing the line.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Fri Feb 27: at about 8:40 price far below Thursday's 4:14 PM settlement, labelled ORG high in advance; the 9:30 open was at a big discount; a high that lined up with the CE (one tick above the CE) printed at about 10:00 (he says he was 2 minutes off in timing), then price entered a messy consolidation.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:57:30](https://youtu.be/FBxxYUvNaY4?t=3450) — Thursday 4:14 PM settlement, charts in NY time
  - [1:00:45](https://youtu.be/FBxxYUvNaY4?t=3645) — how he read it live
  - [1:01:33](https://youtu.be/FBxxYUvNaY4?t=3693) — label as ORG high in advance; 5 PM settlement alternative
  - [1:03:09](https://youtu.be/FBxxYUvNaY4?t=3789) — 70% between 9:30 and 10:00
  - [1:28:37](https://youtu.be/FBxxYUvNaY4?t=5317) — notepad routine, premium vs discount gap
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Same 70% statement appears in L26-113 (March 28) for the 9:31–10:00 window.

=== [2026 #126 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ February 28, 2026 (ID FBxxYUvNaY4)] — Gradient Levels of the RTH Opening Range Gap ("time and price multiplication table")  (aliases: gradient levels, eighths and quarters of the opening gap)
- **Kind:** new coinage / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - Grade the opening range gap (9:30 open to the 4:14 PM settlement) with the fib at the eights (octants) and quarters. Every gradient level is a potential PD array: "that's your order block, breaker, IOFED, propulsion block, Reaper, FVG, first resistance FVG." The hard part (which OB, which FVG, which liquidity pool) goes away because you have built a "multiplication table, the schematic based on time and price".
  - Can also be done with a new week opening gap or new day opening gap.
  - He says he hid this for a long time to avoid others repackaging it; he stresses it is still "elementary" in his toolkit.
  - The level the first presented gap sits on gives validation: on Friday a single-candle BISI lay on the 0.375 level, which validated it as a legitimate entry toward the buy-side liquidity / daily volume imbalance.
  - He calls this "anticipating" (knowing where a gap will form) rather than predicting or reacting.
- **How to identify / use:** Draw fib on the RTH ORG; mark 0.125 increments and quarters; look for FVGs/BISIs/SIBIs that sit on those levels; trade those toward the time-and-price objective (see lunch macro).
- **Example in this video:** NQ Fri Feb 27, 11:30 entering lunch: single-candle BISI on 0.375 gradient level; pyramided on cheaper prices; targeted the buy side above and the low of the daily volume imbalance; position closed after the run to that VI low; figures quoted are unclear in the caption (he mentions "just shy of 9 grand").
- **Timestamps:** [1:07:06](https://youtu.be/FBxxYUvNaY4?t=4026) — gradient levels first mentioned; [1:31:58](https://youtu.be/FBxxYUvNaY4?t=5518) — graded gap into eighths and quarters; [1:32:45](https://youtu.be/FBxxYUvNaY4?t=5565) — "that's your order block, that's your breaker..."; [1:40:37](https://youtu.be/FBxxYUvNaY4?t=6037) — BISI on 0.375
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Same family as the octant/quadrant levels he applies to the first hour's range in L26-113.

=== [2026 #127 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ February 27, 2026 (ID TFyk2jbQzdc)] — Event Horizon  (aliases: midpoint between two static price points, halfway-point draw)
- **Kind:** new coinage / re-teach
- **What ICT says:** When two obvious static price points (old daily lows, old highs, or a NWOG/NDOG above and another below the current price) bracket the market, measure the range between them and mark the halfway point. Price will likely gravitate to that halfway point, and once it breaks beyond it "generally" a sustained price run follows (retracements possible). He says this is the midpoint (a consequent-encroachment-style level) between two key PD arrays. He originally taught it with one new week opening gap above and one below price.
- **How to identify:** Lay a fib across the span between the two points (e.g. yesterday's low to the next old-low pool on the daily). He watches how much "energy" price spends reaching the upper quadrant and then the 50% level.
- **How to use:** Use the 50% as the draw; a break below it (in a bearish case) signals likely sustained delivery toward the far pool. He eyeballs the fib and says it can be "off a couple points".
- **Example in this video:** MNQ daily old lows; yesterday's low (taken at 8:30) to the next outlined pool of sell-side liquidity below; price traded down into the upper quadrant of that range, then made three lower lows on 1m, aiming to gravitate to the halfway point.
- **Timestamps:** [0:18:10](https://youtu.be/TFyk2jbQzdc?t=1090) — static old lows as hard objectives, fib laid over; [0:19:51](https://youtu.be/TFyk2jbQzdc?t=1191) — gravitate to the halfway point; "that's event horizon"; [0:21:26](https://youtu.be/TFyk2jbQzdc?t=1286) — NWOG-to-NWOG version
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He stresses that old highs/lows are precise, finite locations ("Wyckoff doesn't talk about that stuff") and that being old PD arrays they invite price to gravitate to the midpoint.

=== [2026 #129 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ February 23, 2026 (ID NxwfXE6Zyns)] — RTH Opening Range Gap (RTH ORG) — premium vs discount, high/low assignment
- **Kind:** definition (precise)
- **What ICT says:** The gap is between where price opens at 9:30 ET (regular trading hours) and the previous day's RTH close, which prints at 4:14 pm ET on the chart (closing candle; the last 4:14 candle on the time axis). If the 9:30 opening price is above the previous RTH close it is a premium gap: the 9:30 open is the RTH ORG high and the previous close is the low. If the 9:30 open is below the previous RTH close it is a discount gap: the 9:30 open is the RTH ORG low and the previous close is the high. Do this every day and keep several days of levels (he also marks the CE/gradient levels when wide).
- **How to identify:** Toggle the chart to RTH to see the close; then mark the gap. In Monday's case "last trading day" is Friday, so Monday's RTH ORG is also the new week opening gap.
- **How to use:** These levels are the key levels for entries and targets; price reacts at them "to the tick", often during macro times; he keeps roughly a week of ORG levels (Thursday ORG low/high, Friday ORG high/low, Tuesday ORG high/low, etc.) on his chart.
- **Example in this video:** Friday RTH close 25,079.00; Monday 9:30 open 24,981.25 (lower), so a discount gap: RTH ORG high = 25,079.00 (same price as the NWOG top) and RTH ORG low = 24,981.25. Later: Tuesday's RTH ORG high hit at 24,799.5 exactly at a candle high during a macro time, leaving Tuesday's ORG low untouched, then Friday's RTH ORG low was touched.
- **Timestamps:** [0:21:27](https://youtu.be/NxwfXE6Zyns?t=1287) — NWOG and prior close 25,079; [0:22:17](https://youtu.be/NxwfXE6Zyns?t=1337) — 9:30 open 24,981.25 = ORG low; [0:25:34](https://youtu.be/NxwfXE6Zyns?t=1534) — premium vs discount rule; [0:43:53](https://youtu.be/NxwfXE6Zyns?t=2633) — stacking prior days' ORG highs/lows
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Relative to Monday: "Thursday/Friday/Tuesday" ORG levels are from the previous week's sessions. He says the opening range is the 30-minute window starting 9:30, "I don't care what anybody else says".

=== [2026 #131 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ February 11, 2026 (ID V4KPpX-CWXk)] — RTH Opening Range Gap and half-gap (CE) delivery  (aliases: half gap, gap and go, previous RTH settlement to opening)
- **Kind:** rule / entry framework
- **What ICT says:** At 9:30 ET the regular-trading-hours gap is the difference between the previous RTH settlement and the 9:30 open. Pull the fib from the previous RTH settlement to the opening price; the half-gap (CE of the gap) has about a 70 percent chance of being traded back to within the first 30 minutes (he also says "by 10:00 in the morning"). A large gap is better: 100 handles or more is ideal because that offers about 50 handles to the half-gap. After it reaches the half, the next question is whether it continues to the 3/4 gap and the full close, or rejects the half-gap and runs in the direction of the gap ("gap and go").
- **How to identify:** Premium gap (open above prior RTH close) -> expect a trade back down to the half; discount gap -> back up. Mark the half gap, plus any pool of liquidity sitting there (here a sell-side pool just under last Wednesday's RTH ORG low).
- **How to use:** In the beginning, coach yourself to submit to the idea that price goes to the usual draw; combined with a short first-presented FVG at 9:31 onward, the half gap is the target. He frames this as "one of the things I had for my son" (look for that move when there is a sizeable gap). Don't take his 70 percent on faith: test it on your own data every day there is a gap, for as long as you can, and keep recording going forward (he cites 34 years of use and data from 50 years before him).
- **Example in this video:** Premium gap on Feb 11: 9:30 open at ...326.75 (last digits). Price ran the pre-session high, took the bearish-breaker area, sold off, and reached the half-gap about 2 minutes before 10:00 ("there's the delivery"). He also writes the ORG levels manually using the last three numbers of price.
- **Timestamps:** [0:08:26](https://youtu.be/V4KPpX-CWXk?t=506) — premium RTH ORG forms; [0:09:48](https://youtu.be/V4KPpX-CWXk?t=588) — fib from settlement to open, 70 percent to half gap in 30 min; [0:21:34](https://youtu.be/V4KPpX-CWXk?t=1294) — half gap then 3/4 / full / gap-and-go; [0:36:11](https://youtu.be/V4KPpX-CWXk?t=2171) — half gap delivered before 10:00; [0:37:51](https://youtu.be/V4KPpX-CWXk?t=2271) — 100 handles ideal
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Said 30 minutes and "by 10:00"; also says the opening range is 9:30-10:00, "I don't care who says otherwise, it's 30 minutes". Last Wednesday's RTH ORG levels were still active a week later (low and the half-gap).

=== [2026 #132 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ February 10, 2026 (ID E8EtGkn-x5w)] — Higher-timeframe premise from the RTH Opening Range Gap  (aliases: key level, session open/close)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** Every trade needs a higher-timeframe premise: something on a higher time frame that leads to why the market should go up or down. The RTH opening range gap (previous RTH close to the 9:30 open) counts as a higher time frame key level, "you can't get any more higher time frame than session closings and session opens". The high and low of the gap are defined just by delineation; no labels are needed. Such levels act like new week opening gaps and new day opening gaps. He says 24-hour trading, if it happens, would make things easier (the gap would simply be defined differently, other tools exist).
- **How to identify:** Compare today's 9:30 open with the previous RTH close (4:14 pm ET print). With ETH shown continuously you do not see that gap (the only thing missing on ETH is the 5-6 pm hour gap), so toggle to RTH or use annotations.
- **How to use:** Anchor on the old gap and expect price to return to it (he had said on Feb 4 he felt price would come back up there). Fib/gradient levels inside it matter: price traded into its upper half, then quickly failed.
- **Example in this video:** Feb 4 (Wednesday) RTH ORG: 25,440 and 25,385. Five days later, overnight, price traded up into it, into the upper half between the two levels (a bull flag everyone likes to trade), then "gave up the ghost" and went lower; at 7:00 it was in the upper half; then the pre-market ran a buy-side and reversed.
- **Timestamps:** [0:07:26](https://youtu.be/E8EtGkn-x5w?t=446) — higher-time-frame premise needed; [0:10:45](https://youtu.be/E8EtGkn-x5w?t=645) — Feb 4 ORG levels 25,440 / 25,385; [0:13:07](https://youtu.be/E8EtGkn-x5w?t=787) — why the open and close are higher time frame; [0:15:31](https://youtu.be/E8EtGkn-x5w?t=931) — RTH vs ETH and the 4:14 pm close
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Fib settings: eighths zone and quarters (he tells viewers to throw a fib on it and put "the eighth zone and quarters on").

=== [2026 #133 — ICT Technical Review \ NQ Futures March \ February 03, 2026 (ID 486N8ow3U2A)] — New Week Opening Gap (NWOG)
- **Kind:** definition · rule
- **What ICT says:** The gap formed at the opening of the new week (Sunday evening into Monday morning in electronic trading). Treated as a liquidity pool. When price breaks below it and then returns to its halfway point or full range, this becomes a draw on liquidity.
- **How to identify:** Find the Sunday open (first tick of the week) and Monday's first candle; the gap between them is the NWOG. Identify the high and low of this gap, and the midpoint.
- **How to use:** NWOG acts as a liquidity pool. Once broken, traders hunt for equilibrium (halfway point) or full closure. Use it as a target after initial moves away from it. It's a reference structure for intraday traders.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 1-minute chart: market opened (RTH 9:30) above the NWOG, then gravitated down to the gap's low, broke below it, traded to the low, found premium sensitivity, and broke further into the halfway point of the lower discount wick on the daily chart.
- **Timestamps:** [0:04:04](https://youtu.be/486N8ow3U2A?t=244) (trading hours opening range gap, specific to ICT's framework, not a breakout strategy) · [0:08:07](https://youtu.be/486N8ow3U2A?t=487) (NWOG as liquidity reference, "just like a dog whistle") · [0:10:30](https://youtu.be/486N8ow3U2A?t=630) (new week opening gap in the larger price sequence, next area of interest when trading down from higher levels)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** ICT's NWOG framework is distinct from "opening range breakout" strategies—he's not chasing breakouts. He's identifying NWOG as a liquidity structure to run through or draw back to, depending on larger bias.

=== [2026 #133 — ICT Technical Review \ NQ Futures March \ February 03, 2026 (ID 486N8ow3U2A)] — Opening Range Gap Theory vs. Opening Range Breakout
- **Kind:** rule · refinement
- **What ICT says:** ICT does NOT use opening range breakout strategy. His approach is specifically about the opening range *gap* structure and drawing on liquidity within it. "I don't use a opening range breakout strategy. You know, that's all chasing price. I'm not interested in that. I want to know what I'm looking for and then key off of a very specific price level and I'm trading against the direction of where the market's going to go."
- **How to identify:** Mark the RTH (regular trading hours) open time (9:30 ET) and the previous day's close or electronic session settlement. The gap and the range created by these constitute the opening range.
- **How to use:** Instead of chasing a breakout above or below, use the opening range's midpoint, edges, and draws on liquidity as targets. If bearish, scale into shorts on rallies into inefficiencies within or near the opening range, not on breakouts.
- **Example in this video:** NQ opened gapped up from the previous close, but instead of chasing the gap higher, ICT showed how to use the gap's internal structure (halfway point, full range) as targets on down moves. The market opened at the gap high, traded to the midpoint, then broke out of the range low—all of this price action was driven by the gap structure, not by breakout chasing.
- **Timestamps:** [0:04:04](https://youtu.be/486N8ow3U2A?t=244) (explicit statement: "my trading hours opening range gap theory, which is not what everybody else talks about... I don't use a opening range breakout strategy")
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** This is a critical distinction. Breakout chasers buy highs and sell lows; ICT traders use the gap structure to predict reversals and trade against the initial direction.

=== [2026 #134 — How Do I Engage Markets When I Don't Have An Initial Bias? (ID zyKAPNk_prA)] — Hourly and 15-minute context: BISI to CE and new week opening gap
- **Kind:** example only

After the new week opened, price traded up into the gap, lower, then rallied. On the 15m, the bodies respected the CE of the 1-hour gap, then reached up into buy-side and ripped through the new week opening gap high and low; he had no intention of coming back to it. Unless you expected higher prices from the open, it would have run over you. On 1m he shows the NWOG with its eighths and quarters.

=== [2026 #136 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ January 29, 2026 (ID 8wHewi6N6dg)] — Opening range gap eighths carried forward from the previous day (RTH ORG levels)  (aliases ICT uses: eighths, .125, .375, gradient levels, quadrants, "opening range gap on the eighths")
- **Kind:** refinement / reference levels
- **What ICT says:**
  - The horizontal lines on his chart are the eighths (.125 increments) of yesterday's RTH opening range gap (settlement at the prior close to the 9:30 open), still drawn forward after the gap closed. Price continues to react to them, which contradicts the claim "everybody is trading the eighths after the gap closes" (he says they do not, so this is "technical science", not other people's stuff).
  - Overnight they are useful as levels. A rally was rejected exactly at the area just above the .375 level; a sell-off failed to reach a lower eighth; the final sell-off ran through eighth after eighth with one eighth at 26,144.25 and another at 26,122.50 acting as the next targets.
  - Relative equal highs formed with their bodies converging on the .125 level of that gap, which he used to argue the high might be kept intact.
  - "The inability to get to this eighth" above is why the sell-off after the inefficiency was so sharp.
- **How to identify:** From the RTH opening range gap (previous settlement to 9:30 open), divide into eighths; carry those lines to the next sessions.
- **How to use:** Use as magnets/reference levels; failing to reach an eighth before reversing signals strength/weakness; sell-side liquidity rests just below an eighth.
- **Example in this video:** NQ: price dipped at the 9:30 open candle to a low of 26,122.50, "exactly the price level" of an eighth from yesterday's RTH opening range gap.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:04:56](https://youtu.be/8wHewi6N6dg?t=296) — explains the lines as the eighths of the RTH opening range gap
  - [0:27:38](https://youtu.be/8wHewi6N6dg?t=1658) — iFVG sits on the eighth; "technical science"

=== [2026 #137 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ January 13, 2026 (ID Rwao8f6oD0Q)] — Precision from price and time (not volume): NWOG / previous-settlement level, eighths, minor sell-side liquidity  (aliases ICT uses: new week opening gap, regular trading hours opening range gap high, eighth, old daily high, minor sell side pool)
- **Kind:** example / refinement
- **What ICT says:**

  - A minor sell-side pool sat below a low where long stops (protective sell stops) were placed. His stated condition (before the move): do not want to see this low taken before price trades to the NWOG; if it was taken first there would likely be a deeper retracement and he would be done for the morning. In fact price went to the NWOG first and then dropped to take the low (he calls this foresight).
  - After that, price traded down to another reference to the tick: the low of a candle equal to an eighth (quoted as "938 and a quarter", caption leading digits unclear) and then reversed.
  - Earlier reference: an iFVG from 10:40 Friday 9 Jan was extended forward and price reacted at it; it also sits on an eighth.
  - The 7 Jan old daily high was swept (liquidity) and then price broke lower back into the NWOG overnight.
  - He argues that exact price/time knowledge beats volume profile, DOM, iceberg detection or contract counts; volume profile will not show where price will turn. His tooling costs only data fees (about $45 a month at AMP, plus about $7 for TradingView), no "gimmicks".
- **How to identify:** Mark NWOG, RTH previous settlement, eighths of the gap, old daily highs, minor sell-side pools beneath obvious lows.
- **How to use:** Treat NWOG/settlement/eighth levels as targets and reversal levels; state the condition for a failure (low taken before the target reached) in advance.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Monday 12 Jan: price runs to NWOG/previous settlement at 25,933.5 (candle high exactly), then drops to take the minor SSL, then reverses off an eighth (low about 938.25).
