=== [2026 #138 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ January 12, 2026 (ID dt9mk4n5g_o)] — RTH Opening Range Gap (previous settlement → 9:30 open) — procedure and statistics
- **Kind:** entry model / definition
- **What ICT says:**
  - Distinct from the New Day Opening Gap and New Week Opening Gap. RTH opening range gap = difference between previous RTH close/settlement (4:00 pm ET) and the 9:30 am open. Monday's gap = Friday's RTH settlement to Monday 9:30 open.
  - Procedure each morning: switch the chart to Regular Trading Hours (tab at lower right), read the previous close (OHLC at top left), draw a line at it and label it "RTH opening range gap" (high or low unknown until the open), then at 9:30 draw the open price as the other side. Drop a fib across it and grade it on quadrants/eighths: upper quadrant, consequent encroachment, etc.
  - At the open, the market will likely explore into the gap. Stat stated: about 70% of the time the gap's consequent encroachment (CE) is delivered/tagged by 10:00 am; knowing this is a "huge advantage."
  - The gap needs not be fully closed. Three quarters (upper quadrant) is often enough.
  - Vacuum: RTH had no trading between the settlement and the open, so price rushes back into that unpopulated area even though overnight ETH already traded some of it (soda-cap-release analogy).
- **Example in this video:** NQ Jan 12: Friday RTH settlement 25,933.50; Monday 9:30 open 25,746.25, so a discount gap with the settlement line as the gap HIGH. At 9:07 he already draws the 25,933.50 line (well above current price) and labels it ORG high, then adds the low at the 9:30 open. After the open, CE (about 25,839.9) is tagged within roughly a minute and the upper quadrant (25,887) around 9:39 ET (the 3/4 mark), a run of about 150 handles in total; the last quarter up to full closure at 25,933.50 was not needed. Stacked above the open: Asian session high (the range's highest high, run after the open and read as "cleared"), then this week's NWOG low, with Friday's settlement just below it; daily relative equal highs at 26,133.50 are the higher draw. Exact order of the Asian high vs. upper quadrant is read from his walkthrough (caption unclear).
- **Timestamps:** [1:21:42](https://youtu.be/dt9mk4n5g_o?t=4902) — mark previous settlement · [1:25:03](https://youtu.be/dt9mk4n5g_o?t=5103) — what the gap is, fib grading · [1:47:46](https://youtu.be/dt9mk4n5g_o?t=6466) — CE tagged, 70% stat · [1:48:33](https://youtu.be/dt9mk4n5g_o?t=6513) — vacuum / soda analogy
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** CE tag-by-10:00 70% figure restated at [2:09:19](https://youtu.be/dt9mk4n5g_o?t=7759). The 25,746.25 open is read from "746 quarter" in captions and prefixed 25,; the arithmetic of CE/upper quadrant (25,839.88 / 25,886.69) matches the 25,887 he reads out.

=== [2026 #138 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ January 12, 2026 (ID dt9mk4n5g_o)] — Opening gaps as PD arrays: NWOG / NDOG / RTH ORG — keep them after they fill
- **Kind:** rule / refinement
- **What ICT says:** weekly opening gap, new day opening gap, RTH opening range gap are all different. Industry says a filled gap is "one and done"; he says that is a mistake. Keep them on the chart for at least five days, whether they fill or not; filled or traded-through gaps remain reference points. They matter most within the last 20 trading days.
- **How to use:** stack the levels numerically (highest to lowest with shorthand labels, in his notebook) for the last ~5 trading days: first-presented FVG Jan 7, NWOG this week, RTH ORG, etc. He does not use Friday's or Thursday's opening ranges.
- **Example in this video:** the week's levels above the open: Jan 7 first-presented FVG, this week's NWOG (low sits just above Friday's 25,933.50 settlement), then RTH ORG; Asian session high at another "eighth" of the range.
- **Timestamps:** [0:20:59](https://youtu.be/dt9mk4n5g_o?t=1259) · [1:25:51](https://youtu.be/dt9mk4n5g_o?t=5151) · [1:26:39](https://youtu.be/dt9mk4n5g_o?t=5199)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** "at least 5 days" ties to how he introduced them initially.

=== [2026 #139 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts In Action \ January 06, 2026 (ID Jxl5UJk_ylc)] — Electronic Trading Hours vs. Regular Trading Hours (RTH) Settlement
- **Kind:** definition · reference level
- **What ICT says:** Electronic trading hours (Sunday open through Friday close, continuous) have a settlement price; RTH settlement (9:30 to 4:00 ET) is different. "Regular trading hours settlement is down here... we're showing electronic trading hours." The reference changes depending on which "official" settlement you're watching.
- **How to identify:** Note the settlement price for electronic trading (usually from Friday night's settlement) and RTH settlement (from previous day's close at 4:00 ET). These are marked as distinct levels on the chart.
- **How to use:** Use RTH settlement as a reference for the "official" previous day's close in terms of technical analysis. Electronic settlement is where overnight futures trading cleared. Both matter: RTH for daily technicals, electronic for context on overnight positioning.
- **Example in this video:** NQ's RTH settlement was marked below the current price; ICT noted that if price were to trade down to that level, it would represent a full gap closure and bearish rejection of the initial gap-up.
- **Timestamps:** [0:03:50](https://youtu.be/Jxl5UJk_ylc?t=230) (both settlements marked and explained)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Many traders miss the distinction; always note which settlement you're referencing for daily bias confirmation.

=== [2026 #140 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ January 02, 2026 (ID 1X0A-Lgf9RY)] — Opening range gap half (CE) as the low-hanging-fruit objective
- **Kind:** rule / entry model
- **What ICT says:** the RTH opening range gap's consequent encroachment (half the gap) is the primary objective; focus on half the gap only, do not look for a full close (something to evolve into). Stated again: about 70% of the time price trades back to it by 10:00 am. If the higher-timeframe chart is bearish, it is still viable to go short and aim at half gap after 10:00. He used this to coach his sons, a brother-in-law and best friend (a minimalist morning framework); take partials before the target.
- **Example in this video:** gap from the Friday RTH settle to the 9:30 open measured on the RTH chart with a fib (halves and lower quadrants); half-gap target 25,567.25, price tagged and slightly exceeded it.
- **Timestamps:** [0:18:45](https://youtu.be/1X0A-Lgf9RY?t=1125) · [0:20:25](https://youtu.be/1X0A-Lgf9RY?t=1225) — 70% by 10:00 · [0:22:00](https://youtu.be/1X0A-Lgf9RY?t=1320) — focus on half gap only · [0:38:07](https://youtu.be/1X0A-Lgf9RY?t=2287)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** pairs with the RTH ORG procedure in L26-138 (Jan 12) with the same 70%-by-10:00 statistic.

=== [2026 #141 — From Vision To Execution (ID rXgEMIJ1fQg)] — Top-down: always check the daily chart  (aliases: higher-timeframe narrative, daily half-range target, NWOG)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** a one-, five-, 15-minute or one-hour chart alone will not tell you why price rallied and sold. You must do proper top-down analysis and at least consult the daily chart. Without it "you are trading blind" no matter how hot your recent results. On the day, the daily chart showed the suspension block (premium array) and a range whose half (blue line) was the lower reference; price fell just short of it. A new week opening gap (NWOG) was present that week and price dropped off nearby.
- **How to use:** look at what the daily chart is offering (premium or discount array nearby, midpoint of the daily range, opening gap) before engaging intraday.
- **Timestamps:** [0:43:59](https://youtu.be/rXgEMIJ1fQg?t=2639) why it ran up then sold: need top-down · [0:44:48](https://youtu.be/rXgEMIJ1fQg?t=2688) "trading blind" without the daily · [0:48:52](https://youtu.be/rXgEMIJ1fQg?t=2932) half of the daily range as the next reach; NWOG present
- **Nuance / caveats:** exact NWOG price, date and relation to the move are not clearly stated.
